The outward racing is visible, tarā, you see, so believers can recognize hypocrisy by actions, not only by claims. The same visibility appears for those who swear oaths they are with you but deeds void, as verse 53 said. The verse's placement after verse 61's entering and leaving with disbelief shows that hidden disbelief manifests in racing to evil. The remedy is for rabbis and scholars to forbid sinful speech and unlawful consumption, as next verse will command, indicating that leadership should prevent racing to sin.

**Why Racing Is Emphasized**

Yusāri'ūna, racing, emphasizes eagerness and competition in evil, as if they compete to do more sin, transgression, forbidden gain. The same verb appears for racing to disbelief (5:41) and racing for guardianship (5:52), showing pattern of hastening to evil. The opposite is competing in good deeds, fa-istabiqū al-khayrāt, compete in good (5:48), which is commanded for believers. The contrast shows two races: race to evil and race to good. Believers should race to good, not to sin. The verse therefore warns against racing to evil and encourages racing to good, as earlier verses commanded seek wasīlah and strive in Allah's way. The next verse will question why rabbis and scholars do not forbid sinful speech and unlawful consumption, indicating that leadership failure allows racing to evil.

**Extra Verified Note on Suḥt**

Suḥt, forbidden gain, is defined as bribery, as Ibn Mas'ūd said, and as payment for changing rulings, as al-Ḥasan said, and as usury, as some said, all forms of unlawful wealth that strips blessing. Lisān al-'Arab defines suḥt as unlawful gain that is peeled away, stripped of blessing. The same term appears in verses 42 and 62 for same group, showing that consuming forbidden gain is characteristic. The phrase la-bi'sa mā kānū ya'malūn, evil are actions, appears also in verse 62 itself and is among condemnations for those who race to evil. The following verse will condemn inaction of rabbis and scholars who do not forbid evil, showing that both action and inaction can be evil.

**Visible Racing as Indicator**

Tarā, you see, indicates that racing to sin is visible, not hidden, so believers can recognize hypocrisy by observing actions. The same visibility appears for those with sickness racing for guardianship, as verse 52 said you see those with sickness racing. The visibility is mercy: hidden disease manifests in observable racing, allowing community to be wary. The three categories — ithm, 'udwān, suḥt — cover sin in speech and belief, transgression against people, and unlawful wealth, showing comprehensive corruption that is observable in different spheres. The next verse will ask why rabbis and scholars do not forbid sinful speech and unlawful consumption, indicating that leadership should intervene to stop racing.

**Evil Actions Versus Evil Inaction**

La-bi'sa mā kānū ya'malūn, evil are actions, condemns action, while next verse will say la-bi'sa mā kānū yaṣna'ūn, evil is inaction, condemning inaction of scholars who do not forbid. The pair shows that both doing evil and not forbidding evil are evil. The same pair appears in verses 62 and 63 as deliberate contrast: action evil, inaction evil. The verse therefore teaches that community needs both individuals refraining from sin and scholars forbidding sin; failure in either leads to evil. The following verse will question why rabbis and scholars do not forbid, completing argument that both racing to sin and not forbidding it are condemned.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:63

> Why do their rabbis and scholars not forbid them from saying what is sinful and consuming what is unlawful? Evil indeed is their inaction

**Expanded Commentary**

**Why Rabbis and Scholars Do Not Forbid Sinful Speech and Unlawful Consumption**

Law lā yanhāhum al-rabbāniyyūn wa al-aḥbār 'an qawlihim al-ithm wa aklihim al-suḥt questions inaction of religious leaders. Law lā, why not, yanhāhum, forbid them, al-rabbāniyyūn wa al-aḥbār, rabbis and scholars, as in verse 44, those entrusted with Book and witnesses over it, 'an qawlihim al-ithm, from saying sinful, qawlihim al-ithm, sinful speech, such as lying, distorting, as previous verses described listening to lies and distorting words, wa aklihim al-suḥt, and consuming forbidden gain, aklihim from a-k-l, to eat, consume, suḥt, forbidden gain, as in verses 42 and 62, indicates that leaders should forbid both sinful speech and unlawful consumption. The question law lā, why not, is rebuke: why do they not forbid, when they are entrusted as keepers of Book and witnesses. The same question appears in *“Why do rabbis and scholars not forbid”* (5:63) itself, indicating that their inaction is blameworthy. The verse therefore condemns not only those who race to sin but also leaders who do not forbid.

**Evil Indeed Is Their Inaction**

La-bi'sa mā kānū yaṣna'ūn — evil indeed is their inaction — condemns inaction. La-bi'sa, evil indeed, mā kānū yaṣna'ūn, what they used to do, yaṣna'ūn from ṣ-n-', to make, do, craft, indicates that their action, or rather inaction, of not forbidding is evil. The previous verse said la-bi'sa mā kānū ya'malūn, evil are actions, for those racing to sin, and this verse says la-bi'sa mā kānū yaṣna'ūn, evil is inaction, for leaders not forbidding, showing that both doing evil and not forbidding evil are evil. The pair ya'malūn and yaṣna'ūn, both meaning to do, but yaṣna'ūn can imply crafting, making, indicating that leaders' inaction is crafted, deliberate, not accidental. The same condemnation appears in *“Evil is their inaction”* (5:63) and in *“Evil indeed was what they did”* (5:79) for not forbidding evil. The verse therefore teaches that religious leadership has duty to forbid sinful speech and unlawful consumption, and failure is evil.

**Role of Rabbis and Scholars as Entrusted Keepers**

Al-rabbāniyyūn wa al-aḥbār, rabbis and scholars, were described in verse 44 as entrusted with Book and witnesses over it, with command not to fear people but fear Me and not trade revelations for small price. Their role is to preserve, judge by, and forbid violation of Book. When they do not forbid qawl al-ithm, sinful speech, and akl al-suḥt, consumption of forbidden gain, they betray trust. The same role appears for Muslims: *“Let there be among you community inviting to good, enjoining good and forbidding evil”* (3:104) commands enjoining and forbidding. The verse therefore applies to all religious leaders, Jewish, Christian, Muslim: they must forbid evil, not ignore for fear or gain. The question law lā, why not, indicates that forbidding is expected, and its absence is blameworthy. The next verse will describe Jews saying Allah is tight-fisted, another sinful speech that rabbis should forbid but did not.

**Sinful Speech and Unlawful Consumption as Two Evils**

Qawlihim al-ithm, saying sinful, refers to sinful speech such as lying, distorting scripture, saying Allah is tight-fisted (5:64), and claiming Allah is Messiah (5:17, 72) or third of three (5:73). Aklihim al-suḥt, consuming forbidden gain, refers to bribery, usury, taking payment for distorting, as earlier defined. The two cover speech and wealth, showing that corruption is in both words and earnings. Leaders should forbid both, because sinful speech misleads people and unlawful consumption corrupts economy. The verse's pairing of qawl al-ithm and akl al-suḥt shows that they are linked: sinful speech often leads to unlawful gain, as distorting for bribery. The leaders' failure to forbid both allows racing to sin, transgression, forbidden gain described in previous verse to continue. The verse therefore links individual racing to evil and leadership failure to forbid, showing that both are needed for corruption to spread.

**A Verse That Condemns Leadership Failure**

This verse condemns leadership failure to forbid evil, which is among major duties. The same condemnation appears for Children of Israel who did not forbid one another from doing evil, as verse 79 will say *“They did not forbid one another from doing evil, evil was what they did”* (5:79). The failure to forbid is itself evil, la-bi'sa mā kānū yaṣna'ūn, evil is inaction, showing that inaction in face of evil is not neutral but evil. A verified ḥadīth in Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim (49) says whoever sees evil should change with hand, if not, with tongue, if not, with heart, and that is weakest faith, indicating that forbidding evil is duty. The verse's question law lā yanhāhum, why do they not forbid, is rebuke that should motivate leaders to forbid. The next verse will give example of sinful speech that should have been forbidden: saying Allah is tight-fisted.

**Extra Verified Note on Rabbāniyyūn and Aḥbār**

Rabbāniyyūn, rabbis, from rabb, to nurture, those who nurture knowledge and people, and aḥbār, scholars, from ḥibr, ink, scholar who writes, as in verse 44, are entrusted as keepers. The phrase law lā yanhāhum, why do they not forbid, appears also in other contexts for not forbidding evil, showing that forbidding evil is expected from scholars. The pair la-bi'sa mā kānū ya'malūn (62) and la-bi'sa mā kānū yaṣna'ūn (63) is deliberate: first condemns action of racing to sin, second condemns inaction of not forbidding, showing both are evil. The following verse will describe sinful speech about Allah being tight-fisted, which rabbis should have forbidden but did not, illustrating failure.

**Leadership Duty to Forbid as Protection**

The duty to forbid sinful speech and unlawful consumption is protection for community, because if leaders do not forbid, racing to sin spreads, as verse 62 showed many racing. The Qur'an says *“Those who were condemned among Children of Israel were condemned for disobedience and transgression, they did not forbid one another”* (5:78-79) showing that not forbidding leads to condemnation. The verse therefore warns that scholars who do not forbid share blame for spread of sin. The question law lā, why not, is not only rebuke but call to action: why do you not forbid, when you are entrusted. The next verse will give example of sinful speech that should have been forbidden: saying Allah is tight-fisted, which is insult to divine generosity.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:64

> ˹Some among˺ the Jews said, “Allah is tight-fisted.” May their fists be tied and they be condemned for what they said. Rather, He is open-handed, giving freely as He pleases. That which has been revealed to you ˹O Prophet˺ from your Lord will only cause many of them to increase in wickedness and disbelief. We have stirred among them hostility and hatred until the Day of Judgment. Whenever they kindle the fire of war, Allah puts it out. And they strive to spread corruption in the land. And Allah does not like corruptors

**Expanded Commentary**

**Claim That Allah Is Tight-Fisted as Insult**

Wa qālat al-yahūd yad Allāh maghlūlah reports insult: Jews said Allah's hand is tied. Yad Allāh, hand of Allah, maghlūlah, tied, chained, from gh-l-l, to tie, indicates tight-fisted, stingy, withholding provision. The claim was made when Allah withheld provision or when they were in hardship, saying Allah is stingy, as early reports say some Jews in Madinah said Allah is tight-fisted because He asks for loan, as in *“Who will lend to Allah good loan”* (2:245) and they mocked. The same claim appears in *“Jews said Allah's hand is tied”* (5:64) itself. The claim is insult to divine generosity, because Allah is openhanded, as next clause says. The Qur'an elsewhere says *“They said Allah is poor and we are rich”* (3:181) similar insult. The verse therefore records sinful speech that rabbis and scholars should have forbidden but did not, as previous verse condemned.

**May Their Fists Be Tied and Condemned**

Ghullat aydīhim wa lu'inū bi-mā qālū — may their hands be tied and they be condemned for what they said — is curse and condemnation. Ghullat, tied, from gh-l-l, same root, indicates may their hands be tied as they said Allah's hand tied, reciprocal curse. Aydīhim, their hands, plural, and lu'inū, condemned, cursed, from l-'-n, to expel from mercy, bi-mā qālū, for what they said, shows that curse is for saying Allah is tight-fisted. The same reciprocal curse appears for similar insults. The phrase is both invocation and information: their hands will be tied in sense of stinginess and in Hereafter chained, and they are condemned for insult. The verse therefore responds to insult with counter-curse that fits: they said Allah's hand tied, so may their hands be tied. The next clause corrects: rather, He is openhanded.

**Rather, He Is Openhanded Giving Freely**

Bal yadāhu mabsūṭatān yunfiqu kayfa yashā' — rather, His hands are open, giving freely as He pleases — corrects. Bal, rather, negates previous claim, yadāhu, His two hands, mabsūṭatān, open, extended, from b-s-ṭ, to extend, open, indicates generosity, openhandedness, yunfiqu, He spends, gives, kayfa yashā', as He pleases, shows that provision is by divine will and generosity, not stinginess. The mention of two hands, yadāhu, indicates vast generosity, as Arabs say openhanded with both hands. The same correction appears in *“His hands are open, He spends as He pleases”* (5:64) itself. The phrase yunfiqu kayfa yashā' shows that withholding or giving is by wisdom, not stinginess. The Qur'an says *“If Allah extended provision for servants, they would transgress in land, but He sends down in measure”* (42:27), showing that withholding is wisdom, not tight-fistedness. The verse therefore affirms divine generosity.

**Revelation Increases Wickedness and Disbelief for Many**

Wa la-yazīdanna kathīran minhum mā unzila ilayka min rabbika ṭughyānan wa kufran — what was revealed to you will increase many in wickedness and disbelief — describes reaction. La-yazīdanna, will surely increase, kathīran minhum, many of them, mā unzila ilayka min rabbika, what was revealed to you from your Lord, ṭughyānan wa kufran, in wickedness and disbelief, ṭughyān from ṭ-gh-y, to exceed, transgress, and kufr, disbelief, indicates that revelation, which should guide, increases some in transgression because they envy and reject, as *“Revelation increases many in wickedness”* (5:64, 68) repeated. The increase is not because revelation is bad, but because their reaction to it is envy and rejection, which increases existing disease, as *“In hearts is disease, Allah increased disease”* (2:10). The same phenomenon appears for hypocrites: guidance increases them in disbelief when they reject.

**Hostility and Hatred Stirred Until Day of Judgment**

Wa alqaynā baynahum al-'adāwata wa al-baghḍā'a ilā yawm al-qiyāmah — We stirred among them hostility and hatred until Day of Judgment — shows consequence similar to Christians in verse 14. Alqaynā, We cast, stirred, baynahum, among them, al-'adāwata wa al-baghḍā'a, hostility and hatred, as in verse 14, ilā yawm al-qiyāmah, until Day of Judgment, indicates prolonged division among Jews themselves, as history shows sects and enmity. The same phrase appears in verse 14 for Christians and here for Jews, showing that both communities that neglected portion and distorted were left with enmity. The stirring is consequence of saying Allah is tight-fisted and increasing in wickedness when revelation comes, which leads to internal division. The next clause about kindling fire of war shows outward hostility.

**Whenever They Kindle Fire of War, Allah Puts It Out**

Kullamā awqadū nāran li-l-ḥarb aṭfa'ahā Allāh — whenever they kindle fire of war, Allah extinguishes it — describes attempts to wage war. Kullamā, whenever, awqadū nāran, kindle fire, from w-q-d, to kindle, nāran, fire, li-l-ḥarb, for war, aṭfa'ahā Allāh, Allah extinguishes it, aṭfa'a from ṭ-f-', to extinguish, indicates that whenever they try to start war against Muslims or cause strife, Allah puts out fire, preventing success. The same phrase appears in *“Whenever they kindle fire of war, Allah extinguishes”* (5:64) itself. Early reports say Jewish tribes in Madinah tried to incite war between Aws and Khazraj, and Allah extinguished through Prophet's intervention. The phrase shows divine protection of Muslim community from their plots. Wa yas'awna fī al-arḍ fasādan — they strive to spread corruption in land — adds that they strive to spread fasād, corruption, as verse 33 defined waging war and spreading mischief, and Allah does not like corruptors, mufsidīn. The verse therefore ends with condemnation of spreading corruption.

**Extra Verified Note on Yad**

Yad, hand, is among attributes of Allah affirmed in Qur'an and Sunnah without anthropomorphism, as in *“Hand of Allah over their hands”* (48:10) and *“Two hands are open”* (5:64). Early scholars affirmed yad as attribute befitting majesty, without likening to creation, and interpreted maghlūlah, tied, as stingy, and mabsūṭatān, open, as generous, showing that context indicates generosity, not literal hands. The claim yad Allāh maghlūlah was made by some Jews in Madinah when they were in hardship after losing wealth, as Ibn 'Abbās reported. The response bal yadāhu mabsūṭatān, His hands open, affirms generosity. The phrase kullamā awqadū nāran li-l-ḥarb aṭfa'ahā Allāh is used to show that attempts to start war against Muslims fail because Allah protects, as history shows Jewish tribes' plots failed. The next verse will say had People of Book been faithful and mindful, We would have absolved sins and admitted to Gardens of Bliss, offering alternative.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:65

> Had the People of the Book only been faithful and mindful ˹of Allah˺, We would have certainly absolved them of their sins and admitted them into the Gardens of Bliss

**Expanded Commentary**

**Faith and Mindfulness Would Lead to Absolution and Paradise**

Wa law anna ahl al-kitāb āmanū wa-ittaqaw la-kaffarnā 'anhum sayyi'ātihim wa la-adkhalnāhum jannāt al-na'īm offers alternative to previous condemnation. Law anna, if, ahl al-kitāb, People of Book, āmanū, believed, wa-ittaqaw, and were mindful, ittaqaw from w-q-y, to shield, mindfulness, la-kaffarnā 'anhum sayyi'ātihim, We would have absolved sins, kaffarnā from k-f-r, to cover, sayyi'āt, evil deeds, wa la-adkhalnāhum jannāt al-na'īm, admit into Gardens of Bliss, jannāt al-na'īm, Gardens of Bliss, na'īm from n-'-m, bliss, delight, indicates Paradise with bliss. The same conditional appears in *“Had People of Book believed and been mindful, We would have absolved sins”* (5:65) itself. The condition āmanū wa-ittaqaw, believed and were mindful, shows that faith plus mindfulness leads to forgiveness and Paradise, same as for Muslims in verse 9: forgiveness and great reward for belief and good deeds. The verse therefore offers hope: despite previous condemnation for saying Allah tight-fisted and stirring hostility, had they believed and been mindful, forgiveness and Paradise awaited.

**Absolving Sins and Gardens of Bliss as Reward**

La-kaffarnā 'anhum sayyi'ātihim, We would have covered evil deeds, indicates forgiveness that erases sins, and la-adkhalnāhum jannāt al-na'īm, admit into Gardens of Bliss, indicates reward. The pairing appears also for Muslims: forgiveness then great reward (5:9), and for People of Book here: absolution then Gardens of Bliss. The Gardens of Bliss, jannāt al-na'īm, appears also in *“Gardens of Bliss”* (5:65) and in *“In Gardens of Bliss”* (56:12) for righteous, indicating Paradise with bliss, not only gardens. The conditional law, if, shows that outcome depends on faith and mindfulness, not on lineage as People of Book claimed in verse 18 that they are children and beloved. The verse therefore corrects exclusivist claim: being People of Book does not guarantee Paradise; faith and mindfulness does, and had they had it, Paradise would have been theirs.

**A Verse That Offers Hope After Criticism**

This verse offers hope after strong criticism in verses 60-64 that listed worst punishment, cursed, transformed, etc. The hope is conditional: had they believed and been mindful, forgiveness and Gardens of Bliss. The same hope appears for all: *“Had they believed and been mindful”* (5:65) and *“Had they observed Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed”* (5:66) next verse, showing that following revelation leads to provision and forgiveness. The verse's placement after condemnation shows Qur'anic method: critique followed by invitation, as verses 15 and 19 did after describing breach, offering Messenger who clarifies. The invitation is general, not limited to past, so People of Book today can still believe and be mindful and attain Gardens of Bliss. The next verse will add that had they observed Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed from Lord, they would have been overwhelmed with provisions from above and below, showing worldly reward plus otherworldly.

**Why Faith and Mindfulness Are Paired**

Āmanū wa-ittaqaw, believed and were mindful, pairs faith and mindfulness, as many verses do, because faith without mindfulness may be claim without action, and mindfulness without faith may be ritual without foundation. The pairing appears for Muslims in *“Those who believe and are mindful”* (10:63) and for People of Book here, indicating same requirement for all. The reward la-kaffarnā and la-adkhalnāhum shows that forgiveness and Paradise are for those with both. The verse therefore universalizes requirement: being People of Book is not enough, faith and mindfulness needed. The same universalization appears in verse 69 which says believers, Jews, Sabians, Christians, whoever believes in Allah and Last Day and does good, no fear, nor grieve, showing that true success is by faith and good deeds, not by label.

**Extra Verified Note on Jannāt al-Na'īm**

Jannāt al-na'īm, Gardens of Bliss, na'īm from ni'mah, favour, bliss, is among names of Paradise indicating blissful gardens. The phrase appears 8 times in Qur'an for Paradise, often for righteous. The conditional law anna, if, with past tense āmanū wa-ittaqaw, believed and were mindful, indicates hypothetical that did not occur for many, but could have, showing that forgiveness was possible. The verse's promise la-kaffarnā 'anhum sayyi'ātihim, absolve sins, appears also for Muslims who believe and do good, as in *“We will absolve evil deeds”* (29:7). The following verse will add worldly provision for observing Torah, Gospel, and revelation.

**Faith and Mindfulness as Universal Requirement**

Āmanū wa-ittaqaw, believed and were mindful, is universal requirement for all communities, not only People of Book. The same pairing appears for Muslims in *“Believers, be mindful of Allah as He should be feared”* (3:102) and for all who attain success. The reward la-kaffarnā 'anhum sayyi'ātihim, absolve sins, and la-adkhalnāhum jannāt al-na'īm, Gardens of Bliss, shows that forgiveness and Paradise are for those with faith and mindfulness, not for those with lineage. The verse therefore offers hope that even after condemnation for saying Allah tight-fisted and stirring hostility, had they believed and been mindful, forgiveness and Paradise awaited, showing mercy remains open.

**Gardens of Bliss as Abode of Delight**

Jannāt al-na'īm, Gardens of Bliss, na'īm from n-'-m, bliss, delight, indicates Paradise where bliss is continuous, not temporary. The phrase appears for righteous who believe and do good, as in *“Gardens of Bliss”* (5:65) and *“In Gardens of Bliss, upon thrones facing”* (37:44). The bliss includes provision, peace, and pleasure of Allah, which is greater, as *“Allah's pleasure is greater”* (9:72). The conditional law, if they had believed and been mindful, shows that bliss was possible for People of Book had they followed Torah, Gospel, and Qur'an, as next verse will say observing Torah, Gospel, and revelation leads to provisions from above and below. The verse therefore links faith and mindfulness to both forgiveness and bliss, offering alternative to curse and hostility.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:66

> And had they observed the Torah, the Gospel, and what has been revealed to them from their Lord, they would have been overwhelmed with provisions from above and below. Some among them are upright, yet many do nothing but evil

**Expanded Commentary**

**Observing Torah, Gospel, and Revelation Would Bring Provisions**

Wa law annahum aqāmū al-tawrāt wa al-injīl wa mā unzila ilayhim min rabbihim la-akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim offers worldly reward for observing revelation. Law annahum aqāmū, had they observed, aqāmū from q-w-m, to establish, observe properly, al-tawrāt wa al-injīl, Torah and Gospel, wa mā unzila ilayhim min rabbihim, and what was revealed to them from Lord, i.e., Qur'an, as it was revealed to People of Book as well as Muslims, la-akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim — they would have eaten from above and below — akalū from a-k-l, to eat, provision, min fawqihim, from above, rain, crops, and min taḥti arjulihim, from below feet, plants, water, indicating abundance from all directions. The same conditional appears in *“Had they observed Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed, they would have eaten from above and below”* (5:66) itself. The observing, aqāmū, means establishing, following, not merely reading, as *“Establish prayer”* (5:55) means perform properly. The reward is provisions from above and below, indicating worldly abundance that would have come had they followed guidance.

**Provisions From Above and Below as Abundance**

La-akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim, they would have eaten from above and below, is idiom for overwhelming provisions from all directions: rain from above, crops and water from below. The same idiom appears for those who follow guidance, as in *“If they had followed right way, We would have given abundant water”* (72:16). The abundance is both material and spiritual: material provisions and spiritual guidance. The verse therefore says that observing Torah, Gospel, and Qur'an would have led to worldly prosperity plus otherworldly Gardens of Bliss mentioned in previous verse. The conditional law, had they observed, shows that they did not observe, so abundance did not come, and instead hostility and hatred were stirred, as verse 64 said, and many increased in wickedness when revelation came. The verse therefore contrasts what could have been — abundance — with what was — enmity and wickedness.

**Some Among Them Are Upright, Many Do Evil**

Minhum ummatun muqtaṣidah wa kathīrun minhum sā'a mā ya'malūn acknowledges minority upright. Minhum ummatun muqtaṣidah, among them is moderate community, muqtaṣidah from q-ṣ-d, to be moderate, balanced, just, indicates some are upright, moderate, not extreme. Wa kathīrun minhum sā'a mā ya'malūn, many do evil, sā'a from s-w-', to be evil, bad, mā ya'malūn, what they do, indicates many do evil, as previous verses described racing to sin, transgression, forbidden gain. The same acknowledgment appears in *“Among People of Book is upright community”* (3:113) and *“Some are upright, many do evil”* (5:66) itself, showing fairness: Qur'an does not generalize all as evil, but notes some upright, moderate, while many do evil. The upright are those who observed Torah and Gospel truly and recognized Prophet, as verses 82-85 will praise some Christians. The many who do evil are those who said Allah tight-fisted, stirred hostility, kindled fire of war, spread corruption.

**A Verse That Links Observance to Provision**

This verse links observance of revelation to provision, showing that following guidance brings worldly abundance, not only otherworldly reward. The link appears also in *“If people of towns believed and were mindful, We would have opened blessings from heavens and earth”* (7:96) similar. The observance aqāmū includes establishing, following, judging by, as earlier verses commanded judge by what Allah revealed. The reward la-akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim, eat from above and below, indicates that provision would have come from all directions, not only one, showing generosity. The verse's ending with some upright, many evil, shows that even within People of Book, there is diversity, and upright should be recognized. The next verse will command Messenger to convey everything revealed, with protection from people, showing that conveying revelation is duty despite increase in wickedness for many.

**Why Observing Three Books Is Mentioned**

Observing Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed from Lord, i.e., Qur'an, is mentioned because People of Book had Torah and Gospel, and Qur'an was also revealed to them as clarification and protector, as verse 48 said Qur'an is muhaymin, protector over previous books. Observing all three means following true monotheism and preparing for final messenger, as Torah and Gospel contain prophecies of Prophet ﷺ. Had they observed all three, they would have recognized Prophet and followed, leading to provisions from above and below and Gardens of Bliss. Their failure to observe led to increase in wickedness when revelation came, as verse 64 said, and to hostility and hatred until Day of Judgment. The verse therefore offers alternative history: had they observed, abundance, not enmity.

**Extra Verified Note on Aqāmū**

Aqāmū, observed, established, from qāma, to stand, establish, is used for establishing prayer, establishing Torah, etc., meaning to observe properly with pillars and spirit, not merely recite. Lisān al-'Arab defines aqāma as to make stand, establish. The phrase la-akalū min fawqihim wa min taḥti arjulihim, eat from above and below, is idiom for abundance from all directions, as rain from above and plants from below. The same idiom appears in other verses for abundance. The acknowledgment minhum ummatun muqtaṣidah, among them moderate community, appears also in *“Among them moderate community”* (5:66) and is among verses showing Qur'anic fairness in not generalizing. The following verse will command conveying.

**Abundance as Result of Following Guidance**

The promise of eating from above and below shows that following guidance brings material abundance, not only spiritual, because divine law includes justice that leads to prosperity. The same promise appears for those who follow right way, they would have abundant water (72:16), and for people of towns who believe, blessings from heavens and earth would open (7:96). The abundance from above includes rain, sun, and from below includes crops, minerals, water, indicating comprehensive provision. The verse therefore says that had People of Book observed Torah, Gospel, and Qur'an, they would have had worldly abundance plus Gardens of Bliss, showing that following revelation benefits both worlds. Their failure to observe led to hostility and hatred, as verse 64 said, and to increase in wickedness, so they lost both.

**Moderate Community Among Them**

Ummatun muqtaṣidah, moderate community, muqtaṣid from qaṣada, to be moderate, balanced, indicates those who are just, not extreme, who follow truth. The same term appears for moderate among People of Book, showing that some are upright, not all evil. The acknowledgment is important for fairness and for encouraging those moderate to continue. The many who do evil, kathīrun minhum sā'a mā ya'malūn, evil is what they do, shows majority do evil, as previous verses described racing to sin, transgression, forbidden gain, and saying Allah tight-fisted. The verse therefore balances critique with recognition of upright minority, as Qur'an does elsewhere. The next verse will command Messenger to convey everything revealed, with protection.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:67

> O Messenger! Convey everything revealed to you from your Lord. If you do not, then you have not delivered His message. Allah will ˹certainly˺ protect you from the people. Indeed, Allah does not guide the people who disbelieve

**Expanded Commentary**

**Convey Everything Revealed as Duty**

Yā ayyuhā al-rasūl balligh mā unzila ilayka min rabbik commands conveying. Yā ayyuhā al-rasūl, O Messenger, rasūl from r-s-l, to send, messenger, balligh, convey, from b-l-gh, to reach, convey to destination, mā unzila ilayka min rabbik, what was revealed to you from your Lord, indicates all revelation, not part, must be conveyed. The command is emphatic: convey everything, not some, as previous verses warned not to be lured away from some of what was revealed (5:49) and not to hide. The same command appears in *“O Messenger, convey what was revealed”* (5:67) itself. The duty of messenger is to deliver, not to hide for fear of people, as next clause says if you do not, you have not delivered message. The command follows verses about People of Book increasing in wickedness when revelation comes, showing that despite their increase in wickedness, Messenger must still convey.

**If You Do Not, You Have Not Delivered Message**

Wa in lam taf'al fa-mā ballaghta risālatah — if you do not, you have not delivered His message — warns that failing to convey part is like failing to convey all, because message is interconnected. Lam taf'al, you do not do, fa-mā ballaghta, then you have not conveyed, risālatah, His message, indicates that duty is to convey all, not selective. The same warning appears in *“If you do not, you have not delivered message”* (5:67) itself, showing that partial conveyance is considered non-delivery. The warning is severe to emphasize importance of conveying everything, even if it causes increase in wickedness for some, as verse 64 said revelation increases many in wickedness. The Messenger's duty is to convey, not to ensure acceptance, as *“Your duty is only to deliver clearly”* (5:99) will say. The verse therefore establishes that Messenger must convey even difficult rulings about food, ḥudūd, and alliances, despite opposition.

**Allah Will Protect You From People**

Wa Allāhu ya'ṣimuka min al-nās — Allah will protect you from people — promises protection. Ya'ṣimuka, protect you, from '-ṣ-m, to protect, preserve, min al-nās, from people, indicates that Allah will protect Messenger from harm of people who plot, as earlier verses described those who sought to harm and Allah held hands back (5:11) and those who kindle fire of war and Allah extinguishes (5:64). The same promise appears in *“Allah will protect you from people”* (5:67) itself. Early reports say Prophet was guarded by companions until this verse revealed, then he said leave me, Allah will protect me, as recorded in Sunan al-Tirmidhī (3046, graded ḥasan). The protection is both physical and from being prevented from conveying. The promise allows Messenger to convey without fear, because Allah protects from people who would harm for conveying.

**Allah Does Not Guide Disbelieving People**

Inna Allāha lā yahdī al-qawm al-kāfirīn — Allah does not guide disbelieving people — seals with lack of guidance for kāfirīn. Lā yahdī, does not guide, al-qawm al-kāfirīn, disbelieving people, indicates that those who persist in disbelief after revelation conveyed are not guided, because they chose disbelief. The same seal appears in many verses after warnings, as in *“Allah does not guide disbelieving people”* (5:67) and *“Allah does not guide wrongdoing people”* (5:51). The lack of guidance is consequence of disbelief, not arbitrary. The verse therefore says convey, Allah will protect you, and those who disbelieve will not be guided, so do not fear them. The next verse will command saying to People of Book you have nothing to stand on unless you observe Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed from Lord, continuing conveyance.

**A Verse That Commands Conveyance Despite Opposition**

This verse commands conveyance despite opposition and increase in wickedness for many, with promise of protection. It is central to prophetic duty: convey everything, not fear people, trust Allah's protection. The verse's placement after verses about People of Book increasing in wickedness when revelation comes shows that increase in wickedness should not stop conveyance; rather, conveyance must continue. The same command appears for all messengers: convey. The promise ya'ṣimuka min al-nās, protect you from people, is among verses cited for Prophet's protection, and is verified by reports that after revelation, guards left. The verse ends with Allah does not guide disbelieving people, indicating that those who disbelieve after conveyance are not guided because they chose disbelief. The following verse will command saying to People of Book you have nothing to stand on unless you observe Torah, Gospel, and revelation, which is part of conveying everything.

**Extra Verified Note on Balligh**

Balligh, convey, from balāgh, to reach, is used for delivering message to destination, ensuring it reaches. The same root appears in *“Convey from me even one verse”* (Bukhārī 3461) for general conveyance duty for Muslims. The phrase fa-mā ballaghta risālatah, you have not delivered message, shows that partial conveyance is considered non-delivery, emphasizing completeness. The promise ya'ṣimuka min al-nās, Allah will protect you, is verified as reason Prophet dismissed guards, as report in Tirmidhī (3046) says. The next verse will command observing Torah, Gospel, and revelation, which is part of what must be conveyed.

**Protection as Assurance for Conveyance**

Ya'ṣimuka min al-nās, protect you from people, is assurance that allows conveyance without fear. The same protection was shown when Allah held hands back from harming believers (5:11) and extinguished fire of war (5:64). The protection is not only physical but also from being prevented from delivering message, as *“Allah will protect you from people”* (5:67) promises. Early reports say Prophet had guards from Anṣār until verse revealed, then he told them to leave, trusting Allah's protection, as Tirmidhī (3046) records. The assurance shows that Messenger's duty is to convey, and Allah's duty is to protect, so Messenger should not fear people. The next verse will command saying you have nothing to stand on unless you observe Torah, Gospel, and revelation, which is part of conveying that requires protection.

**Conveyance as Complete Delivery**

Balligh mā unzila, convey what was revealed, indicates complete delivery, not selective, because message is interconnected and leaving part is like not delivering at all, as fa-mā ballaghta risālatah, you have not delivered message, warns. The same completeness is required for all who convey after Prophet, as *“Convey from me even one verse”* (Bukhārī 3461) says, indicating that conveying is duty for all Muslims, not only Prophet, though Prophet's duty is to convey everything. The verse's placement after verses about increase in wickedness shows that conveyance must continue even when some increase in disbelief, because guidance is for those who seek, as verses 15-16 said light guides those who seek pleasure to ways of peace.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:68

> Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “O People of the Book! You have nothing to stand on unless you observe the Torah, the Gospel, and what has been revealed to you from your Lord.” And your Lord’s revelation to you ˹O Prophet˺ will only cause many of them to increase in wickedness and disbelief. So do not grieve for the people who disbelieve

**Expanded Commentary**

**You Have Nothing to Stand On Unless You Observe Three**

Qul yā ahl al-kitāb lastum 'alā shay'in ḥattā tuqīmū al-tawrāt wa al-injīl wa mā unzila ilaykum min rabbikum commands saying you have nothing to stand on unless you observe. Qul, say, yā ahl al-kitāb, O People of Book, lastum 'alā shay', you are not upon anything, shay' means thing of substance, foundation, indicates you have no valid foundation, no standing, ḥattā tuqīmū, until you observe, establish, tuqīmū from q-w-m, to stand, establish, al-tawrāt wa al-injīl wa mā unzila ilaykum min rabbikum, Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed to you from Lord, i.e., Qur'an, as previous verse 66 said had they observed Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed, they would have eaten from above and below. The same phrasing appears in *“You have nothing to stand on unless you observe Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed”* (5:68) itself. The observing, tuqīmū, means establishing, following, judging by, not merely reading, as earlier verses commanded judge by what Allah revealed. The verse therefore says People of Book have no valid religious standing unless they observe all three: Torah, Gospel, and Qur'an.

**Revelation Increases Many in Wickedness and Disbelief**

Wa la-yazīdanna kathīran minhum mā unzila ilayka min rabbika ṭughyānan wa kufran repeats from verse 64: what was revealed to you will increase many in wickedness and disbelief. La-yazīdanna, will surely increase, kathīran minhum, many of them, mā unzila ilayka min rabbika, what was revealed to you from Lord, ṭughyānan wa kufran, in wickedness and disbelief, as before, indicates that revelation increases some in transgression because they envy and reject, not because revelation is bad. The same phrase appears in verses 64 and 68, showing that reaction of many People of Book to Qur'an is increase in wickedness, which is why Messenger must still convey despite opposition, as verse 67 commanded convey everything and Allah will protect. The increase is due to their choice, as *“In hearts disease, Allah increased disease”* (2:10) for similar.

**Do Not Grieve for Disbelieving People**

Fa-lā ta'sa 'alā al-qawm al-kāfirīn — do not grieve for disbelieving people — consoles, similar to verse 26's do not grieve for rebellious people. Lā ta'sa, do not grieve, from a-s-y, to grieve, 'alā al-qawm al-kāfirīn, for disbelieving people, indicates that after conveying and they increase in wickedness, Messenger should not grieve, because guidance is from Allah, as verse 67 said Allah does not guide disbelieving people. The same consolation appears in *“Do not grieve for disbelieving people”* (5:68) and in *“Do not grieve for them”* (5:26, 41). The consolation shows that prophet's duty is to convey, not to ensure acceptance, and that increase in wickedness for many should not cause grief because it is their choice. The verse therefore balances command to say you have nothing unless you observe with consolation not to grieve when many increase in disbelief.

**A Verse That Defines Standing**

Lastum 'alā shay', you are not upon anything, indicates no foundation, no valid standing, unless you observe three books. The phrase is strong: you have nothing to stand on, no basis, unless you establish Torah, Gospel, and Qur'an. The observing includes following monotheism, justice, and preparing for final messenger, as Torah and Gospel contain prophecies of Prophet ﷺ. The verse therefore says that People of Book who claim to follow Torah and Gospel but reject Qur'an have no standing, because Qur'an is protector, muhaymin, over previous books, as verse 48 said, and confirms them. The same idea appears for Muslims: they have nothing unless they observe Qur'an. The verse's placement after command to convey everything shows that conveying includes telling People of Book they have no standing unless they observe all three.

**Why Three Books Are Mentioned Together**

Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed from Lord, Qur'an, are mentioned together because they form chain: Torah, then Gospel confirming Torah, then Qur'an confirming both and protector. Observing all three means following true monotheism and recognizing final messenger, as Torah and Gospel contain descriptions of Prophet ﷺ. Had they observed, as verse 66 said, they would have eaten from above and below and been admitted to Gardens of Bliss (5:65). Their failure to observe leads to increase in wickedness and disbelief when Qur'an comes, and to having no standing. The verse therefore defines valid religious standing as observing all revelation, not selective. The next verse will say believers, Jews, Sabians, Christians, whoever believes in Allah and Last Day and does good, no fear, nor grieve, showing that true standing is by faith and good deeds, not by label.

**Extra Verified Note on Lasta 'Alā Shay'**

Lasta 'alā shay', you are not upon anything, is idiom meaning you have no valid foundation, no substance, as in Arabic you are not upon thing means you have no basis. The phrase appears also for those who have no standing unless they follow guidance. The command tuqīmū, observe, establish, from qāma, to stand, means to establish properly, as in establishing prayer. The same verb appears for observing Torah and Gospel in verses 66 and 68, showing that observing means establishing with pillars and spirit. The verse's ending fa-lā ta'sa 'alā al-qawm al-kāfirīn, do not grieve for disbelieving people, appears also in verse 26 for rebellious and verse 41 for those racing to disbelief, showing consistent consolation for prophets when people reject. The following verse will give universal principle for salvation.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:69

> Indeed, the believers, Jews, Sabians and Christians—whoever ˹truly˺ believes in Allah and the Last Day and does good, there will be no fear for them, nor will they grieve

**Expanded Commentary**

**Believers, Jews, Sabians, Christians and Universal Principle**

Inna alladhīna āmanū wa alladhīna hādū wa al-ṣābi'ūn wa al-naṣārā states groups. Alladhīna āmanū, believers, Muslims, wa alladhīna hādū, Jews, from h-w-d, to be Jewish, return, wa al-ṣābi'ūn, Sabians, group known for following previous scriptures or star worshippers, wa al-naṣārā, Christians, indicates four groups. The same grouping appears in *“Believers, Jews, Christians, Sabians”* (2:62) with similar principle. The verse then gives universal principle: man āmana bi-Llāhi wa al-yawm al-ākhir wa 'amila ṣāliḥan — whoever believes in Allah and Last Day and does good — man, whoever, āmana bi-Llāhi, believes in Allah, wa al-yawm al-ākhir, Last Day, wa 'amila ṣāliḥan, does good, ṣāliḥan from ṣ-l-ḥ, sound, good, indicates that true success is by faith in Allah and Last Day and good deeds, not by label. The same principle appears in *“Whoever believes in Allah and Last Day and does good”* (5:69) itself and in 2:62. The verse therefore universalizes salvation: it is not by being Jew, Christian, Sabian, or Muslim by label, but by true belief and good deeds.

**No Fear, Nor Grieve as Reward**

Fa-lā khawfun 'alayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn — no fear for them, nor will they grieve — gives reward. Lā khawfun 'alayhim, no fear upon them, from kh-w-f, fear, and lā hum yaḥzanūn, nor they grieve, from ḥ-z-n, grief, indicates that in Hereafter, they will have no fear of punishment and no grief over loss, because they believed and did good. The same reward appears in many verses for believers, as in *“No fear for them, nor grieve”* (2:62, 5:69) itself. The reward is both worldly and otherworldly: no fear of future and no grief over past. The verse therefore offers hope after previous verses that said many People of Book increase in wickedness and have no standing unless they observe Torah, Gospel, Qur'an. It says that among those groups, whoever truly believes in Allah and Last Day and does good will have no fear nor grief, showing that label alone does not save, but true faith and good deeds do.

**Why This Verse Follows No Standing Verse**

This verse follows verse 68 which said you have nothing to stand on unless you observe Torah, Gospel, and what was revealed from Lord. Verse 69 clarifies that observing means true belief in Allah and Last Day and good deeds, not merely claiming label. The verse therefore defines what observing means: belief and good deeds. The same principle appears in verse 65 which said had People of Book believed and been mindful, We would have absolved sins and admitted to Gardens of Bliss, and verse 66 which said had they observed Torah, Gospel, and revelation, they would have eaten from above and below. Verse 69 gives universal formula: belief in Allah, Last Day, good deeds, leads to no fear nor grief. The verse therefore balances previous critique that many People of Book increase in wickedness with hope that whoever among them truly believes and does good will succeed.

**A Verse Often Cited for Pluralism**

This verse is often cited in discussions of religious pluralism, because it mentions Jews, Sabians, Christians alongside believers and gives universal principle of belief and good deeds leading to no fear nor grief. Early exegetes debated whether it refers to those before Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ who followed true Torah and Gospel before they were distorted, or whether it includes those after who follow true monotheism and do good. The majority say it refers to those who believed in their time and then believed in final messenger when he came, because believing in Allah includes believing in His messengers, as verse 12 said covenant required belief in messengers. The verse therefore does not mean that one can reject final messenger and still have no fear, but that among those groups, whoever truly believes in Allah, Last Day, and does good, including believing in final messenger, will have no fear. The next verse will describe covenant with Children of Israel and messengers they denied and killed.

**Extra Verified Note on Ṣābi'ūn**

Al-ṣābi'ūn, Sabians, are identified as group who followed previous scriptures, or who worshipped stars, or who were between Jews and Christians, with various interpretations. Al-Ṭabarī records several views, indicating uncertainty, but includes them among People of Book. The universal principle man āmana bi-Llāhi wa al-yawm al-ākhir wa 'amila ṣāliḥan appears also in 2:62 with same groups, showing consistent principle. The reward lā khawfun 'alayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn, no fear nor grief, appears over 10 times for believers, indicating that true success is absence of fear and grief in Hereafter. The following verse will describe covenant and messengers denied and killed, showing failure of many among Children of Israel to meet universal principle.

**Belief in Allah and Last Day and Good Deeds as Core**

Man āmana bi-Llāhi wa al-yawm al-ākhir wa 'amila ṣāliḥan, whoever believes in Allah and Last Day and does good, defines core of faith that leads to no fear nor grief. Belief in Allah includes belief in His oneness, His messengers, His books, as earlier verses about covenant required belief in messengers (5:12). Belief in Last Day includes accountability, as verses 36-37 showed disbelievers cannot ransom and want to exit Fire but cannot. Doing good, 'amila ṣāliḥan, includes establishing prayer, paying alms, being just, fulfilling contracts, as earlier verses commanded. The verse therefore says that among believers, Jews, Sabians, Christians, whoever has true belief and good deeds will have no fear nor grief, showing that label is not enough, but core faith and deeds matter. The next verse will show covenant with Children of Israel and how they denied and killed messengers, failing core.

**No Fear Nor Grief as Comprehensive Reward**

Lā khawfun 'alayhim wa lā hum yaḥzanūn, no fear for them nor grieve, is comprehensive reward: no fear of future punishment and no grief over past loss, because they believed and did good. The same reward appears for those who believe and are mindful (10:62) and for those who spend in Allah's way (2:262). The reward is both worldly, peace of heart, and otherworldly, Paradise. The verse therefore offers hope after critique that many People of Book have no standing unless they observe Torah, Gospel, Qur'an. It says that among them, whoever truly believes and does good will have no fear nor grief, showing that true success is by faith and deeds, not label. The following verse will describe how Children of Israel broke covenant by denying and killing messengers.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:70

> Indeed, We took a covenant from the Children of Israel and sent them messengers. Whenever a messenger came to them with what they did not desire, they denied some and killed others

**Expanded Commentary**

**Covenant From Children of Israel and Messengers Sent**

Laqad akhadhnā mīthāq banī Isrā'īl wa arsalnā ilayhim rusulan reports covenant and messengers. Laqad akhadhnā, We took, mīthāq, covenant, firm bond, banī Isrā'īl, Children of Israel, as in verse 12, and arsalnā ilayhim rusulan, sent to them messengers, rusulan plural of rasūl, messenger, indicates many messengers sent to them, not one, as history shows many prophets among Israelites. The covenant included establishing prayer, paying alms, believing in messengers, supporting them, lending good loan, as verse 12 detailed, and not killing messengers. The sending of messengers is part of divine mercy: after covenant, messengers remind and clarify, as verse 15 said Messenger reveals much hidden. The same phrasing appears in *“We took covenant from Children of Israel and sent messengers”* (5:70) itself. The verse therefore recalls that Children of Israel had covenant and many messengers, so they had ample guidance, yet response was denial and killing.

**Whenever Messenger Came With What They Did Not Desire, Denied Some and Killed Others**

Kullamā jā'ahum rasūlun bi-mā lā tahwā anfusuhum farīqan kadhdhabū wa farīqan yaqtulūn describes response. Kullamā, whenever, jā'ahum rasūlun, messenger came to them, bi-mā lā tahwā anfusuhum, with what souls did not desire, tahwā from h-w-y, to desire, love, anfusuhum, souls, selves, indicates that messengers brought rulings that souls disliked, such as forbidding unlawful gain, enjoining justice, etc. Farīqan kadhdhabū wa farīqan yaqtulūn — some they denied, some they killed — farīq, group, kadhdhabū, denied, from k-dh-b, to deny, and yaqtulūn, kill, from q-t-l, indicates that whenever messenger came with undesired ruling, they responded with denial for some and killing for others. The same phrasing appears in *“Whenever messenger came with what they did not desire, they denied some and killed others”* (5:70) and in *“Whenever messenger came, they denied”* (2:87) similar. The pattern shows that following desire, hawā, leads to denying and killing messengers, which is worst rebellion.

**A History of Denial and Killing**

The history of Children of Israel includes denying and killing prophets, as Qur'an says *“They killed prophets unjustly”* (2:61) and *“They killed prophets”* (3:21). Examples include Zakariyyā, Yaḥyā, and attempts to kill 'Īsā, as *“They did not kill him nor crucify him”* (4:157) indicates attempt. The pattern kullamā jā'ahum rasūlun bi-mā lā tahwā anfusuhum shows that criterion for acceptance was desire, not truth: if messenger brought what souls desired, they accepted, if not, denied or killed. The same criterion appears for those who take rulings out of context, saying if you are given this, take it, if not, beware (5:41), showing desire-based acceptance. The verse therefore diagnoses that following desire leads to worst crimes: denying and killing messengers. The next verse will say they thought no consequences, turned blind and deaf, then Allah turned in forgiveness, but many became blind and deaf again, showing persistence.

**Why This Verse Follows Universal Principle**

This verse follows verse 69 which gave universal principle that whoever believes in Allah, Last Day, does good, no fear nor grief, regardless of label. Verse 70 shows that Children of Israel had covenant and messengers, yet whenever messenger came with undesired, they denied and killed, failing universal principle. The failure was not due to lack of messengers but due to following desire. The verse therefore illustrates why many among People of Book have no standing unless they observe Torah, Gospel, and revelation, as verse 68 said, and why many increase in wickedness when revelation comes, as verses 64 and 68 said. The history serves as warning for Muslims not to follow desire and deny or harm messengers, which in this era means rejecting Sunnah or harming scholars.

**A Verse That Summarizes Prophetic Experience**

This verse summarizes prophetic experience among Israelites: covenant taken, messengers sent, but whenever messenger brought undesired, denied some, killed others. The same experience appears for Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ: covenant with Muslims, messenger sent, but some with sickness in hearts raced for guardianship, swore false oaths, and mocked, as previous verses described. The pattern is consistent: following desire leads to rejecting messenger. The verse's ending with farīqan kadhdhabū wa farīqan yaqtulūn, some denied, some killed, shows two levels of rejection: denial and killing, both grave. The next verse will say they thought no consequences, turned blind and deaf, showing that denial and killing stem from thinking no consequences.

**Extra Verified Note on Killing Prophets**

Killing prophets is verified as among major sins of Israelites, as Qur'an says *“They killed prophets unjustly”* (2:61, 3:21, 4:155) multiple times. The phrase bi-mā lā tahwā anfusuhum, with what souls do not desire, appears also in *“Whenever messenger came with what they did not desire”* (2:87, 5:70) showing that desire-based acceptance is pattern. The verse's placement after universal principle shows that despite universal principle being clear, many fail due to following desire. The following verse will describe blindness and deafness as consequence of thinking no consequences.

**Covenant and Messengers as Ample Guidance**

Laqad akhadhnā mīthāq banī Isrā'īl wa arsalnā ilayhim rusulan, We took covenant and sent messengers, shows ample guidance: covenant plus many messengers, not one, so they had no excuse, as verse 19 said Messenger came after interval so you do not say no warner came. The covenant included prayer, alms, belief in messengers, support, good loan (5:12), and messengers reminded of it. The response kullamā jā'ahum rasūlun bi-mā lā tahwā anfusuhum, whenever messenger came with undesired, they denied and killed, shows that guidance was rejected not for lack but for desire. The same pattern appears for those who distort and take rulings out of context, saying if given this, take it, if not, beware (5:41), showing desire-based acceptance. The next verse will say they thought no consequences, turned blind and deaf, showing that following desire leads to thinking no consequences.

**Denial and Killing as Worst Rebellion**

Farīqan kadhdhabū wa farīqan yaqtulūn, some denied, some killed, shows two levels of rejection, both grave, with killing worse. The history includes killing Zakariyyā, Yaḥyā, and attempting to kill 'Īsā, as *“They did not kill him nor crucify”* (4:157) indicates attempt. The Qur'an says *“They killed prophets unjustly”* (2:61, 3:21) multiple times, showing that killing prophets is characteristic of those who follow desire. The verse therefore warns that following desire can lead to worst crimes, and that true belief requires following messenger even when he brings undesired, not only desired. The following verse will describe blindness and deafness as consequence.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:71

> They thought there would be no consequences, so they turned a blind eye and a deaf ear. Yet Allah turned to them in forgiveness ˹after their repentance˺, but again many became blind and deaf. And Allah is All-Seeing of what they do

**Expanded Commentary**

**Thought No Consequences, Turned Blind and Deaf**

Wa ḥasibū allā takūna fitnah fa-'amū wa ṣammū reports thinking no trial would come. Ḥasibū, thought, from ḥ-s-b, to think, reckon, allā takūna fitnah, there would be no trial or consequence, fitnah from f-t-n, to test with fire, trial, punishment, indicates they thought no punishment would afflict for denying and killing messengers, as previous verse described. Fa-'amū wa ṣammū — turned blind and deaf — 'amū from '-m-y, blind, and ṣammū from ṣ-m-m, deaf, indicates they became blind to signs and deaf to warnings, as *“Deaf, dumb, blind, they do not return”* (2:18) for similar. The blindness and deafness are moral, not physical: they chose not to see truth and not to hear warning because they thought no consequences. The same phrase appears in *“They turned blind and deaf”* (5:71) itself. The thinking no consequences led to moral blindness and deafness, which then led to further denial and killing, as previous verse said.

**Allah Turned in Forgiveness After Repentance**

Thumma tāba Allāhu 'alayhim — then Allah turned to them in forgiveness — shows mercy after blindness and deafness. Tāba Allāhu 'alayhim, Allah turned to them, from t-w-b, to return, indicates Allah returned with forgiveness when they repented, as *“Allah turned to them”* (5:71) itself. The forgiveness was after repentance, as early reports say after worshipping calf and other sins, they repented and Allah forgave, as *“Then Allah turned to you, forgiving”* (2:54) for calf worship. The turning shows that even after denying and killing messengers and becoming blind and deaf, forgiveness remains possible with repentance, as verse 39 said whoever repents after wrongdoing and mends, Allah turns in forgiveness. The mercy is shown to encourage repentance, not to condone persistence.

**Again Many Became Blind and Deaf**

Thumma 'amiya kathīrun minhum wa ṣammū — then many became blind and deaf again — shows recurrence. Thumma, then, 'amiya kathīrun minhum, many became blind, wa ṣammū, and deaf, indicates that after forgiveness, many returned to blindness and deafness, showing persistence in following desire. The same phrase appears in *“Many became blind and deaf”* (5:71) itself, with kathīrun, many, indicating majority, not all, as some remained upright, as verse 66 said some are moderate. The recurrence shows that forgiveness alone does not prevent relapse unless heart softens, as earlier verses about hard hearts (5:13) and forgetting portion (5:13-14) showed. The pattern is: thought no consequences, became blind and deaf, Allah forgave after repentance, but many became blind and deaf again, indicating cycle of sin, repentance, relapse.

**Allah Is All-Seeing of What They Do**

Wa Allāhu Baṣīr bi-mā ya'malūn — Allah is All-Seeing of what they do — seals with divine sight. Baṣīr, All-Seeing, from b-ṣ-r, to see, indicates Allah sees all deeds, including denial, killing, blindness, deafness, repentance, relapse. Bi-mā ya'malūn, what they do, includes all actions. The same seal appears in many verses after describing deeds, as in *“Allah is All-Seeing of what you do”* (5:71) itself and *“Allah is All-Seeing of what they do”* (2:96) for similar. The seeing is both knowledge and accountability: nothing hidden from sight. The verse therefore ends with reminder that despite thinking no consequences, Allah sees and will recompense. The next verse will discuss those who say Allah is Messiah, showing another form of blindness and deafness: theological blindness.

**A Verse That Describes Cycle of Sin and Forgiveness**

This verse describes cycle: thought no fitnah, became blind and deaf, Allah forgave, many became blind and deaf again, Allah sees what they do. The cycle appears for many communities: sin, thinking no consequences, blindness, forgiveness after repentance, relapse. The verse's placement after verse 70's denial and killing shows that denial and killing stem from thinking no consequences, which leads to blindness and deafness, which prevents seeing truth. The remedy is repentance that leads to forgiveness, but many relapse, showing need for continual mindfulness, as verse 7 said remember favour and covenant and be mindful, Allah knows what is in breasts. The verse therefore warns against thinking no consequences, because Allah is All-Seeing.

**Extra Verified Note on Fitnah**

Fitnah, trial, from fatana, to test with fire, means trial, punishment, discord, and here means consequences, punishment for denying and killing. Lisān al-'Arab defines fitnah as trial, test, and also as punishment. The phrase ḥasibū allā takūna fitnah, thought no trial would be, appears also for those who thought no consequences for breaking covenant. The blindness 'amū and deafness ṣammū are used for moral inability to see and hear truth, as in *“Deaf, dumb, blind”* (2:18, 2:171) for similar. The turning tāba Allāhu 'alayhim, Allah turned to them, indicates forgiveness after repentance, as in *“Then Allah turned to them”* (5:71) itself. The recurrence thumma 'amiya kathīrun minhum wa ṣammū shows that many relapse after forgiveness, indicating need for steadfastness. The following verse will discuss disbelief of those saying Allah is Messiah, another form of blindness.

**Thinking No Consequences as Root of Blindness**

Ḥasibū allā takūna fitnah, thought no trial, shows root of blindness and deafness: thinking no consequences leads to ignoring warnings, so they become blind and deaf. The same root appears for those who broke covenant and thought no curse, but curse came as hard hearts (5:13). The thinking no fitnah is itself fitnah, trial, because it leads to persistence. The verse therefore warns that thinking no consequences is dangerous, because Allah is Baṣīr, All-Seeing, of what they do, so consequences will come. The cycle of sin, forgiveness, relapse shows need for continual taqwā, mindfulness, not only one-time repentance. The next verse will discuss theological blindness of saying Allah is Messiah, showing that blindness can be doctrinal as well as moral.

**Forgiveness After Repentance as Mercy**

Thumma tāba Allāhu 'alayhim, Allah turned in forgiveness, shows mercy that remains even after denial and killing and blindness. The same mercy appears for calf worshippers who repented and were forgiven (2:54) and for those who repent after wrongdoing and mend (5:39). The forgiveness is conditional on repentance, as early reports say they repented after Moses' intercession. The recurrence thumma 'amiya kathīrun minhum wa ṣammū, many became blind and deaf again, shows that forgiveness alone does not prevent relapse; steadfastness and mindfulness needed, as verse 7 said remember favour and covenant and be mindful, Allah knows what is in breasts. The verse therefore teaches that after forgiveness, vigilance needed to avoid relapse into blindness and deafness.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:72

> Those who say, “Allah is the Messiah, son of Mary,” have certainly fallen into disbelief. The Messiah ˹himself˺ said, “O Children of Israel! Worship Allah—my Lord and your Lord.” Whoever associates others with Allah ˹in worship˺ will surely be forbidden Paradise by Allah. Their home will be the Fire. And the wrongdoers will have no helpers

**Expanded Commentary**

**Saying Allah Is Messiah as Disbelief Repeated**

Laqad kafara alladhīna qālū inna Allāha huwa al-Masīḥ ibn Maryam repeats judgment from verse 17: those who say Allah is Messiah have disbelieved. Laqad kafara, indeed disbelieved, as before, indicates that claim nullifies tawḥīd. The repetition from verse 17 shows emphasis, because claim is grave and appears among Christians, as earlier verses about Trinity will show. The Messiah himself, al-Masīḥ, said yā banī Isrā'īl u'budū Allāha rabbī wa rabbakum — O Children of Israel, worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord — showing that Jesus himself called to worship Allah, not himself. The same call appears in *“Worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord”* (5:72) and in *“Indeed Allah is my Lord and your Lord, so worship Him”* (3:51) from Jesus. The call uses rabbī wa rabbakum, my Lord and your Lord, indicating that Jesus has Lord and they have same Lord, so he is servant, not Lord. The verse therefore refutes divinity claim by quoting Messiah's own monotheistic call.

**Worship Allah, My Lord and Your Lord as Core Message**

U'budū Allāha rabbī wa rabbakum, worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord, is core message of all messengers, as *“We sent no messenger before you except We revealed to him, there is no god but Me, so worship Me”* (21:25). Jesus' message is same: worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord. The phrase rabbī wa rabbakum shows that Jesus acknowledges Allah as his Lord, so he is not Allah, and that his Lord is same as theirs, so all should worship same Lord. The same phrase appears for Jesus in *“Allah is my Lord and your Lord, worship Him”* (19:36) and for other messengers. The verse therefore shows that Messiah's own words contradict claim that he is Allah; he says worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord, not worship me. The claim that Allah is Messiah is thus disbelief that contradicts Messiah's own teaching.

**Whoever Associates Others With Allah Forbidden Paradise**

Innahu man yushrik bi-Llāh fa-qad ḥarrama Allāhu 'alayhi al-jannah wa ma'wāhu al-nār — whoever associates with Allah, Allah has forbidden Paradise for him, abode Fire — gives consequence of shirk. Man yushrik bi-Llāh, whoever associates with Allah, shirk from sh-r-k, to share, associate, means to associate other with Allah in worship, such as saying Allah is Messiah or Trinity. Fa-qad ḥarrama Allāhu 'alayhi al-jannah, Allah forbade Paradise for him, ḥarrama from ḥ-r-m, to forbid, indicates Paradise forbidden, cannot enter. Wa ma'wāhu al-nār, abode Fire, ma'wā from a-w-y, to shelter, abode, indicates Fire is home. The same consequence appears in *“Whoever associates with Allah, Paradise forbidden, abode Fire”* (5:72) itself and in *“Indeed Allah does not forgive association, but forgives less than that”* (4:48, 116). The verse therefore warns that saying Allah is Messiah is shirk that leads to Paradise forbidden and Fire as abode.

**Wrongdoers Will Have No Helpers**

Wa mā li-l-ẓālimīn min anṣār — wrongdoers will have no helpers — closes. Ẓālimīn, wrongdoers, as before, those who place worship not in its place, associating with Allah, and anṣār, helpers, plural of naṣīr, helper, from n-ṣ-r, to help, indicates that on Day of Judgment, no one can help those who associated. The same phrase appears in *“Wrongdoers will have no helpers”* (5:72) and in many verses for those who do shirk. The lack of helpers shows that shirk is not forgiven unless repented before death, and that no intercession benefits those who die in shirk without repentance. The verse therefore ends with warning that associating leads to no help. The next verse will discuss Trinity, saying Allah is one in Trinity have disbelieved, showing another form of association.

**A Verse That Quotes Messiah to Refute Deification**

This verse quotes Messiah himself to refute deification, method of using opponent's accepted authority against claim. Since Christians accept Jesus as Messiah, quoting his call to worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord, refutes claim that he is Allah. The same method appears for other claims: quoting Torah to refute distortion, as verse 44-45 did. The verse's repetition of laqad kafara from verse 17 emphasizes that claim is disbelief, not mere error, because it nullifies core of faith. The consequence Paradise forbidden and Fire as abode shows severity. A verified ḥadīth in Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī (3445) says do not exaggerate about me as Christians exaggerated about son of Mary, linking exaggeration to deification, supporting verse's warning. The next verse will discuss Trinity, another exaggeration.

**Extra Verified Note on Shirk and Paradise Forbidden**

The principle man yushrik bi-Llāh fa-qad ḥarrama Allāhu 'alayhi al-jannah, whoever associates, Paradise forbidden, is among most severe warnings, and is used to show that shirk is unforgiven if not repented before death, as *“Allah does not forgive association”* (4:48). The phrase ma'wāhu al-nār, abode Fire, indicates that Fire is permanent home for those who die in shirk without repentance. The phrase mā li-l-ẓālimīn min anṣār, no helpers for wrongdoers, appears also in *“Wrongdoers have no helpers”* (2:270, 3:192) for similar. The verse's quotation of Messiah's words yā banī Isrā'īl u'budū Allāha rabbī wa rabbakum is also in Gospel of Mark 12:29 where Jesus says Hear O Israel, Lord our God is One, supporting monotheistic call. The following verse will discuss Trinity.

**Messiah's Call as Proof Against Deification**

The Messiah's call yā banī Isrā'īl u'budū Allāha rabbī wa rabbakum, O Children of Israel, worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord, is proof against deification because it shows Messiah himself worships Allah and calls others to worship same Lord. The phrase rabbī wa rabbakum, my Lord and your Lord, indicates that Messiah has Lord and that his Lord is same as theirs, so he is servant, not Lord. The same call appears for all messengers: worship Allah alone. The verse therefore uses Messiah's own words to refute claim that he is Allah, method that is powerful because it uses accepted authority. The consequence man yushrik bi-Llāh fa-qad ḥarrama Allāhu 'alayhi al-jannah, Paradise forbidden, Fire as abode, no helpers, shows severity of shirk that deifies Messiah.

**Paradise Forbidden as Consequence of Shirk**

Fa-qad ḥarrama Allāhu 'alayhi al-jannah, Paradise forbidden, indicates that shirk prevents entry to Paradise, as *“Allah does not forgive association”* (4:48) states. The forbidding is not temporary but permanent for those who die in shirk without repentance. The abode Fire, ma'wāhu al-nār, indicates that Fire becomes home, not temporary visit. The phrase mā li-l-ẓālimīn min anṣār, no helpers for wrongdoers, shows that on Day, no intercession benefits those who die in shirk. The verse therefore warns that saying Allah is Messiah is shirk that leads to Paradise forbidden and Fire as home, with no helpers, so it should be avoided. The next verse will discuss Trinity, another form of shirk.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:73

> Those who say, “Allah is one in a Trinity,” have certainly fallen into disbelief. There is only One God. If they do not stop saying this, those who disbelieve among them will be afflicted with a painful punishment

**Expanded Commentary**

**Saying Allah Is Third of Three as Disbelief**

Laqad kafara alladhīna qālū inna Allāha thālithu thalāthah repeats judgment for Trinity claim. Laqad kafara, indeed disbelieved, as before, alladhīna qālū inna Allāha thālithu thalāthah, those who say Allah is third of three, thālithu thalāthah, third of three, indicates Trinity doctrine that Allah is one of three gods: Father, Son, Holy Spirit. The Qur'an judges this as kufr, disbelief, because it associates with Allah and says three while Allah is One. The same judgment appears in *“Those who say Allah is third of three have disbelieved”* (5:73) itself. The claim is similar to previous verse's claim Allah is Messiah, but adds third, indicating more complex association. The verse therefore refutes Trinity as disbelief, not mere error, because it nullifies tawḥīd.

**There Is Only One God as Correction**

Wa mā min ilāhin illā ilāhun wāḥid — there is no god except One God — corrects. Mā min ilāhin illā ilāhun wāḥid, no god except One God, ilāh, god, deity, wāḥid, One, indicates absolute oneness, tawḥīd, that there is only One God, not three. The same correction appears in *“There is only One God”* (5:73) and in *“Your God is One God”* (2:163) and *“Say, He is Allah, One”* (112:1). The oneness is not only in number but in essence, attributes, and right to worship. The Trinity claim says three, but verse says only One, so three is false. The correction is simple and direct: no god except One, so claim of third of three is disbelief. The next clause warns that if they do not stop, painful punishment will afflict disbelievers among them.

**If They Do Not Stop, Painful Punishment**

Wa in lam yantahū 'ammā yaqūlūna la-yamassanna alladhīna kafarū minhum 'adhābun alīm — if they do not stop saying this, those who disbelieve among them will be afflicted with painful punishment — warns. In lam yantahū, if they do not stop, yantahū from n-h-y, to stop, forbid, 'ammā yaqūlūna, from what they say, Trinity, la-yamassanna, will surely afflict, yamassanna from m-s-s, to touch, afflict, alladhīna kafarū minhum, those who disbelieve among them, 'adhābun alīm, painful punishment, alīm from a-l-m, painful, indicates that persistent Trinity claim after warning leads to painful punishment. The same warning appears in *“If they do not stop, painful punishment will afflict disbelievers”* (5:73) itself. The punishment is for those who disbelieve among them, indicating that not all who were raised in Trinity are necessarily disbelievers if they did not understand, but those who persist after clear warning are. The verse therefore offers chance to stop, as next verse will call to repentance and forgiveness.

**A Verse That Refutes Trinity**

This verse refutes Trinity, doctrine that Allah is third of three, as disbelief, and affirms only One God. The refutation is simple: no god except One, so three is false. The same refutation appears for claim Allah is Messiah in verses 17 and 72, and for claim Allah is third of three here, showing that all forms of associating Messiah with Allah are disbelief. The warning of painful punishment for those who do not stop shows that persistence after warning leads to punishment, but stopping and repenting leads to forgiveness, as next verse will say will they not turn to Allah in repentance and seek forgiveness, Allah is Forgiving, Merciful. The verse therefore balances warning with hope, as earlier verses did.

**Why Trinity Is Judged as Disbelief**

Trinity is judged as disbelief because it says three gods while Allah is One, and because it associates Messiah and Holy Spirit with Allah in divinity, which is shirk, association, as verse 72 said whoever associates, Paradise forbidden, Fire abode. The Qur'an says *“They disbelieve who say Allah is third of three, there is only One God”* (5:73) itself, linking disbelief to saying three. The doctrine of Trinity developed after Jesus, with councils, and was not taught by Jesus himself, who said worship Allah, my Lord and your Lord (5:72). The verse therefore returns to Messiah's own monotheistic call to refute Trinity. The next verse will call to repentance and forgiveness, offering exit.

**Extra Verified Note on Thālithu Thalāthah**

Thālithu thalāthah, third of three, is interpreted as one of three gods, Trinity, Father, Son, Holy Spirit, as in Christian doctrine. The phrase appears only here in Qur'an, showing specific refutation of Trinity. The correction mā min ilāhin illā ilāhun wāḥid, no god except One, appears also in other verses for oneness. The warning la-yamassanna alladhīna kafarū minhum 'adhābun alīm, painful punishment will afflict disbelievers among them, appears also for other persistent disbelief, showing that persistence after warning leads to punishment, but repentance leads to forgiveness, as next verse will say. The following verse will call to repentance.

**Trinity as Association and Disbelief**

The claim inna Allāha thālithu thalāthah, Allah is third of three, is association, shirk, because it says three gods, while Allah is One, wāḥid, as verse corrects mā min ilāhin illā ilāhun wāḥid, no god except One. The same correction appears for all forms of shirk: no god except One. The warning in lam yantahū, if they do not stop, painful punishment will afflict disbelievers, shows that persistence after warning leads to punishment, but stopping and repenting leads to forgiveness, as next verse will call to repentance. The verse therefore offers chance to stop, as earlier verses offered forgiveness for repentance after wrongdoing and mending (5:39) and for repentance before seizure (5:34). The next verse will ask will they not turn to Allah in repentance and seek forgiveness, Allah is Forgiving, Merciful, offering exit from Trinity claim.

**One God as Foundation**

Ilāhun wāḥid, One God, is foundation of all revelation, as Torah, Gospel, Qur'an all contain guidance and light calling to One God, as verses 44 and 46 said Torah and Gospel contain guidance and light. The Trinity claim contradicts foundation, so it is judged as disbelief, laqad kafara, indeed disbelieved.
