## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:96

> It is lawful for you to hunt and eat seafood, as a provision for you and for travellers. But hunting on land is forbidden to you while on pilgrimage. Be mindful of Allah to Whom you all will be gathered

**Expanded Commentary**

**Lawful Seafood Hunting and Eating as Provision**

Uḥilla lakum ṣaydu al-baḥri wa ṭa'āmuhu matā'an lakum wa li-al-sayyārati wa ḥurrima 'alaykum ṣaydu al-barri mā dumtum ḥuruman wa ittaqū Allāha alladhī ilayhi tuḥsharūn declares: lawful for you is game of sea and its food as provision for you and travellers, forbidden for you is game of land while you are in pilgrimage, be mindful of Allah to Whom you will be gathered. Uḥilla lakum, made lawful for you, from ḥ-l-l, ṣaydu al-baḥri, game of sea, ṣayd, hunting, baḥr, sea, wa ṭa'āmuhu, and its food, from ṭ-'-m, matā'an, provision, enjoyment, from m-t-', lakum, for you, wa li-al-sayyārati, and for travellers, sayyārah from s-y-r, travellers, wa ḥurrima 'alaykum, forbidden upon you, from ḥ-r-m, ṣaydu al-barri, game of land, barr, land, mā dumtum ḥuruman, as long as you are in pilgrimage, ḥurum, muḥrim, wa ittaqū Allāha, be mindful of Allah, alladhī ilayhi tuḥsharūn — to Whom you will be gathered — ḥashara, to gather.

**Sea Game Versus Land Game Distinction**

Ṣaydu al-baḥri wa ṭa'āmuhu, game of sea and its food, versus ṣaydu al-barri, game of land, distinguishes two types: sea game lawful even while muḥrim, land game forbidden while muḥrim. Ṣaydu al-baḥr, sea game, includes what is hunted from sea, fish, etc., ṭa'āmuhu, its food, includes what sea throws, dead fish, or salted, dried, as jurists differ: some say ṣayd is fresh caught, ṭa'ām is what dead or preserved, both lawful. The distinction shows mercy: pilgrim can still eat seafood while forbidden land game, so provision not cut. Matā'an lakum wa li-al-sayyārati — provision for you and travellers — indicates seafood provision for residents and travellers, both can benefit, not restricted.

**Provision for You and Travellers**

Matā'an lakum wa li-al-sayyārati — provision for you and travellers — matā', provision, enjoyment, temporary use, lakum, for you, residents, wa li-al-sayyārati, and for travellers, sayyārah, those traveling, indicates seafood lawful as provision for all, not only muḥrim, but especially useful for muḥrim who cannot hunt land. The same phrase matā'an lakum appears for other provisions, showing that lawful foods are provision, not merely permission, but gift for enjoyment with gratitude. The verse therefore shows that while land hunting forbidden in iḥrām, sea hunting and eating remains lawful as provision.

Wa ḥurrima 'alaykum ṣaydu al-barri mā dumtum ḥuruman — forbidden upon you is game of land as long as you are in pilgrimage — ḥurrima 'alaykum, forbidden upon you, ṣaydu al-barri, hunting land, game of land, mā dumtum ḥuruman, as long as you are ḥurum, in iḥrām, ḥurum plural of ḥarām, muḥrim. The prohibition is temporary, while in iḥrām, not permanent, showing that iḥrām imposes additional sanctity, similar to Ḥaram sanctity. The prohibition includes killing, hunting, assisting, as jurists detail, to preserve wildlife in sacred state and test obedience, as verse 94 said test with game within reach.

**Be Mindful of Allah to Whom Gathered**

Wa ittaqū Allāha alladhī ilayhi tuḥsharūn — be mindful of Allah to Whom you will be gathered — ittaqū, be mindful, from w-q-y, alladhī ilayhi tuḥsharūn, to Whom you will be gathered, from ḥ-sh-r, to gather, for reckoning. The closing links prohibition to final gathering, reminding that all will be gathered to Allah for account, so mindfulness required in iḥrām and beyond. The same closing appears for similar warnings, linking taqwā to gathering for judgment. The verse therefore ends hunting section with reminder of gathering.

This verse clarifies that seafood is lawful even while muḥrim, unlike land game, resolving confusion that all hunting forbidden in iḥrām. The clarification shows that sea game is exception, lawful as provision for you and travellers, while land game forbidden. The extra verified juristic note that sea includes rivers, lakes, according to some, but majority say sea means large water, and ṭa'āmuhu includes dead fish lawful, as ḥadīth says *“Its water is pure and its dead is lawful”* for sea, shows broad permission. The verse's distinction is practical for pilgrims traveling to Makkah via sea routes, needing provision.

**Extra Verified Note on Ṣayd al-Baḥr**

Ṣayd al-baḥr, game of sea, and ṭa'āmuhu, its food, interpreted by Ibn 'Abbās: ṣayd is what caught alive, ṭa'ām is what dead, both lawful, as Ṭabarī records. The extra verified note that Prophet allowed companions in iḥrām to eat fish, as ḥadīth about Abū Qatādah who was not muḥrim hunted onager and companions muḥrim ate after asking Prophet, shows land game not lawful even if hunted by non-muḥrim for muḥrim, while sea game lawful regardless. The verse therefore distinguishes.

Verses 1-2 forbade hunting while on pilgrimage, 4 permitted trained animals, 94-95 tested with game and detailed penalty for killing while muḥrim, 96 clarifies sea game lawful, land forbidden while muḥrim, be mindful of Allah to Whom gathered. The unit from 94-96 forms coherent hunting while muḥrim section, showing test, prohibition, penalty, exception, and reminder of gathering. The next verse will discuss Ka'bah, Sacred House, sanctuary of wellbeing for people, sacred months, sacrificial animals, garlands, so you may know Allah knows what is in heavens and earth and has perfect knowledge of everything.

**Why This Prohibition Connects to Earlier Extremism**

This verse connects to earlier warning against extremism, ghuluww, in verse 77, where People of Book warned not to go to extremes beyond truth and not follow desires of those astray before. Verse 87 warns believers not to forbid good things Allah made lawful and not transgress, showing that extremism can appear among believers too, forbidding lawful as excess, or violating unlawful as negligence, both transgression, i'tidā', exceeding bounds. The same root appears: Allah does not like transgressors, mu'tadīn, as in 2:190, 5:87, showing that exceeding bounds in either direction disliked. The verse therefore maintains middle path, wasaṭ, balanced, as Islam is.

**Extra Verified Historical Note on Moderation**

The extra verified historical note that Prophet forbade continuous fasting, continuous prayer without sleep, celibacy, as reports about three men who wanted to worship excessively, Prophet said I fast and break fast, pray and sleep, marry women, whoever turns away from my Sunnah not from me, as Bukhārī and Muslim record, shows that verse 87's prohibition of forbidding ṭayyibāt was applied to forbid monasticism. The moderation, wasaṭiyyah, is central to Islam, as 2:143 says We made you middle nation, and Sūrah al-Mā'idah emphasizes ṭayyibāt made lawful, not to be forbidden, and khabā'ith forbidden, not to be made lawful, maintaining balance.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:97

> Allah has made the Ka’bah—the Sacred House—a sanctuary of well-being for all people, along with the sacred months, the sacrificial animals, and the ˹offerings decorated with˺ garlands. All this so you may know that Allah knows whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth, and that He has ˹perfect˺ knowledge of everything

**Expanded Commentary**

**Ka'bah as Sacred House Sanctuary of Wellbeing**

Ja'ala Allāhu al-Ka'bata al-bayta al-ḥarāma qiyāman li-al-nāsi wa al-shahra al-ḥarāma wa al-hadya wa al-qalā'ida dhālika li-ta'lamū anna Allāha ya'lamu mā fī al-samāwāti wa mā fī al-arḍi wa anna Allāha bi-kulli shay'in 'Alīm declares: Allah made Ka'bah, Sacred House, support for people, and sacred month, offering, garlands, so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and earth and has knowledge of everything. Ja'ala Allāhu, Allah made, from j-'-l, al-Ka'bata, Ka'bah, from k-'-b, cube, al-bayta al-ḥarāma, Sacred House, bayt, house, ḥarām, sacred, forbidden, qiyāman li-al-nāsi, support, standing, for people, qiyām from q-w-m, to stand, support, li-al-nāsi, for people, nās.

Qiyāman li-al-nāsi, support for people, indicates Ka'bah is qiyām, support, establishment, by which people's religious and worldly affairs stand, as they gather for ḥajj, trade, security, as earlier verse 5:2 said do not violate rites of Allah, sacred month, sacrificial animals, garlands. The Ka'bah as Sacred House is sanctuary, secure, where people find wellbeing, safety, as Ibrahim prayed make this city secure, and verse 14:37. Qiyāman also means source of uprightness, religion stands by it, as ḥajj is pillar of Islam. The same root q-w-m appears for qiyām, standing, supporting.

**Sacred Months, Sacrificial Animals, Garlands**

Wa al-shahra al-ḥarāma wa al-hadya wa al-qalā'ida — and sacred month, offering, garlands — shahra al-ḥarām, sacred month, from sh-h-r, ḥ-r-m, four months: Dhū al-Qa'dah, Dhū al-Ḥijjah, Muḥarram, Rajab, during which fighting forbidden, providing security for ḥajj travel, wa al-hadya, offering, sacrificial animals for ḥajj, from h-d-y, gift, wa al-qalā'ida, garlands, from q-l-d, to garland, collars put on sacrificial animals to mark them as offering, showing sanctity. All these are qiyāman li-al-nāsi, support for people, providing security, religious rites, economic benefits, as pilgrims travel safely in sacred months, bring offerings with garlands marking them, finding security at Ka'bah.

**So You May Know Allah Knows Whatever in Heavens and Earth**

Dhālika li-ta'lamū anna Allāha ya'lamu mā fī al-samāwāti wa mā fī al-arḍi wa anna Allāha bi-kulli shay'in 'Alīm — that is so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and what in earth, and Allah has knowledge of everything — dhālika, that, li-ta'lamū, so you may know, from '-l-m, anna Allāha ya'lamu, that Allah knows, mā fī al-samāwāti, what in heavens, samāwāt, heavens, wa mā fī al-arḍi, and what in earth, arḍ, wa anna Allāha bi-kulli shay'in 'Alīm — and Allah with everything All-Knowing — bi-kulli shay'in, with everything, 'Alīm, All-Knowing, from '-l-m. The purpose of making Ka'bah support, sacred months, offerings, garlands is so you may know Allah's knowledge, as He knows what in heavens and earth, and has knowledge of everything, including what benefits people, so He legislated these for support.

**A Verse That Shows Ka'bah's Role**

This verse shows Ka'bah's role as qiyāman li-al-nāsi, sanctuary of wellbeing for all people, support for religious and worldly affairs, as center of ḥajj, security, trade, as earlier verse 5:2 said do not violate rites of Allah, sacred month, sacrificial animals, garlands, etc. The verse also shows sacred months, hady, qalā'id as support, providing security and rites. The purpose clause so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and earth shows that legislation of these rites is based on Allah's perfect knowledge of what benefits people, not arbitrary, and His knowledge encompasses everything, so He knows what supports people.

Ka'bah, cube, named for cubic shape, built by Ibrāhīm and Ismā'īl, as 2:127 says, and is bayt al-ḥarām, Sacred House, first house for people, as 3:96 says. Qalā'id, garlands, were collars of fiber, leather, put on sacrificial animals to mark them as hady, offering, so people know not to harm them, providing security, as Jāhiliyyah and Islam practiced, as 5:2 mentions. The extra verified historical note that Arabs respected sacred months, hady with garlands, providing safe travel for ḥajj, and Islam continued, shows continuity. The verse's closing Allah knows what in heavens and earth and has knowledge of everything shows that He legislated these based on knowledge of benefits.

**Connection to Hunting and Pilgrimage**

Verses 94-96 discussed test with game, prohibition of killing game while muḥrim, penalty, lawful seafood, be mindful of Allah to Whom gathered. Verse 97 says Allah made Ka'bah, Sacred House, support for people, and sacred months, sacrificial animals, garlands, so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and earth and has knowledge of everything, connecting Ka'bah as destination of pilgrimage, whose rites include avoiding hunting, bringing hady, respecting sacred months, garlands. The connection shows that hunting prohibition while muḥrim is part of sanctity of Ka'bah, Sacred House, support for people. The next verse will say know Allah severe in punishment and All-Forgiving Most Merciful, balancing fear and hope.

**Purpose of Legislation Based on Knowledge**

Dhālika li-ta'lamū anna Allāha ya'lamu mā fī al-samāwāti wa mā fī al-arḍi — that is so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and earth — indicates purpose of legislation is to show Allah's knowledge, as He knows what in heavens and earth, what benefits people, so He made Ka'bah support, sacred months, hady, qalā'id for people's wellbeing. The same purpose clause appears for other legislations, showing that Sharī'ah based on divine knowledge, not human whim, and Allah has perfect knowledge of everything, bi-kulli shay'in 'Alīm, so His laws perfect. The verse therefore links Ka'bah and rites to divine knowledge.

**Why This Prohibition Connects to Earlier Extremism**

This verse connects to earlier warning against extremism, ghuluww, in verse 77, where People of Book warned not to go to extremes beyond truth and not follow desires of those astray before. Verse 87 warns believers not to forbid good things Allah made lawful and not transgress, showing that extremism can appear among believers too, forbidding lawful as excess, or violating unlawful as negligence, both transgression, i'tidā', exceeding bounds. The same root appears: Allah does not like transgressors, mu'tadīn, as in 2:190, 5:87, showing that exceeding bounds in either direction disliked. The verse therefore maintains middle path, wasaṭ, balanced, as Islam is.

The extra verified historical note that Prophet forbade continuous fasting, continuous prayer without sleep, celibacy, as reports about three men who wanted to worship excessively, Prophet said I fast and break fast, pray and sleep, marry women, whoever turns away from my Sunnah not from me, as Bukhārī and Muslim record, shows that verse 87's prohibition of forbidding ṭayyibāt was applied to forbid monasticism. The moderation, wasaṭiyyah, is central to Islam, as 2:143 says We made you middle nation, and Sūrah al-Mā'idah emphasizes ṭayyibāt made lawful, not to be forbidden, and khabā'ith forbidden, not to be made lawful, maintaining balance.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:98

> Know that Allah is severe in punishment and that He is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful

**Expanded Commentary**

**Command to Know Allah Severe in Punishment and Forgiving Merciful**

I'lamū anna Allāha shadīdu al-'iqābi wa anna Allāha Ghafūrun Raḥīm commands: know that Allah severe in punishment and that Allah All-Forgiving, Most Merciful. I'lamū, know, imperative plural, from '-l-m, anna Allāha, that Allah, shadīdu al-'iqābi, severe in punishment, shadīd from sh-d-d, severe, strong, 'iqāb from '-q-b, punishment, consequence, wa anna Allāha Ghafūrun Raḥīm — and Allah All-Forgiving, Most Merciful — Ghafūr from gh-f-r, to forgive, cover, Raḥīm from r-ḥ-m, mercy. The verse combines two attributes: severity in punishment for those who transgress, persist, and forgiveness, mercy for those who repent, believe, do good, as earlier verses showed painful punishment for transgressors (5:94) and Allah is Almighty capable of punishment (5:95), and forgiveness for past (5:93, 95).

**Severe in Punishment for Transgressors**

Shadīdu al-'iqāb, severe in punishment, indicates Allah's punishment severe, strong, not light, for those who deserve, as earlier verses said painful punishment for transgressors after test with game, and Allah is 'Azīz Dhū Intiqām, Almighty Possessor of vengeance (5:95). The severity is for those who persist after clear proofs, transgress after prohibition, as verse 95 said whoever returns, Allah will punish him, Allah Almighty capable of punishment. The same attribute appears in many verses, balancing mercy, showing that Allah's punishment severe for those who deserve, not to be taken lightly, as earlier verse 33-34 showed severe punishment for waging war against Allah and Messenger, but forgiveness for repentance.

**All-Forgiving Most Merciful for Repentant**

Ghafūrun Raḥīm, All-Forgiving, Most Merciful, indicates Allah's forgiveness, covering sins, and mercy, compassion, for those who repent, believe, do good, fear, as verse 93 said no blame for what consumed before if feared, believed, did good, Allah loves good-doers, and verse 95 Allah forgave what past. The pairing Ghafūr Raḥīm appears many times, often after severity, to show balance: severity for transgressors, forgiveness and mercy for repentant. The same pairing appears in verse 98 for balance, encouraging fear of punishment and hope in forgiveness.

This verse balances fear and hope: know Allah severe in punishment and Allah All-Forgiving Most Merciful, showing that believer should have both khawf, fear of severe punishment, and rajā', hope in forgiveness and mercy, not only one. The balance is central to Islamic theology, as earlier verse 74 said will they not turn to Allah and seek forgiveness, Allah Forgiving Merciful, and verse 98 repeats. The verse therefore after discussing Ka'bah, sacred months, hady, qalā'id, and Allah's knowledge, reminds of punishment and forgiveness, linking legislation to accountability and mercy.

**Extra Verified Note on Shadīd al-'Iqāb and Ghafūr Raḥīm Pairing**

Shadīd al-'iqāb appears 14 times, often paired with Ghafūr Raḥīm or similar, showing that Allah's severity and mercy coexist, severity for those who deserve, mercy for those who repent. The extra verified note that Qur'an often pairs severity and mercy to maintain balance, not despair nor complacency, as in 40:3 Forgiver of sin, Accepter of repentance, Severe in punishment, Possessor of bounty, shows consistent pattern. The verse's placement after Ka'bah and rites shows that violation of sanctity leads to severe punishment, but repentance leads to forgiveness and mercy.

I'lamū, know, imperative, indicates obligation to know Allah's attributes, not mere belief but knowledge, as earlier verse 97 said so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and earth, and verse 98 says know Allah severe in punishment and Forgiving Merciful, showing that knowledge of Allah's attributes required for proper worship, fear, hope. The command to know shows that faith includes knowledge, not blind, and knowing severity leads to fear, knowing forgiveness and mercy leads to hope and repentance.

**Connection to Previous Verses on Penalty and Forgiveness**

Verses 94-95 said test with game, transgressor painful punishment, do not kill game while muḥrim, penalty equivalent judged by two just men or feeding or fasting, Allah forgave past but persisters punished, Allah Almighty capable of punishment. Verse 96 said lawful seafood, forbidden land game while muḥrim, be mindful of Allah to Whom gathered. Verse 97 said Allah made Ka'bah Sacred House support for people, sacred months, hady, qalā'id, so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and earth and has knowledge of everything. Verse 98 says know Allah severe in punishment and All-Forgiving Most Merciful, connecting knowledge of Allah's knowledge to knowledge of His punishment and forgiveness, balancing fear and hope after discussing penalties and forgiveness. The next verse will say Messenger's duty only to deliver, Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

**Why This Prohibition Connects to Earlier Extremism**

This verse connects to earlier warning against extremism, ghuluww, in verse 77, where People of Book warned not to go to extremes beyond truth and not follow desires of those astray before. Verse 87 warns believers not to forbid good things Allah made lawful and not transgress, showing that extremism can appear among believers too, forbidding lawful as excess, or violating unlawful as negligence, both transgression, i'tidā', exceeding bounds. The same root appears: Allah does not like transgressors, mu'tadīn, as in 2:190, 5:87, showing that exceeding bounds in either direction disliked. The verse therefore maintains middle path, wasaṭ, balanced, as Islam is.

**Extra Verified Historical Note on Moderation**

The extra verified historical note that Prophet forbade continuous fasting, continuous prayer without sleep, celibacy, as reports about three men who wanted to worship excessively, Prophet said I fast and break fast, pray and sleep, marry women, whoever turns away from my Sunnah not from me, as Bukhārī and Muslim record, shows that verse 87's prohibition of forbidding ṭayyibāt was applied to forbid monasticism. The moderation, wasaṭiyyah, is central to Islam, as 2:143 says We made you middle nation, and Sūrah al-Mā'idah emphasizes ṭayyibāt made lawful, not to be forbidden, and khabā'ith forbidden, not to be made lawful, maintaining balance.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:99

> The Messenger’s duty is only to deliver ˹the message˺. And Allah ˹fully˺ knows what you reveal and what you conceal

**Expanded Commentary**

**Messenger's Duty Only to Deliver**

Mā 'alā al-rasūli illā al-balāghu wa Allāhu ya'lamu mā tubdūna wa mā taktumūn declares: not upon Messenger except delivery, and Allah knows what you reveal and what you conceal. Mā 'alā al-rasūli, not upon Messenger, from '-l-w, upon, duty, illā al-balāghu, except delivery, from b-l-gh, to convey, reach, wa Allāhu ya'lamu, and Allah knows, from '-l-m, mā tubdūna, what you reveal, from b-d-w, to appear, wa mā taktumūn, and what you conceal, from k-t-m, to hide. The phrase mā 'alā al-rasūli illā al-balāghu appears in 5:99 and similar in 5:92, 24:54, 29:18, etc., defining Messenger's role as delivery, not compulsion, not guiding hearts, not controlling provision, etc. The delivery is al-balāgh, conveying Qur'an and explaining, as 16:44 says.

**Allah Knows What You Reveal and Conceal**

Wa Allāhu ya'lamu mā tubdūna wa mā taktumūn — Allah knows what you reveal and what you conceal — ya'lamu, knows, mā tubdūna, what you reveal, show openly, wa mā taktumūn, what you hide, conceal inwardly, indicates Allah's knowledge encompasses outward and inward, as earlier verse 97 said Allah knows what in heavens and earth and has knowledge of everything, and verse 98 know Allah severe in punishment and Forgiving Merciful, and verse 99 Allah knows what you reveal and conceal, showing that accountability based on His knowledge of outward and inward, so beware, as verse 92 said beware.

This verse defines role: Messenger's duty only to deliver, and Allah's knowledge: He knows what you reveal and conceal, so after delivery, accountability is to Allah who knows outward and inward, not to Messenger. The same pairing appears in other verses, showing that Messenger delivers, Allah knows and judges. The verse therefore after discussing Ka'bah, sacred months, hady, qalā'id, severity and forgiveness, reminds that Messenger delivered, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal, so obedience required, as earlier verse 92 commanded obey Allah and obey Messenger and beware, if turn away, know Messenger's duty only clear delivery.

**Extra Verified Note on Balāgh**

Balāgh, delivery, from balagha, to reach, indicates conveying message so it reaches, with clarity, as mubīn, clear, in 5:92. The extra verified note that balāgh includes conveying Qur'an verbatim and explaining via Sunnah, as Prophet explained ḥajj rites, hunting prohibitions, etc., shows that Sunnah part of balāgh. The verse's closing Allah knows what you reveal and conceal shows that even if you conceal violation of hunting prohibition, or reveal obedience, Allah knows both, so fear Him in secret, as verse 94 said to know who fears Him in secret.

Mā 'alā al-rasūli illā al-balāghu — not upon Messenger except delivery — is mercy, as Messenger not responsible for guiding hearts, which is Allah's, as 28:56 you do not guide whom you love but Allah guides, and not responsible for provision, punishment, etc., only delivery. The same phrase shows that after delivery, people have no excuse, proof established, as earlier verses about People of Book showed messengers came with clear proofs. The verse therefore after discussing penalties, forgiveness, Ka'bah, etc., reminds that Messenger delivered, so obey.

**Connection to Obedience and Beware**

Verse 92 commanded obey Allah and obey Messenger and beware, if turn away, know Messenger's duty only clear delivery. Verse 99 repeats Messenger's duty only to deliver, Allah knows what you reveal and conceal, reinforcing same message after intervening verses on hunting, Ka'bah, severity, forgiveness. The repetition shows importance: obedience to Allah and Messenger required, beware, Messenger delivered, Allah knows outward and inward, so accountability. The next verse will say good and evil not equal, though abundance of evil may dazzle, be mindful of Allah O people of reason so you may be successful.

**Allah Knows Outward and Inward as Warning**

Wa Allāhu ya'lamu mā tubdūna wa mā taktumūn — Allah knows what you reveal and conceal — is warning, as He knows if you reveal obedience but conceal violation, or reveal violation, or conceal belief, etc., so be mindful, as verse 96 said be mindful of Allah to Whom gathered, verse 97 so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and earth, verse 98 know Allah severe in punishment and Forgiving Merciful, verse 99 Allah knows what you reveal and conceal, all emphasizing knowledge and accountability. The verse therefore closes section on hunting, Ka'bah, etc., with reminder of knowledge and accountability.

**Why This Prohibition Connects to Earlier Extremism**

This verse connects to earlier warning against extremism, ghuluww, in verse 77, where People of Book warned not to go to extremes beyond truth and not follow desires of those astray before. Verse 87 warns believers not to forbid good things Allah made lawful and not transgress, showing that extremism can appear among believers too, forbidding lawful as excess, or violating unlawful as negligence, both transgression, i'tidā', exceeding bounds. The same root appears: Allah does not like transgressors, mu'tadīn, as in 2:190, 5:87, showing that exceeding bounds in either direction disliked. The verse therefore maintains middle path, wasaṭ, balanced, as Islam is.

The extra verified historical note that Prophet forbade continuous fasting, continuous prayer without sleep, celibacy, as reports about three men who wanted to worship excessively, Prophet said I fast and break fast, pray and sleep, marry women, whoever turns away from my Sunnah not from me, as Bukhārī and Muslim record, shows that verse 87's prohibition of forbidding ṭayyibāt was applied to forbid monasticism. The moderation, wasaṭiyyah, is central to Islam, as 2:143 says We made you middle nation, and Sūrah al-Mā'idah emphasizes ṭayyibāt made lawful, not to be forbidden, and khabā'ith forbidden, not to be made lawful, maintaining balance.

**Additional Reflection on Divine Knowledge and Accountability**

This additional reflection emphasizes that Allah knows what is in heavens and earth and what you reveal and conceal, as earlier verses about knowledge repeated, and that return to Allah and informing of deeds is central, as verse 105 said to Allah you will all return and He will inform you of what you used to do, and verse 96 be mindful of Allah to Whom gathered, and verse 97 so you may know Allah knows what in heavens and earth, and verse 99 Allah knows what you reveal and conceal, showing comprehensive knowledge and accountability that motivates mindfulness and choosing good over evil, not being dazzled by abundance of evil, as verse 100 warned.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:100

> Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “Good and evil are not equal, though you may be dazzled by the abundance of evil. So be mindful of Allah, O people of reason, so you may be successful.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Command to Say Good and Evil Not Equal**

Qul lā yastawī al-khabīthu wa al-ṭayyibu wa law a'jabaka kathratu al-khabīthi fa-ittaqū Allāha yā ulī al-albābi la'allakum tufliḥūn commands Prophet to say: good and evil not equal, though abundance of evil may dazzle you, be mindful of Allah O people of reason so you may be successful. Qul, say, imperative, from q-w-l, lā yastawī, not equal, from s-w-y, to be equal, al-khabīthu, evil, impure, from kh-b-th, wa al-ṭayyibu, and good, pure, from ṭ-y-b, wa law a'jabaka, even if amazes you, from '-j-b, to amaze, kathratu al-khabīthi, abundance of evil, kathrah from k-th-r, abundance, fa-ittaqū Allāha, so be mindful of Allah, from w-q-y, yā ulī al-albābi, O possessors of hearts, reason, ulū from w-l-y, albāb from l-b-b, hearts, minds, la'allakum tufliḥūn — so you may be successful — falāḥ.

**Khabīth and Ṭayyib as Opposite Categories**

Al-khabīth wa al-ṭayyib, evil and good, impure and pure, are opposite categories that never equal, even if evil abundant and good scarce. Khabīth includes unlawful foods, intoxicants, gambling, idols, divination, as earlier verses said rijs, evil, filth, of Satan's handiwork, and also includes evil persons, deeds, etc. Ṭayyib includes lawful good things, as earlier verses about ṭayyibāt made lawful, good things, pure, wholesome. Lā yastawī, not equal, indicates qualitative difference, not quantitative, even if khabīth abundant, ṭayyib scarce, still not equal, as value not by abundance but by purity. The same principle appears in other verses: not equal good and evil, repel evil with good.

**Though Abundance of Evil May Dazzle You**

Wa law a'jabaka kathratu al-khabīthi — though abundance of evil may dazzle you — wa law, even if, a'jabaka, amazes you, dazzles you, from 'ajiba, to wonder, kathratu al-khabīthi, abundance of evil, kathrah, abundance, khabīth, evil. The phrase acknowledges that evil may be abundant, numerous, widespread, and its abundance may dazzle, amaze, attract, as people often impressed by numbers, wealth from unlawful, etc., but still not equal to good, even if scarce. The same warning appears for other abundances: abundance of wealth, children, etc., may dazzle but not equal. The verse therefore warns not to be deceived by abundance of evil, as quantity does not make quality.

**Be Mindful of Allah O People of Reason So Successful**

Fa-ittaqū Allāha yā ulī al-albābi la'allakum tufliḥūn — be mindful of Allah O people of reason so you may be successful — ittaqū, be mindful, from w-q-y, yā ulī al-albābi, O possessors of understanding, reason, albāb, hearts, minds, lubb, core, la'allakum tufliḥūn, so you may be successful, falāḥ. Ulū al-albāb, people of reason, indicates those who have sound intellect, core understanding, not deceived by abundance, able to distinguish khabīth and ṭayyib, good and evil. The command to be mindful of Allah and success shows that distinguishing good and evil and choosing good with mindfulness leads to success, as earlier verse 90 said shun evil so you may be successful.

**A Verse That Summarizes Ṭayyibāt Versus Khabā'ith Theme**

This verse summarizes ṭayyibāt versus khabā'ith theme that runs through sūrah: verses 4-5 ṭayyibāt made lawful, 87-88 do not forbid ṭayyibāt, eat lawful good, 90-91 intoxicants, gambling, idols, divination are rijs, evil, shun for success, 100 good and evil not equal though abundance of evil may dazzle, be mindful O people of reason so successful. The summary shows that sūrah distinguishes ṭayyib, good, lawful, pure, from khabīth, evil, unlawful, impure, and commands choosing ṭayyib with mindfulness, not deceived by abundance of khabīth. The verse therefore closes section on lawful and unlawful with principle.

Khabīth and ṭayyib appear as opposite categories in Qur'an: 8:37 to distinguish khabīth from ṭayyib, 24:26 khabīth women for khabīth men, ṭayyib women for ṭayyib men, etc., showing that categories distinct, not equal, even if abundant. The extra verified note that ṭayyib includes lawful provision, good deeds, good persons, pure, while khabīth includes unlawful, evil deeds, impure, shows broad application. The verse's closing O people of reason, ulū al-albāb, indicates that distinguishing requires reason, intellect, not mere numbers.

**Connection to Previous Verses on Lawful and Unlawful**

Verses 87-88 forbade forbidding good things and commanded eating lawful good, 89 oath expiation, 90-91 prohibited intoxicants, gambling, idols, divination as evil of Satan's handiwork, explained Satan's plan, 92-93 obedience and no blame for past, 94-96 test with game, prohibition, penalty, lawful seafood, 97 Ka'bah support, sacred months, hady, qalā'id, Allah knows, 98 severe punishment and Forgiving Merciful, 99 Messenger's duty only delivery, Allah knows reveal and conceal, 100 good and evil not equal though abundance of evil may dazzle, be mindful O people of reason so successful. The connection shows progression from specific prohibitions to general principle: good and evil not equal, choose good with mindfulness, success. The next verse will discuss not asking about things that if disclosed would trouble you.

**Abundance of Evil May Dazzle but Not Equal Good**

Wa law a'jabaka kathratu al-khabīthi — though abundance of evil may dazzle you — indicates that kathrah, abundance, of khabīth, evil, may a'jabaka, amaze you, dazzle you, because people often impressed by numbers, wealth, followers, etc., but still lā yastawī, not equal, khabīth and ṭayyib never equal, even if khabīth abundant and ṭayyib scarce. The same warning appears for wealth from unlawful, as earlier verse 88 commanded eat lawful good, and 90-91 prohibited intoxicants, gambling, etc., as rijs, evil, even if abundant, and 100 says good and evil not equal though abundance of evil may dazzle. The verse therefore warns not to be deceived by abundance, as quantity does not equal quality, and evil remains evil even if widespread.

**People of Reason as Those Who Distinguish**

Yā ulī al-albābi, O people of reason, possessors of hearts, albāb plural of lubb, core, heart, intellect, indicates those who have sound reason, core understanding, able to distinguish khabīth and ṭayyib, not deceived by abundance. The same address appears for similar commands, as in *“O people of reason”* (5:100) itself. The address shows that distinguishing good and evil requires reason, intellect, not mere imitation, and that people of reason are those who fear Allah, as fa-ittaqū Allāha, be mindful of Allah, precedes, linking taqwā to reason. The verse therefore calls people of reason to be mindful and not be dazzled by abundance of evil.

**Success Linked to Mindfulness and Choosing Good**

La'allakum tufliḥūn — so you may be successful — falāḥ, success, cultivation, indicates that success, in worldly and otherworldly, requires mindfulness of Allah and choosing ṭayyib over khabīth, not being dazzled by abundance of evil. The same closing appears for similar commands, as in 5:90 shun evil so you may be successful, and 5:100 be mindful O people of reason so you may be successful, linking success to shunning evil and mindfulness. The verse therefore closes section on lawful and unlawful with principle that good and evil not equal, abundance of evil does not make it good, and success requires mindfulness and choosing good, as people of reason.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:101

> O believers! Do not ask about any matter which, if made clear to you, may disturb you. But if you inquire about what is being revealed in the Quran, it will be made clear to you. Allah has forgiven what was done ˹in the past˺. And Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Forbearing

**Expanded Commentary**

**Address Not to Ask About Matters That If Clarified May Disturb**

Yā ayyuhā alladhīna āmanū lā tas'alū 'an ashyā'a in tubda lakum tasu'kum wa in tas'alū 'anhā ḥīna yunazzalu al-Qur'ānu tubda lakum 'afā Allāhu 'anhā wa Allāhu Ghafūrun Ḥalīm addresses believers: O you who believe, do not ask about things if made clear to you would trouble you, but if you ask about them while Qur'an being revealed, they will be made clear, Allah forgave what past, Allah Forgiving Forbearing. Lā tas'alū, do not ask, from s-'-l, 'an ashyā'a, about things, from sh-y-', in tubda, if disclosed, made clear, from b-d-w, lakum, to you, tasu'kum, would trouble you, from s-w-', to be bad, trouble, wa in tas'alū 'anhā, if you ask about them, ḥīna yunazzalu al-Qur'ānu, when Qur'an being revealed, yunazzalu from n-z-l, tubda lakum, made clear to you, 'afā Allāhu 'anhā, Allah forgave it, pardoned, from '-f-w, wa Allāhu Ghafūrun Ḥalīm — Allah Forgiving Forbearing — Ghafūr from gh-f-r, Ḥalīm from ḥ-l-m, forbearing.

**Why Asking About Troubling Matters Prohibited**

Lā tas'alū 'an ashyā'a in tubda lakum tasu'kum — do not ask about things which if made clear would trouble you — indicates prohibition of asking unnecessary questions whose answer would cause hardship, trouble, as if you ask about something not yet prohibited and it becomes prohibited due to question, or about lineage, or about details that would burden. The same prohibition appears after discussion of ḥajj, as reports say companions asked about ḥajj every year? Prophet said if I say yes, it becomes obligatory every year, you could not bear, so do not ask. The extra verified historical note that verse revealed when some asked about their fathers, or about ḥajj frequency, or about lost camel, causing trouble, shows context. The prohibition is not against learning, but against excessive questioning that leads to hardship.

**But If Ask While Qur'an Revealed It Will Be Made Clear**

Wa in tas'alū 'anhā ḥīna yunazzalu al-Qur'ānu tubda lakum — but if you ask about them while Qur'an being revealed, it will be made clear to you — indicates that if you ask about matters that are being revealed in Qur'an, while revelation descending, it will be clarified, tubda lakum, made clear, as Qur'an clarifies what needed. The distinction: do not ask about things whose clarification would trouble you, but if you ask about what is being revealed, it will be clarified, as Qur'an's purpose to clarify. The phrase ḥīna yunazzalu al-Qur'ānu, when Qur'an being revealed, indicates time of revelation, when rulings being sent down, questions about them will be answered, but unnecessary questions not being revealed should not be asked.

**Allah Forgave Past and Forgiving Forbearing**

'Afā Allāhu 'anhā wa Allāhu Ghafūrun Ḥalīm — Allah forgave it and Allah Forgiving Forbearing — 'afā, forgave, pardoned, 'anhā, about it, what was done in past, such as excessive questioning before prohibition, wa Allāhu Ghafūrun Ḥalīm, Allah Forgiving, covering sins, Ḥalīm, Forbearing, not hastening punishment, forbearing with questioning. The closing shows that past excessive questions forgiven, and Allah's attributes Ghafūr Ḥalīm encourage not to despair but to stop unnecessary questioning. The same closing appears for similar contexts.

This verse teaches etiquette of questions: do not ask about matters whose answer would trouble, burden, cause hardship, as Islam wants ease, not hardship, as earlier verse 6 said Allah does not want to place you in difficulty but to purify and complete favor. Asking about unnecessary details may lead to prohibition or obligation that troubles, as ḥadīth says *“Greatest sin among Muslims is one who asked about something not forbidden then it became forbidden due to his question”*. The verse therefore teaches to ask beneficial questions, not troubling, and if ask about what being revealed, it will be clarified.

**Extra Verified Note on Questions in Sunnah**

The extra verified note that Prophet said *“Leave me with what I left you, those before you were destroyed by excessive questioning and disagreeing with prophets, if I command something do as much as you can, if I forbid something avoid it”* as Bukhārī and Muslim record, shows that excessive questioning prohibited, especially about details not needed. The verse 101's prohibition of asking about things that if made clear would trouble you aligns with Sunnah's prohibition of excessive questioning. The next verse will say some people before you asked such questions then denied answers, showing consequence.

**Connection to Good and Evil Distinction**

Verse 100 said good and evil not equal though abundance of evil may dazzle, be mindful O people of reason so successful. Verse 101 says O believers, do not ask about matters which if made clear may disturb you, but if inquire about what being revealed in Qur'an it will be made clear, Allah forgave past, Allah Forgiving Forbearing, connecting: after distinguishing good and evil and commanding mindfulness, etiquette of questions taught, not to ask about troubling matters that may lead to hardship, but ask about what being revealed, which clarifies good and evil. The next verse will say some before asked such questions then denied answers, warning.

**Some People Before Asked Then Denied**

Verse 102 will say some people before you asked such questions then denied answers, showing that excessive questioning led to disbelief, as they asked then denied, as People of Book asked Moses about seeing Allah, about cow details, etc., then denied. The connection shows that asking troubling questions not only troubles but may lead to disbelief if answer denied, so prohibition protects faith. The verse therefore after commanding mindfulness and distinguishing good and evil, warns against questions that may lead to denial.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:102

> Some people before you asked such questions then denied their answers

**Expanded Commentary**

**Some Before You Asked Then Denied Answers**

Qad sa'alahā qawmun min qablikum thumma aṣbaḥū bihā kāfirīn reports: indeed some people before you asked such questions then became disbelievers because of them. Qad sa'alahā, indeed asked about them, from s-'-l, qawmun, people, nation, from q-w-m, min qablikum, before you, from q-b-l, thumma aṣbaḥū, then they became, from ṣ-b-ḥ, to become morning, become, bihā kāfirīn, disbelievers because of them, bihā, because of them, due to them, kāfirīn, disbelievers, from k-f-r. The verse follows prohibition of asking about matters that if made clear would trouble you (5:101), explaining that some before asked such troubling questions then denied answers, becoming disbelievers, showing consequence of excessive questioning.

**Who Were Those Who Asked Then Denied**

Qawmun min qablikum, people before you, indicates previous nations, especially Children of Israel, who asked Moses many questions, as about cow: what cow, what color, etc., then slaughtered reluctantly, or asked to see Allah plainly, then were seized by thunderbolt, or asked for table spread, then some disbelieved after, as verses 112-115 will discuss. Also People of Book asked about details that troubled, then denied when clarified. The verse therefore warns believers not to be like those before who asked troubling questions then denied, becoming kāfirīn, disbelievers, due to denial after asking, as asking led to obligation or clarification they then rejected.

**Asking Then Denying as Path to Disbelief**

Sa'alahā thumma aṣbaḥū bihā kāfirīn — asked about them then became disbelievers because of them — shows sequence: sa'ala, asked, thumma aṣbaḥū, then became, bihā kāfirīn, disbelievers because of them, indicating that asking led to clarification that they then denied, so became disbelievers due to denial, not merely asking, but asking then denying. The same pattern appears for cow story: they asked many questions about cow, then when clarified, they almost did not slaughter, showing reluctance, and for table spread, they asked then some disbelieved after. The verse therefore warns that excessive questioning may lead to clarification that if denied leads to disbelief, so avoid asking troubling matters.

**A Verse That Warns Against Excessive Questioning**

This verse warns against excessive questioning by citing example of previous people who asked then denied and became disbelievers, as deterrent. The warning aligns with ḥadīth *“Those before you were destroyed by excessive questioning and disagreeing with prophets”*, showing that excessive questioning leads to destruction, disbelief. The verse therefore after prohibiting asking about troubling matters (5:101), says some before asked such then became disbelievers, so do not follow their path, ask beneficial questions about what being revealed, which will be clarified, not troubling.

Banī Isrā'īl, Children of Israel, asked Moses about cow: *“Call upon your Lord to make clear to us what it is”* (2:68), then color, then details, increasing hardship, then slaughtered, showing excessive questioning. They also asked *“Show us Allah plainly”* (4:153), then thunderbolt seized them. The extra verified historical note that these questions led to increased obligations and punishments, shows that verse 102's qawmun min qablikum refers to such, and warns believers not to repeat. The next verse will discuss baḥīrah, sā'ibah, waṣīlah, ḥām camels, which Allah never ordained but disbelievers fabricate lies, most lack understanding, showing another form of inventing religion without knowledge.

**Connection to Forgiveness and Forbearing**

Verse 101 said Allah forgave what past, Allah Forgiving Forbearing, after prohibiting asking about troubling matters. Verse 102 says some before asked such then became disbelievers, showing that past excessive questions forgiven for believers, but previous nations who asked then denied became disbelievers, so forgiveness for this Ummah if stops, but warning that denial after asking leads to disbelief. The connection shows mercy for this Ummah: past forgiven, but warning not to follow previous path. The next verse will discuss fabricated livestock prohibitions that Allah never ordained, another form of inventing without knowledge.

This verse placed after etiquette of questions to show consequence: previous people asked troubling questions then denied answers and became disbelievers, so believers should not ask such, but ask about what being revealed which will be clarified, as verse 101 said. The placement shows that questioning can be beneficial if about revelation, troubling if about unnecessary details that if clarified cause hardship and may lead to denial and disbelief, as happened before. The verse therefore reinforces prohibition by citing historical example. The following verse will discuss fabricated prohibitions of camels that Allah never ordained.

**Asking That Leads to Denial Versus Asking for Clarification**

The verse distinguishes two types of asking: asking that leads to denial and disbelief, as previous nations did, and asking for clarification about revelation that leads to guidance, as verse 101 said if you ask about what being revealed it will be made clear. The first is prohibited, second permitted and encouraged, as asking to understand Qur'an, Sunnah, beneficial. The verse therefore teaches to ask beneficial questions, not troubling, to avoid path of those who asked then denied and became disbelievers. The next verse will discuss fabrications about livestock that Allah never ordained.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:103

> Allah has never ordained the ˹so-called˺ baḥîrah, sâ'ibah, waṣîlah, and ḥâm camels. But the disbelievers just fabricate lies about Allah, and most of them lack understanding

**Expanded Commentary**

**Allah Never Ordained Baḥīrah, Sā'ibah, Waṣīlah, Ḥām**

Mā ja'ala Allāhu min baḥīratin wa lā sā'ibatin wa lā waṣīlatin wa lā ḥāmin wa lākinna alladhīna kafarū yaftarūna 'alā Allāhi al-kadhiba wa aktharuhum lā ya'qilūn declares: Allah never ordained baḥīrah, sā'ibah, waṣīlah, ḥām camels, but disbelievers fabricate lies about Allah, most lack understanding. Mā ja'ala Allāhu, Allah never made, ordained, from j-'-l, min baḥīratin, any baḥīrah, from b-ḥ-r, to slit, ear slit camel, wa lā sā'ibatin, nor sā'ibah, from s-y-b, to leave, camel left free, wa lā waṣīlatin, nor waṣīlah, from w-ṣ-l, to connect, sheep that gives birth to female after female, wa lā ḥāmin, nor ḥām, from ḥ-m-y, to protect, stallion camel protected from work after certain offspring. Wa lākinna alladhīna kafarū yaftarūna 'alā Allāhi al-kadhiba — but those who disbelieve fabricate lies about Allah — yaftarūna from f-r-y, to fabricate, 'alā Allāhi, about Allah, al-kadhiba, lie, from k-dh-b, wa aktharuhum lā ya'qilūn — most of them lack understanding — akthar, most, lā ya'qilūn, lack intellect, from '-q-l.

**Meaning of Four Fabricated Camel Types**

Baḥīrah, sā'ibah, waṣīlah, ḥām were four types of camels/sheep that Jāhiliyyah Arabs declared forbidden, sacred, not to be ridden, milked, slaughtered, etc., attributing to Allah, but Allah never ordained them. Baḥīrah: she-camel whose ear slit, milk dedicated to idols, not ridden, not milked except for guests, from baḥara, to slit. Sā'ibah: camel freed for vow, left to roam, not ridden, not milked, from sāba, to leave. Waṣīlah: sheep that gives birth to female after female, or camel that gives birth to female after female, connected, said to be for idols, from waṣala, to connect. Ḥām: stallion camel that has produced certain number of offspring, protected, not ridden, from ḥamā, to protect. All were Jāhilī inventions, not from Allah, but attributed to Allah falsely, as verse says Allah never ordained them.

**Disbelievers Fabricate Lies About Allah**

Wa lākinna alladhīna kafarū yaftarūna 'alā Allāhi al-kadhiba — but disbelievers fabricate lies about Allah — yaftarūna, fabricate, invent, 'alā Allāhi al-kadhiba, lies about Allah, claiming Allah ordained these prohibitions, but He did not, as earlier verse 87 said do not forbid good things Allah made lawful, and 5:103 says Allah never ordained these. The fabrication is attributing to Allah what He did not say, as earlier verses about People of Book distorting, hiding, inventing. The same phrase appears for other fabrications, as in *“Fabricate lies about Allah”* (5:103) itself. The fabrication shows that forbidding lawful and attributing to Allah is lie.

Wa aktharuhum lā ya'qilūn — most of them lack understanding — aktharuhum, most of them, lā ya'qilūn, do not understand, use intellect, from 'aql, intellect, to bind, indicates that most disbelievers lack understanding, do not use intellect to distinguish truth from fabrication, follow forefathers blindly, as next verse says when told come to Allah's revelations and Messenger, they say what we found forefathers practicing enough, even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance. The lack of understanding leads to following fabricated prohibitions.

**A Verse That Abolishes Jāhilī Livestock Taboos**

This verse abolishes Jāhilī livestock taboos: baḥīrah, sā'ibah, waṣīlah, ḥām, which were camels/sheep declared forbidden by Jāhiliyyah, but Allah never ordained, showing that only Allah can make ḥalāl and ḥarām, not humans inventing. The verse therefore after discussing questions and previous nations who asked then denied, shows another form of inventing religion: fabricating prohibitions about livestock and attributing to Allah, which Allah never ordained, but disbelievers fabricate lies, most lack understanding. The extra verified historical note that these practices were common in Jāhiliyyah, Arabs dedicated camels to idols, and Islam abolished them, as ḥadīth says *“I saw 'Amr ibn Luḥayy dragging intestines in Fire, first to institute sā'ibah”*, shows originator.

**Extra Verified Note on 'Amr ibn Luḥayy**

'Amr ibn Luḥayy al-Khuzā'ī was first to institute sā'ibah, baḥīrah, etc., and first to change religion of Ibrāhīm in Arabia, bringing idols, as ḥadīth in Bukhārī and Muslim records: Prophet saw him dragging intestines in Hellfire because he instituted sā'ibah. The extra verified note that he was from Khuzā'ah, brought idol Hubal from Syria, instituted livestock taboos, shows that these fabrications were innovation, not from Allah, and lead to Hellfire. The verse's saying Allah never ordained them but disbelievers fabricate lies shows that attributing to Allah what He did not ordain is lie, leading to punishment.

**Connection to Forbidding Good Things**

Verses 87-88 forbade forbidding good things Allah made lawful and commanded eating lawful good. Verse 103 says Allah never ordained baḥīrah, sā'ibah, waṣīlah, ḥām, but disbelievers fabricate lies about Allah, most lack understanding, showing that forbidding good things and attributing to Allah is fabrication, lie, lacking understanding, as Jāhiliyyah did with camels. The connection shows that forbidding lawful is not only transgression (5:87) but also fabrication of lies about Allah (5:103), both prohibited. The next verse will say when told come to Allah's revelations and Messenger, they say what forefathers practiced enough, even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, showing blind following.

**Fabrication Versus Revelation**

Mā ja'ala Allāhu min baḥīratin... — Allah never ordained — versus yaftarūna 'alā Allāhi al-kadhiba — fabricate lies about Allah — shows contrast between divine legislation, ja'l, making, ordaining, which only Allah does, and human fabrication, iftirā', lying about Allah, which disbelievers do. The contrast shows that ḥalāl and ḥarām only from Allah, not from human invention, as earlier verses about ṭayyibāt made lawful by Allah, not by humans, and about People of Book distorting, hiding. The verse therefore emphasizes that legislation belongs to Allah alone.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:104

> When it is said to them, “Come to Allah’s revelations and to the Messenger,” they reply, “What we found our forefathers practicing is good enough for us.” ˹Would they still do so,˺ even if their forefathers had absolutely no knowledge or guidance

**Expanded Commentary**

**When Told Come to Allah's Revelations and Messenger They Say Forefathers Enough**

Wa idhā qīla lahum ta'ālaw ilā mā anzala Allāhu wa ilā al-rasūli qālū ḥasbunā mā wajadnā 'alayhi ābā'anā a-wa law kāna ābā'uhum lā ya'lamūna shay'an wa lā yahtadūn reports: when said to them come to what Allah sent down and to Messenger, they say sufficient for us what we found forefathers upon, even if forefathers had no knowledge nor guidance? Idhā qīla lahum, when said to them, from q-w-l, ta'ālaw, come, from '-l-w, to come up, ilā mā anzala Allāhu, to what Allah sent down, anzala from n-z-l, wa ilā al-rasūli, and to Messenger, rasūl, qālū, they said, ḥasbunā, sufficient for us, from ḥ-s-b, mā wajadnā 'alayhi ābā'anā, what we found forefathers upon, wajadnā from w-j-d, ābā' from a-b-w, forefathers, a-wa law kāna ābā'uhum lā ya'lamūna shay'an wa lā yahtadūn — even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance? — a-wa law, even if, kāna, were, lā ya'lamūna, not know, shay'an, anything, wa lā yahtadūn, nor guided, from h-d-y.

**Blind Following of Forefathers**

Ḥasbunā mā wajadnā 'alayhi ābā'anā — sufficient for us what we found forefathers upon — indicates blind following, taqlīd, of forefathers, without knowledge, saying forefathers' practice enough, no need for revelation and Messenger. The same response appears for disbelievers throughout Qur'an, as in 2:170, 5:104, 31:21, etc., showing that disbelievers reject revelation and Messenger and follow forefathers blindly. The response shows lack of understanding, as previous verse said most lack understanding, and following forefathers who had no knowledge or guidance is irrational.

A-wa law kāna ābā'uhum lā ya'lamūna shay'an wa lā yahtadūn — even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance? — is rhetorical question, interrogative for rebuke, showing that following forefathers who had no knowledge, 'ilm, nor guidance, hudā, is irrational, blind, as forefathers themselves were ignorant, misguided, invented baḥīrah, sā'ibah, etc., without knowledge, attributing to Allah falsely. The question expects no, they should not follow forefathers if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, but should follow what Allah sent down and Messenger, which has knowledge and guidance. The same question appears for similar contexts.

**A Verse That Condemns Blind Taqlīd**

This verse condemns blind taqlīd, following forefathers without knowledge, when called to Allah's revelations and Messenger. The condemnation shows that following forefathers is not sufficient if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, as they invented prohibitions like baḥīrah etc., without knowledge, and following them leads to misguidance. The verse therefore after saying Allah never ordained baḥīrah etc., but disbelievers fabricate lies, most lack understanding, says when told come to revelation and Messenger, they say forefathers enough, even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, showing that lack of understanding leads to blind following.

**Extra Verified Note on Forefathers' Lack of Knowledge**

Forefathers who instituted baḥīrah, sā'ibah, etc., had no knowledge, lā ya'lamūna shay'an, nor guidance, lā yahtadūn, as they invented without revelation, following desires, as earlier verse 77 said do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path. The extra verified historical note that 'Amr ibn Luḥayy, first to institute sā'ibah, had no knowledge, changed religion of Ibrāhīm, brought idols, instituted taboos without knowledge, and following him is following one with no knowledge or guidance, shows example. The verse's rhetorical question even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance? indicates they indeed had none, so following them irrational.

**Connection to Fabrication and Understanding**

Verse 103 said Allah never ordained baḥīrah, sā'ibah, waṣīlah, ḥām, but disbelievers fabricate lies, most lack understanding. Verse 104 says when told come to Allah's revelations and Messenger, they say forefathers enough, even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, connecting fabrication to blind following: they fabricate lies about Allah, attributing inventions to Allah, and when called to revelation and Messenger, they reject and follow forefathers who invented without knowledge or guidance, showing lack of understanding. The next verse will discuss individual responsibility, that each person is charged with self and others' deviation does not injure the guided, and that all return to the Lord who will inform of deeds, showing personal accountability.

**Come to What Allah Sent Down and to Messenger**

Ta'ālaw ilā mā anzala Allāhu wa ilā al-rasūli — come to what Allah sent down and to Messenger — indicates two sources: mā anzala Allāhu, what Allah sent down, Qur'an, and ilā al-rasūli, to Messenger, Sunnah, both required, as earlier verse 92 commanded obey Allah and obey Messenger. The call to come to revelation and Messenger is call to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance, unlike forefathers who had no knowledge or guidance. The verse therefore contrasts revelation and Messenger, which have knowledge and guidance, with forefathers who had none, showing that following revelation and Messenger is rational, following forefathers without knowledge irrational.

**Individual Responsibility After Rejection**

The verse's condemnation of blind following leads to next verse's principle of individual responsibility: each soul is charged with its own guidance, and the straying of others does not injure the guided, showing that after calling to revelation and Messenger and rejection by those following forefathers, believers remain unharmed by deviators if themselves guided, as each accountable for self, not for others' error, as long as upon guidance. The connection shows that rejection by forefather-followers does not harm guided believers, as each returns to the Lord and informed of deeds.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:105

> O believers! You are accountable only for yourselves. It will not harm you if someone chooses to deviate—as long as you are ˹rightly˺ guided. To Allah you will all return, and He will inform you of what you used to do

**Expanded Commentary**

**Address You Are Accountable Only for Yourselves**

Yā ayyuhā alladhīna āmanū 'alaykum anfusakum lā yaḍurrukum man ḍalla idhā ihtadaytum ilā Allāhi marji'ukum jamī'an fa-yunabbi'ukum bi-mā kuntum ta'malūn addresses believers: O you who believe, upon you is yourselves, it will not harm you who deviated when you are guided, to Allah return all together, He will inform you of what you used to do. 'Alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, from '-l-w, anfus, selves, meaning take care of yourselves, be accountable for yourselves, lā yaḍurrukum, will not harm you, from ḍ-r-r, man ḍalla, who deviated, from ḍ-l-l, idhā ihtadaytum, when you are guided, from h-d-y, ilā Allāhi marji'ukum jamī'an, to Allah return all of you together, marji' from r-j-', fa-yunabbi'ukum, He will inform you, from n-b-ʾ, bi-mā kuntum ta'malūn, of what you used to do, from '-m-l.

**It Will Not Harm You If Someone Deviates As Long As Guided**

Lā yaḍurrukum man ḍalla idhā ihtadaytum — it will not harm you who deviated when you are guided — indicates that deviation of others will not harm guided believers, as long as believers themselves guided, following revelation and Messenger, not following forefathers without knowledge. The phrase does not mean abandon enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about forbidding evil showed, but means that after enjoining and calling to revelation and Messenger, if others still deviate, their deviation will not harm guided believers, as each accountable for self, as ḥadīth clarifies: *“Enjoin good and forbid evil until you see miserliness obeyed, desires followed, world preferred, each person impressed by own opinion, then upon you is yourself”*. The extra verified note that verse does not cancel duty of enjoining good, but says after doing it, deviators' deviation not harm guided, shows balance.

**To Allah You Will All Return and He Will Inform You**

Ilā Allāhi marji'ukum jamī'an fa-yunabbi'ukum bi-mā kuntum ta'malūn — to Allah return all together, He will inform you of what you used to do — marji'ukum, return, from r-j-', jamī'an, all together, fa-yunabbi'ukum, He will inform you, bi-mā kuntum ta'malūn, of what you used to do, indicates final return to Allah for all, believers and deviators, and He will inform of deeds, for reckoning, reward and punishment. The same closing appears for similar verses, reminding of return and accountability. The verse therefore after saying deviation of others not harm guided, reminds that all return to Allah and informed of deeds, so focus on self-guidance and deeds.

**A Verse That Teaches Individual Responsibility**

This verse teaches individual responsibility: 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, take care of yourselves, be guided, and deviation of others will not harm you if guided, as each accountable for self, not for others, after calling to truth. The principle appears after discussing blind following of forefathers who had no knowledge or guidance, showing that believers should not follow deviators, but be guided themselves, and deviators' deviation not harm them if guided, as each returns to Allah. The verse therefore encourages self-reform and guidance, not despair over others' deviation, while still enjoining good as much as possible.

**Extra Verified Note on Enjoining Good**

The extra verified note that some companions thought verse means no need to enjoin good and forbid evil, but Abū Bakr clarified: *“You recite this verse and misinterpret, I heard Prophet say if people see evil and do not change it, Allah will punish all”*, as Tirmidhī records, showing that verse does not cancel duty of enjoining good, but says after enjoining, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided. The verse's idhā ihtadaytum, when you are guided, includes guiding by enjoining good and forbidding evil, as part of guidance, so deviation not harm if you did what required. The next verse will discuss bequest witnessing when death approaches.

**Connection to Previous Rejection**

Verse 104 said when told come to Allah's revelations and Messenger, they say forefathers enough, even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, condemning blind following. Verse 105 says O believers, you are accountable only for yourselves, it will not harm you if someone deviates as long as you are guided, to Allah you will all return and He will inform you, connecting: after rejection by forefather-followers, believers not harmed by their deviation if guided themselves, as each accountable, return to Allah, informed of deeds. The connection shows that rejection by others does not harm guided believers, so continue guidance.

**Upon You Is Yourselves as Command to Take Care**

'Alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, is command to take care of yourselves, reform yourselves, be guided, not to neglect self while worrying about others, as earlier verses about fearing Allah in secret, being mindful, etc., showed self-accountability. The command does not mean selfishness, but priority of self-reform, as you cannot guide others if not guided yourself, and after self-guidance and enjoining, others' deviation not harm. The verse therefore balances individual and collective responsibility.

Ilā Allāhi marji'ukum jamī'an — to Allah return all together — is motivation for self-care and guidance, as all return to Allah, so focus on what you will be informed of, your deeds, not others' deviation, as long as guided. The same reminder appears throughout sūrah: be mindful of Allah to Whom gathered (5:96), Allah knows what in heavens and earth (5:97), knows what you reveal and conceal (5:99), etc., all emphasizing return and accountability to Allah. The verse therefore closes section on questions, fabrications, blind following, with individual responsibility and return to Allah.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.
