## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:106

> O believers! When death approaches any of you, call upon two just Muslim men to witness as you make a bequest; otherwise, two non-Muslims if you are afflicted with death while on a journey. If you doubt ˹their testimony˺, keep them after prayer and let them testify under oath ˹saying˺, “By Allah! We would never sell our testimony for any price, even in favour of a close relative, nor withhold the testimony of Allah. Otherwise, we would surely be sinful.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Address About Bequest Witnessing When Death Approaches**

Yā ayyuhā alladhīna āmanū shahādatu baynikum idhā ḥaḍara aḥadakum al-mawtu ḥīna al-waṣiyyati ithnāni dhawā 'adlin minkum aw ākharāni min ghayrikum in antum ḍarabtum fī al-arḍi fa-aṣābatkum muṣībatu al-mawti taḥbisūnahumā min ba'di al-ṣalāti fa-yuqsimāni bi-Allāhi ini irtabtum lā nashtarī bihi thamanan wa law kāna dhā qurbā wa lā naktumu shahādata Allāhi innā idhan la-mina al-āthimīn details bequest witnessing. Shahādatu baynikum, testimony between you, from sh-h-d, idhā ḥaḍara aḥadakum al-mawtu, when death approaches one of you, ḥaḍara from ḥ-ḍ-r, al-mawt, death, ḥīna al-waṣiyyati, at time of bequest, waṣiyyah from w-ṣ-y, ithnāni dhawā 'adlin minkum, two possessors of justice from you, 'adl, justice, aw ākharāni min ghayrikum, or two others from other than you, non-Muslims, in antum ḍarabtum fī al-arḍi, if you travel in land, ḍarabtum from ḍ-r-b, fa-aṣābatkum muṣībatu al-mawti, afflicted by calamity of death, muṣībah from ṣ-w-b.

**Two Just Muslim Men or Two Non-Muslims While on Journey**

Ithnāni dhawā 'adlin minkum aw ākharāni min ghayrikum in antum ḍarabtum fī al-arḍi fa-aṣābatkum muṣībatu al-mawti — two just men from you or two others from other than you if you travel and afflicted by death — ithnāni, two, dhawā 'adlin, possessors of justice, just, minkum, from you, Muslims, aw ākharāni min ghayrikum, or two others from other than you, non-Muslims, allowed when traveling and no Muslim witnesses available, as Asbāb reports about Tamīm al-Dārī and 'Adī ibn Baddā' who were Christians, traveled with Muslim who died and made bequest, dispute over silver bowl, verse revealed. The permission for non-Muslim witnesses in travel shows necessity and preservation of rights.

**Keep Them After Prayer and Let Them Testify Under Oath**

Taḥbisūnahumā min ba'di al-ṣalāti fa-yuqsimāni bi-Allāhi ini irtabtum lā nashtarī bihi thamanan wa law kāna dhā qurbā wa lā naktumu shahādata Allāhi innā idhan la-mina al-āthimīn — keep them after prayer and let them swear by Allah if you doubt, we would not sell testimony for price even if close relative, nor hide testimony of Allah, otherwise we would be sinful — taḥbisūnahumā, detain them both, from ḥ-b-s, min ba'di al-ṣalāti, after prayer, ṣalāh, fa-yuqsimāni bi-Allāhi, they swear by Allah, from q-s-m, ini irtabtum, if you doubt, from r-y-b, lā nashtarī bihi thamanan, we would not sell it for price, from sh-r-y, thaman, price, wa law kāna dhā qurbā, even if close relative, dhā qurbā, possessor of closeness, wa lā naktumu shahādata Allāhi, nor hide testimony of Allah, naktumu from k-t-m, innā idhan la-mina al-āthimīn — indeed then among sinful — āthimīn from a-th-m.

**A Verse That Details Bequest Procedure**

This verse details bequest procedure when death approaches: two just Muslim witnesses, or two non-Muslims if traveling and death afflicts and no Muslims available, and if doubt, detain after prayer and let them swear oath not selling testimony for price even for relative, nor hiding Allah's testimony, otherwise sinful. The procedure shows importance of bequest, waṣiyyah, and preserving rights, and use of oath after prayer to emphasize seriousness, as prayer time sacred, oath by Allah serious. The extra verified historical note that verse revealed about Tamīm and 'Adī, Christians, who witnessed bequest of Muslim traveler, hid silver bowl, then oath, shows context and application.

**Extra Verified Note on Waṣiyyah**

Waṣiyyah, bequest, is to give up to one-third of wealth to non-heirs, as ḥadīth says one-third and one-third much, and to clarify debts, rights, etc., when death approaches. The verse's shahādatu baynikum idhā ḥaḍara aḥadakum al-mawtu ḥīna al-waṣiyyati, testimony between you when death approaches at bequest, shows that bequest should be witnessed to preserve. The extra verified juristic note that two just witnesses required, 'adl, justice, meaning upright, not sinful, and if no Muslim just witnesses while traveling, two non-Muslims allowed, shows necessity permits, but if doubt, oath after prayer required, showing caution.

Taḥbisūnahumā min ba'di al-ṣalāti — detain them after prayer — indicates after 'Aṣr prayer, as reports say, because prayer time sacred, people gathered, oath after prayer more emphatic, fear of Allah greater, as they just prayed, and gathering ensures publicity. The same practice appears for li'ān and other oaths after prayer. The verse therefore emphasizes seriousness of testimony about bequest, as it involves rights of heirs and deceased.

**Connection to Individual Responsibility and Return**

Verse 105 said you are accountable only for yourselves, deviation of others not harm if guided, to Allah you will all return and He will inform you of what you did. Verse 106-108 detail bequest witnessing when death approaches, testimony, oath, replacement if guilty, more likely true testimony or fear oaths refuted, be mindful and obey, Allah does not guide rebellious, connecting: after individual responsibility and return to Allah, practical ruling about death, bequest, witnessing, oath, showing that return to Allah includes accountability for bequests and testimonies, and procedure to ensure true testimony. The next verse will say if witnesses found guilty of false testimony, let two closest heirs replace and testify oath, our testimony truer, not transgressed, otherwise wrongdoers.

**Oath Not Selling Testimony for Price**

Lā nashtarī bihi thamanan wa law kāna dhā qurbā — we would not sell testimony for price even if close relative — indicates oath that testimony not sold for worldly price, thaman, even if beneficiary close relative, dhā qurbā, showing impartiality, justice, not favoring relative. Wa lā naktumu shahādata Allāhi — nor hide testimony of Allah — indicates not concealing testimony that Allah required to be given, as hiding testimony sinful, as 2:283 says do not hide testimony, whoever hides it, heart sinful. The oath therefore includes both not selling and not hiding, ensuring true testimony.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:107

> If they are found guilty ˹of false testimony˺, let the deceased’s two closest heirs affected by the bequest replace the witnesses and testify under oath ˹saying˺, “By Allah! Our testimony is truer than theirs. We have not transgressed. Otherwise, we would surely be wrongdoers.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**If Found Guilty Let Two Closest Heirs Replace and Testify**

Fa-in 'uthira 'alā annahumā istaḥaqqā ithman fa-ākharāni yaqūmāni maqāmahumā mina alladhīna istaḥaqqa 'alayhim al-awlayāni fa-yuqsimāni bi-Allāhi la-shahādatunā aḥaqqu min shahādatihimā wa mā i'tadaynā innā idhan la-mina al-ẓālimīn details replacement if first witnesses guilty. Fa-in 'uthira 'alā annahumā, if found, discovered, that they two, 'uthira from '-th-r, to find, discover, annahumā, that they two, istaḥaqqā ithman, deserve sin, earned sin, from ḥ-q-q, ithm, sin, fa-ākharāni, then two others, yaqūmāni maqāmahumā, stand in their place, from q-w-m, maqām, place, mina alladhīna istaḥaqqa 'alayhim al-awlayāni, from those who were wronged, closest heirs affected, al-awlayān, two closest, from w-l-y, fa-yuqsimāni bi-Allāhi, they swear by Allah, la-shahādatunā aḥaqqu min shahādatihimā, our testimony truer than theirs, aḥaqqu from ḥ-q-q, wa mā i'tadaynā, we did not transgress, from '-d-w, innā idhan la-mina al-ẓālimīn — indeed then among wrongdoers — ẓālimīn from ẓ-l-m.

**Replacement Witnesses From Heirs Affected**

Fa-ākharāni yaqūmāni maqāmahumā mina alladhīna istaḥaqqa 'alayhim al-awlayāni — two others stand in their place from those entitled, the two closest — indicates that if first two witnesses found guilty of false testimony, sin, then two others from heirs, those who have right, al-awlayān, two closest, most entitled, affected by bequest, stand in their place and testify with oath. The replacement ensures that false testimony corrected by heirs' testimony, which is more likely true, as heirs know deceased and bequest, and have interest, but oath ensures truth. The procedure shows justice: if witnesses guilty, heirs replace.

**Oath Our Testimony Truer Than Theirs Not Transgressed**

Fa-yuqsimāni bi-Allāhi la-shahādatunā aḥaqqu min shahādatihimā wa mā i'tadaynā innā idhan la-mina al-ẓālimīn — they swear by Allah, our testimony truer than theirs, we did not transgress, otherwise among wrongdoers — la-shahādatunā, our testimony, aḥaqqu, truer, more rightful, min shahādatihimā, than theirs, wa mā i'tadaynā, we did not transgress, exceed bounds, innā idhan la-mina al-ẓālimīn, otherwise among wrongdoers, ẓālimīn, wrongdoers, indicates oath that heirs' testimony truer, not transgressing, otherwise wrongdoers, similar to first witnesses' oath not selling testimony, etc., showing parallel oaths to ensure truth.

**A Verse That Provides Correction for False Testimony**

This verse provides correction for false testimony in bequest: if first witnesses found guilty of sin, false testimony, then two closest heirs affected replace and testify with oath that their testimony truer, not transgressed, otherwise wrongdoers. The correction ensures that bequest rights preserved, false testimony not allowed, and heirs can refute false witnesses with oath. The extra verified historical note that Tamīm and 'Adī, Christian witnesses, hid silver bowl, later found, then heirs swore and took bowl, shows application: first witnesses guilty, heirs replaced and testified truer, took right. The verse therefore details procedure for false testimony.

**Extra Verified Note on Istaḥaqqa Ithman**

Istaḥaqqā ithman, deserve sin, earned sin, indicates they committed sin deserving punishment, false testimony, hiding, etc., as ithm, sin, includes lying, betrayal. The phrase fa-in 'uthira 'alā annahumā istaḥaqqā ithman, if found that they deserve sin, indicates discovery of sin after oath, perhaps via evidence, confession, etc., then replacement. The extra verified juristic note that if witnesses found guilty after oath, their testimony invalidated, and heirs' testimony with oath accepted, shows that oath alone not sufficient if proven false, correction needed.

**Connection to Previous Bequest Verse**

Verse 106 said when death approaches, two just Muslim witnesses for bequest, or two non-Muslims if traveling and death afflicts, if doubt detain after prayer and let them swear not selling testimony for price even for relative nor hiding Allah's testimony otherwise sinful. Verse 107 says if they found guilty of sin, let two closest heirs affected replace and testify oath our testimony truer than theirs, not transgressed, otherwise wrongdoers, connecting: first verse details initial witnessing and oath, second verse details correction if guilty, replacement by heirs with oath, ensuring true testimony. The next verse will say in this way more likely witnesses give true testimony or fear oaths refuted by heirs, be mindful and obey, Allah does not guide rebellious.

**More Likely True Testimony as Purpose**

The purpose of this procedure, as next verse says, is more likely witnesses give true testimony or fear oaths refuted by heirs, showing deterrence: witnesses know if they lie, heirs can replace and swear truer, so they more likely give true testimony initially, or fear their oaths refuted. The procedure therefore encourages truthfulness and deters false testimony in bequests, preserving rights of deceased and heirs. The verse therefore after detailing initial and replacement witnessing, will explain wisdom: more likely true testimony.

Al-awlayāni, two closest, most entitled, from walī, close, guardian, indicates heirs who are closest to deceased, most affected by bequest, have right to claim, so they replace false witnesses. The term awlā, closer, more entitled, appears for similar contexts, showing priority. The verse therefore gives right to those most affected to correct false testimony, ensuring justice.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:108

> In this way it is more likely that witnesses will give true testimony or else fear that their oaths could be refuted by those of the heirs. Be mindful of Allah and obey. For Allah does not guide the rebellious people

**Expanded Commentary**

**In This Way More Likely True Testimony or Fear Oaths Refuted**

Dhālika adnā an ya'tū bi-al-shahādati 'alā wajhihā aw yakhāfū an turadda aymānun ba'da aymānihim wa ittaqū Allāha wa isma'ū wa Allāhu lā yahdī al-qawma al-fāsiqīn explains wisdom of bequest witnessing procedure: that is closer that they give testimony on its face or fear oaths returned after oaths, be mindful and listen, Allah does not guide rebellious. Dhālika, that, procedure of two just witnesses, oath after prayer, replacement if guilty, adnā, closer, nearer, more likely, from d-n-w, an ya'tū bi-al-shahādati 'alā wajhihā, that they bring testimony on its face, true form, wajh, face, true, aw yakhāfū, or fear, from kh-w-f, an turadda aymānun ba'da aymānihim, that oaths returned after their oaths, turadda from r-d-d, to return, refute, aymān, oaths, ba'da aymānihim, after their oaths.

**Purpose of Procedure: Encourage Truth and Deter Falsehood**

Dhālika adnā an ya'tū bi-al-shahādati 'alā wajhihā — that is closer that they bring testimony in its true form — indicates purpose of detailed procedure: to make witnesses more likely to give true testimony, 'alā wajhihā, on its face, true, not distorted, not sold, not hidden. Aw yakhāfū an turadda aymānun ba'da aymānihim — or fear that oaths will be returned after their oaths — indicates deterrence: if they give false testimony and swear, they fear that heirs will replace and swear refuting, turadda aymānun, oaths returned, refuted, after their oaths, showing that false witnesses' oaths can be refuted by heirs' oaths, so they fear, thus more likely to give true testimony initially. The dual purpose: encourage truth and deter falsehood via fear of refutation.

**Be Mindful of Allah and Listen**

Wa ittaqū Allāha wa isma'ū — be mindful of Allah and listen, obey — ittaqū, be mindful, from w-q-y, wa isma'ū, and listen, obey, from s-m-', indicates command after explaining wisdom: be mindful of Allah, fear Him in testimony, and listen, obey commands about bequest, witnessing, oath, etc. The command to listen indicates obedience, as earlier verse 92 commanded obey Allah and obey Messenger and beware. The same command appears for similar rulings.

Wa Allāhu lā yahdī al-qawma al-fāsiqīn — Allah does not guide rebellious people — lā yahdī, does not guide, al-qawma al-fāsiqīn, rebellious people, fāsiqīn from f-s-q, to rebel, transgress, indicates that those who are rebellious, fāsiq, who give false testimony, hide, sell, transgress, will not be guided by Allah, as guidance requires obedience, mindfulness. The same closing appears for similar contexts, warning that rebellion prevents guidance. The verse therefore after detailing bequest procedure and wisdom, warns that rebellious not guided.

**A Verse That Explains Wisdom of Bequest Rulings**

This verse explains wisdom, ḥikmah, of bequest rulings in 106-107: detailed procedure of two just witnesses, oath after prayer, replacement if guilty, ensures more likely true testimony or fear of refutation, encouraging truth and deterring falsehood, preserving rights of deceased and heirs. The explanation shows that Sharī'ah rulings have wisdom, not arbitrary, and purpose is to achieve justice, truth, preservation of rights. The extra verified note that procedure includes witnesses, oath, replacement, deterrence, all to ensure true testimony, shows comprehensive justice system for bequests, especially while traveling.

**Extra Verified Note on Shahādah 'Alā Wajhihā**

Shahādah 'alā wajhihā, testimony on its face, true form, indicates testimony as it should be, without distortion, selling, hiding, as earlier verse 106 oath not selling for price even for relative nor hiding Allah's testimony, and verse 107 oath our testimony truer, not transgressed. The phrase 'alā wajhihā, on its face, indicates true, proper, as it is, not altered. The extra verified linguistic note that wajh, face, indicates essence, true form, as in 'alā wajhihi, as it should be, shows that testimony should be true, proper.

**Connection to Bequest Verses and Individual Responsibility**

Verses 106-108 form unit on bequest witnessing: 106 initial witnessing with two just Muslims or two non-Muslims if traveling, doubt oath after prayer not selling nor hiding, otherwise sinful; 107 if found guilty, two closest heirs replace and testify oath truer, not transgressed, otherwise wrongdoers; 108 wisdom more likely true testimony or fear oaths refuted, be mindful and obey, Allah does not guide rebellious. The unit follows verse 105 about individual responsibility and return to Allah, showing practical ruling about death and accountability for bequests. The next verse will shift to Day Allah will gather messengers and ask what response received, they say we have no knowledge, You Knower of unseen, moving to eschatological scene with messengers.

**Fear of Oaths Returned as Deterrence**

Aw yakhāfū an turadda aymānun ba'da aymānihim — or fear that oaths returned after their oaths — shows deterrence mechanism: if witnesses give false oath, heirs can swear refuting, returning oaths, so false witnesses fear refutation, thus deterred from lying. The mechanism is similar to other oaths where refutation possible, encouraging truth. The verse therefore shows that Sharī'ah uses both encouragement and deterrence to achieve justice.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:109

> ˹Consider˺ the Day Allah will gather the messengers and say, “What response did you receive?” They will reply, “We have no knowledge ˹compared to You˺! You ˹alone˺ are indeed the Knower of all unseen.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Day Allah Will Gather Messengers and Ask Response**

Yawma yajma'u Allāhu al-rusula fa-yaqūlu mādhā ujibtum qālū lā 'ilma lanā innaka Anta 'Allāmu al-ghuyūb reports Day when Allah gathers messengers and asks what response received, they say no knowledge for us, You Knower of unseen. Yawma, Day, from y-w-m, yajma'u Allāhu al-rusula, Allah gathers messengers, yajma'u from j-m-', rusul from r-s-l, fa-yaqūlu, then He says, mādhā ujibtum, what were you answered, from j-w-b, qālū, they said, lā 'ilma lanā, no knowledge for us, 'ilm, knowledge, lanā, for us, innaka Anta 'Allāmu al-ghuyūb — indeed You are Knower of unseen — innaka, indeed You, Anta, You, 'Allām, great Knower, intensive, al-ghuyūb, unseen, plural of ghayb.

**What Response Did You Receive**

Mādhā ujibtum — what response did you receive? — question to messengers about response of their peoples, did they believe, obey, or disbelieve, deny, as earlier verses showed messengers came with clear proofs yet many transgressed, and People of Book distorted, hid, etc. The question is not for Allah's lack of knowledge, as He knows, but to establish proof, and to show messengers' humility, and to make peoples' response manifest, as messengers will say we have no knowledge compared to You, You Knower of unseen, indicating they know outward response but not inward, or they disclaim knowledge due to awe of Day, or they refer knowledge to Allah who knows unseen.

**We Have No Knowledge You Knower of Unseen**

Qālū lā 'ilma lanā innaka Anta 'Allāmu al-ghuyūb — they said no knowledge for us, indeed You Knower of unseen — lā 'ilma lanā, no knowledge for us, compared to Your knowledge, or no knowledge of unseen, inner, as we only know outward, innaka Anta 'Allāmu al-ghuyūb, You Knower of unseen, 'Allām, intensive knower, al-ghuyūb, unseen, plural, indicating all unseen, inner, hidden. The response shows messengers' humility, adab, and referral of knowledge to Allah, who knows unseen, including what peoples concealed, as earlier verse 99 Allah knows what you reveal and conceal, and verse 109 messengers say no knowledge, You Knower of unseen, showing that true knowledge of response, including inner, belongs to Allah.

**A Verse That Shows Gathering of Messengers on Judgment Day**

This verse shows gathering of messengers on Judgment Day, yawma yajma'u Allāhu al-rusula, Day Allah gathers messengers, and asks what response received, to establish proof against peoples, and messengers respond with humility, no knowledge for us, You Knower of unseen. The scene is eschatological, similar to other verses where Allah gathers messengers and questions, as 7:6 We will question those sent to and question messengers. The verse therefore after discussing bequest and return to Allah (5:105-108), moves to Day of gathering messengers and questioning, showing accountability.

'Allām al-ghuyūb, Knower of unseen, intensive form, appears for Allah's knowledge of all unseen, hidden, as in 5:109, 116, 34:48, etc. The extra verified note that ghuyūb plural indicates all types of unseen, inner, hidden, past, future, etc., and 'Allām intensive indicates great knowledge, encompassing all, shows that messengers refer knowledge to Allah who knows all unseen, including what peoples concealed, their inner beliefs, etc., not just outward response. The verse therefore emphasizes Allah's comprehensive knowledge.

**Connection to Previous Return to Allah**

Verse 105 said to Allah you will all return and He will inform you of what you used to do, 108 be mindful and obey, Allah does not guide rebellious, 109 Day Allah gathers messengers and says what response received, they say no knowledge, You Knower of unseen, connecting return to Allah and informing of deeds to gathering messengers and questioning response, showing that return includes questioning messengers and peoples, and Allah's knowledge of unseen, including what peoples revealed and concealed, as 5:99 said Allah knows what you reveal and conceal, and messengers disclaim knowledge, referring to Allah Knower of unseen.

**Why Messengers Say No Knowledge**

Messengers say lā 'ilma lanā, no knowledge for us, not because they have no knowledge of peoples' outward response, as they know who believed, who disbelieved, but because on Day of Judgment, awe, or because they know Allah knows inner, unseen, better, or because they disclaim knowledge of peoples' inner, or because they refer to Allah's knowledge as true, comprehensive, compared to theirs partial. The same response appears for similar questions, showing humility and adab of messengers, not claiming knowledge that belongs to Allah alone, as earlier verse 116-118 will show 'Īsā's response similarly humble, referring knowledge to Allah.

**Day of Gathering as Proof**

Yawma yajma'u Allāhu al-rusula fa-yaqūlu mādhā ujibtum — Day Allah gathers messengers and says what response — is for proof, to make peoples' response manifest, and to show messengers fulfilled duty of delivery, as earlier verse 92, 99 said Messenger's duty only clear delivery, and if turn away, know duty only delivery, and verse 109 shows gathering and questioning to establish that delivery occurred and response known to Allah Knower of unseen. The next verse will focus on 'Īsā, son of Maryam, and Allah's favors upon him and his mother, support with holy spirit, teaching, miracles, prevention from Children of Israel harming him when he came with clear proofs.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:110

> And ˹on Judgment Day˺ Allah will say, “O Jesus, son of Mary! Remember My favour upon you and your mother: how I supported you with the holy spirit so you spoke to people in ˹your˺ infancy and adulthood. How I taught you writing, wisdom, the Torah, and the Gospel. How you moulded a bird from clay—by My Will—and breathed into it and it became a ˹real˺ bird—by My Will. How you healed the blind and the lepers—by My Will. How you brought the dead to life—by My Will. How I prevented the Children of Israel from harming you when you came to them with clear proofs and the disbelievers among them said, “This is nothing but pure magic.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Allah Says O Jesus Son of Mary Remember Favor Upon You and Mother**

Idh qāla Allāhu yā 'Īsā ibna Maryama udhkur ni'matī 'alayka wa 'alā wālidatika idh ayyadtuka bi-rūḥi al-qudusi tukallimu al-nāsa fī al-mahdi wa kahlan wa idh 'allamtuka al-kitāba wa al-ḥikmata wa al-Tawrāta wa al-Injīla wa idh takhluqu mina al-ṭīni ka-hay'ati al-ṭayri bi-idhnī fa-tanfukhu fīhā fa-takūnu ṭayran bi-idhnī wa tubri'u al-akmaha wa al-abraṣa bi-idhnī wa idh tukhriju al-mawtā bi-idhnī wa idh kafaftu Banī Isrā'īla 'anka idh ji'tahum bi-al-bayyināti fa-qāla alladhīna kafarū minhum in hādhā illā siḥrun mubīn recounts favors upon 'Īsā and Maryam. Idh qāla Allāhu, when Allah said, yā 'Īsā ibna Maryama, O Jesus son of Mary, udhkur ni'matī, remember My favor, ni'mah from n-'-m, 'alayka wa 'alā wālidatika, upon you and your mother, wālidah, mother.

**Supported With Holy Spirit So Spoke in Infancy and Adulthood**

Idh ayyadtuka bi-rūḥi al-qudusi tukallimu al-nāsa fī al-mahdi wa kahlan — when I supported you with holy spirit, you spoke to people in cradle and adulthood — ayyadtuka from a-y-d, to support, strengthen, bi-rūḥi al-qudusi, with holy spirit, rūḥ, spirit, qudus, holy, pure, tukallimu al-nāsa, you speak to people, from k-l-m, fī al-mahdi, in cradle, mahd, wa kahlan, and adulthood, kahl, middle age. The support with holy spirit, rūḥ al-qudus, is Jibrīl, or spirit of holiness, strengthening 'Īsā, enabling speaking in cradle, as 19:29-33 reports 'Īsā spoke in cradle defending Maryam, and in adulthood, kahlan, speaking wisdom. The same favor appears in 2:87, 2:253, 5:110.

**Taught Writing, Wisdom, Torah, Gospel**

Wa idh 'allamtuka al-kitāba wa al-ḥikmata wa al-Tawrāta wa al-Injīla — and when I taught you writing, wisdom, Torah, Gospel — 'allamtuka from '-l-m, al-kitāba, writing, book, al-ḥikmata, wisdom, from ḥ-k-m, al-Tawrāta, Torah, al-Injīla, Gospel, indicates Allah taught 'Īsā kitāb, writing, or Book, ḥikmah, wisdom, understanding, Tawrāt, Torah, Injīl, Gospel, showing comprehensive knowledge, as earlier verse 46 said We gave him Gospel in which guidance and light, confirming Torah. The teaching is favor, ni'mah, upon 'Īsā.

**Moulded Bird From Clay By Will and Healed Blind Lepers**

Wa idh takhluqu mina al-ṭīni ka-hay'ati al-ṭayri bi-idhnī fa-tanfukhu fīhā fa-takūnu ṭayran bi-idhnī wa tubri'u al-akmaha wa al-abraṣa bi-idhnī — and when you mould from clay like form of bird by My permission, then breathe into it and it becomes bird by My permission, and you heal blind and leper by My permission — takhluqu, you create, mould, from kh-l-q, mina al-ṭīni, from clay, ṭīn, ka-hay'ati al-ṭayri, like form of bird, hay'ah, form, ṭayr, bird, bi-idhnī, by My permission, idhn, permission, fa-tanfukhu fīhā, you breathe into it, from n-f-kh, fa-takūnu ṭayran bi-idhnī, becomes bird by My permission, wa tubri'u al-akmaha, heal blind, akmah, born blind, wa al-abraṣa, leper, abraṣ, bi-idhnī, by My permission. The miracles by Allah's permission, bi-idhnī repeated, emphasizes that miracles not by 'Īsā's own divinity but by Allah's permission, as earlier verse 75 said Messiah and mother ate food, showing neediness, not divinity.

**Brought Dead to Life By Will and Prevented Children of Israel From Harming**

Wa idh tukhriju al-mawtā bi-idhnī wa idh kafaftu Banī Isrā'īla 'anka idh ji'tahum bi-al-bayyināti fa-qāla alladhīna kafarū minhum in hādhā illā siḥrun mubīn — and when you bring dead to life by My permission, and when I prevented Children of Israel from you when you came with clear proofs, disbelievers said this is nothing but clear magic — tukhriju al-mawtā, bring dead out, from kh-r-j, mawtā, dead, bi-idhnī, by My permission, wa idh kafaftu Banī Isrā'īla 'anka, I prevented Children of Israel from you, kafaftu from k-f-f, to prevent, withhold, 'anka, from you, idh ji'tahum bi-al-bayyināti, when you came with clear proofs, bayyināt, clear proofs, fa-qāla alladhīna kafarū minhum, disbelievers among them said, in hādhā illā siḥrun mubīn — this is nothing but clear magic — siḥr, magic, mubīn, clear. The prevention from harming shows Allah protected 'Īsā from Children of Israel when they wanted to kill, as they said magic.

**A Verse That Lists Favors Upon 'Īsā and Maryam**

This verse lists favors, ni'am, upon 'Īsā and his mother Maryam: support with holy spirit, speaking in cradle and adulthood, taught writing, wisdom, Torah, Gospel, moulding bird from clay by permission and breathing and becoming bird by permission, healing blind and leper by permission, bringing dead to life by permission, preventing Children of Israel from harming when came with clear proofs and disbelievers said clear magic. The list shows that all miracles by Allah's permission, bi-idhnī, repeated four times, emphasizing not divinity but permission, as earlier verses about 'Īsā not divine, Messiah only messenger, ate food, etc., and verse 75-76 warned not to worship besides Allah. The verse therefore after gathering messengers and asking response, focuses on 'Īsā as example, listing favors and miracles by permission, and disbelievers' accusation of magic.

**Extra Verified Note on Bi-Idhnī Repetition**

Bi-idhnī, by My permission, repeated four times in verse for bird, healing blind, leper, bringing dead to life, shows that miracles not independent but by Allah's permission, will, as 'Īsā himself said by Allah's leave, not own divinity, as earlier verse 110 itself says. The extra verified note that Qur'an emphasizes bi-idhn Allāh for 'Īsā's miracles to refute divinity claim, as Christians claimed divinity due to miracles, but Qur'an says by Allah's permission, not own, as in 3:49 I create bird by Allah's permission, heal by Allah's permission, bring dead by Allah's permission, shows consistent emphasis. The following verse will discuss inspiring disciples to believe in Allah and messenger, they declared belief and bear witness we submit.

**Connection to Previous Gathering of Messengers**

Verse 109 said Day Allah gathers messengers and asks what response received, they say no knowledge, You Knower of unseen. Verse 110 says and Allah will say O Jesus son of Mary remember favor upon you and mother, how supported with holy spirit, spoke in infancy and adulthood, taught writing, wisdom, Torah, Gospel, moulded bird by permission, healed blind and lepers by permission, brought dead by permission, prevented Children of Israel from harming when came with clear proofs and disbelievers said clear magic, connecting: after general gathering of messengers, specific questioning of 'Īsā about favors and response, as 'Īsā is among messengers gathered, and his story detailed, including miracles by permission and disbelievers' accusation of magic, leading to next verses about disciples, table spread, and questioning about worshipping him and mother as gods.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:111

> And how I inspired the disciples, “Believe in Me and My messenger!” They declared, “We believe and bear witness that we fully submit ˹to Allah˺.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**How I Inspired Disciples Believe in Me and My Messenger**

Wa idh awḥaytu ilā al-ḥawāriyyīna an āminū bī wa bi-rasūlī qālū āmannā wa ishhad bi-annanā muslimūn continues favors upon 'Īsā: and when I inspired disciples, believe in Me and My messenger, they said we believe and bear witness we submit. Wa idh awḥaytu, and when I inspired, from w-ḥ-y, to inspire, reveal, ilā al-ḥawāriyyīna, to disciples, ḥawāriyyūn from ḥ-w-r, white, pure, helpers, an āminū bī, that believe in Me, āminū from ā-m-n, wa bi-rasūlī, and in My messenger, rasūl, 'Īsā, qālū, they said, āmannā, we believe, wa ishhad, bear witness, from sh-h-d, bi-annanā muslimūn — that we are submitters — muslimūn from s-l-m, submit.

**Ḥawāriyyūn as Disciples Helpers**

Al-ḥawāriyyūn, disciples, from ḥawārī, white, pure, or helper, supporter, were close companions of 'Īsā, twelve disciples, who supported him, as Qur'an says ḥawāriyyūn as helpers of Allah, as in 3:52, 61:14. Awḥaytu ilā al-ḥawāriyyīna, I inspired disciples, waḥy, inspiration, not prophetic revelation but inspiration to believe, as Allah inspires hearts, or via 'Īsā's call. An āminū bī wa bi-rasūlī — believe in Me and My messenger — indicates Allah inspired them to believe in Allah and His messenger 'Īsā, as belief in Allah and messenger required, as earlier verses about believing in Allah and messenger.

**We Believe and Bear Witness We Submit**

Qālū āmannā wa ishhad bi-annanā muslimūn — they said we believe and bear witness we are submitters — āmannā, we believe, ishhad, bear witness, bi-annanā muslimūn, that we are muslimūn, submitters to Allah, indicates disciples declared belief in Allah and messenger 'Īsā and submission, islām, to Allah, as true followers of 'Īsā were muslims, submitters, not Christians claiming Trinity, as earlier verses about 'Īsā's true message worship Allah my Lord and your Lord (5:117). The declaration shows that ḥawāriyyūn were muslims, submitters, as Qur'an calls them.

This verse shows inspiration of disciples to believe in Allah and messenger 'Īsā, and their declaration of belief and submission, as favor upon 'Īsā, supporting him with helpers who believed and submitted. The inspiration is favor, as Allah inspired disciples to support 'Īsā, believe, submit, as earlier verse 110 said support with holy spirit, teaching, miracles, prevention, and verse 111 inspiration of disciples, showing comprehensive support for 'Īsā. The extra verified note that ḥawāriyyūn were twelve, as Gospels mention, and Qur'an mentions them as helpers, supporters, who believed and submitted, shows continuity.

**Extra Verified Note on Muslimūn**

Muslimūn, submitters, indicates that disciples of 'Īsā were muslims, submitters to Allah, following true message of 'Īsā which was islām, submission, worship Allah alone, as 3:52 ḥawāriyyūn said we are helpers of Allah, we believe in Allah, bear witness we are muslims. The extra verified note that Qur'an calls followers of previous prophets muslims, submitters, as Ibrāhīm was muslim, not Jew nor Christian, and disciples muslims, shows that islām is religion of all prophets, submission to Allah, not specific to Muhammad's followers only, but true followers of all prophets were muslims. The verse therefore shows disciples as muslims.

**Connection to Favors Upon 'Īsā**

Verse 110 listed favors upon 'Īsā and mother: support with holy spirit, speaking in cradle and adulthood, teaching writing, wisdom, Torah, Gospel, bird miracle, healing blind, leper, bringing dead, prevention from Children of Israel harming. Verse 111 continues: and how I inspired disciples, believe in Me and My messenger, they declared we believe and bear witness we submit, connecting as additional favor: inspiration of disciples to believe and support, showing that 'Īsā not alone but supported by helpers who believed and submitted. The next verse will discuss when disciples asked O Jesus son of Mary would your Lord send down table spread from heaven, Jesus said fear Allah if believers, showing their request.

**Inspiration Versus Revelation**

Awḥaytu ilā al-ḥawāriyyīna, I inspired disciples, waḥy, inspiration, not necessarily prophetic revelation, wahy nubuwwah, but ilhām, inspiration to heart, or via messenger's call, as Allah inspires believers to believe, as earlier verse about inspiring disciples. The distinction shows that disciples not prophets but inspired to believe, as believers inspired to believe, but prophets receive revelation. The verse therefore shows Allah's favor in inspiring disciples to believe in Him and messenger 'Īsā, and their response belief and submission.

Al-ḥawāriyyūn as helpers of Allah, anṣār Allāh, as 61:14 says O you who believe be helpers of Allah as Jesus son of Mary said to disciples who are my helpers to Allah, disciples said we are helpers of Allah. The verse therefore shows disciples as helpers, supporters of 'Īsā and Allah's religion, who believed and submitted, and bore witness. The next verse will show their request for table spread from heaven.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:112

> ˹Remember˺ when the disciples asked, “O Jesus, son of Mary! Would your Lord be willing to send down to us a table spread with food from heaven?” Jesus answered, “Fear Allah if you are ˹truly˺ believers.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**When Disciples Asked Would Your Lord Send Down Table Spread**

Idh qāla al-ḥawāriyyūna yā 'Īsā ibna Maryama hal yastaṭī'u rabbuka an yunazzila 'alaynā mā'idatan mina al-samā'i qāla ittaqū Allāha in kuntum mu'minīn reports disciples' request. Idh qāla al-ḥawāriyyūna, when disciples said, yā 'Īsā ibna Maryama, O Jesus son of Mary, hal yastaṭī'u rabbuka, would your Lord be able, from ṭ-w-', to be able, an yunazzila 'alaynā, to send down upon us, from n-z-l, mā'idatan, table spread, from m-y-d, to move, because table moves with food, mina al-samā'i, from heaven, samā', qāla, he said, ittaqū Allāha, fear Allah, from w-q-y, in kuntum mu'minīn, if you are believers, from ā-m-n.

Mā'idah, table spread with food, from māda, to move, because food moves, or table that has food, indicates table with food, feast, as disciples asked for table spread from heaven as sign and provision. The request for mā'idah from heaven is miracle, similar to manna and quails for Banī Isrā'īl, but specific table spread. The sūrah named al-Mā'idah after this table spread, as central story, showing importance. The request shows disciples wanted tangible sign to reassure hearts, verify truthfulness, become witnesses, as next verse says.

**Would Your Lord Be Able Versus Would Your Lord Be Willing**

Hal yastaṭī'u rabbuka an yunazzila 'alaynā mā'idatan mina al-samā'i — would your Lord be able to send down table spread from heaven — qirā'ah variation: hal yastaṭī'u rabbuka, would your Lord be able, or hal tastatī'u rabbaka, would you be able to ask your Lord, as some readings, indicating question about ability or about asking. The translation says would your Lord be willing, interpreting as willingness, not doubting ability, as disciples believers, not doubting Allah's ability, but asking if willing to send, as courtesy, or asking 'Īsā if he can ask his Lord. The extra verified note that reading yastaṭī'u rabbuka, would your Lord be able, does not indicate doubt about Allah's ability but about 'Īsā's ability to ask, or about whether Allah will, as tafsīr explains, showing disciples did not doubt Allah's power.

**Jesus Answered Fear Allah If Truly Believers**

Qāla ittaqū Allāha in kuntum mu'minīn — he said fear Allah if you are believers — qāla, 'Īsā said, ittaqū Allāha, fear Allah, be mindful, in kuntum mu'minīn, if you are believers, indicates 'Īsā's response: fear Allah, do not ask for such sign if you are truly believers, as asking for miracles without need may be inappropriate, or fear Allah's punishment if you ask then disbelieve, as next verse about torment never inflicted. The response shows 'Īsā's piety, advising fear of Allah, not hastening to request miracles, as believers should fear Allah and not test.

**A Verse That Introduces Table Spread Story**

This verse introduces story of table spread, mā'idah, which gives sūrah its name, Sūrat al-Mā'idah, Chapter of Table Spread. The story: disciples asked 'Īsā to ask Lord to send down table spread from heaven, 'Īsā said fear Allah if believers, they explained wish to eat, reassure hearts, verify truthfulness, become witnesses, 'Īsā prayed O Allah our Lord send table spread as feast for first and last and sign from You, provide, Best Provider, Allah answered I am sending it down, but whoever denies afterwards will be subjected to torment never inflicted. The story shows request, purpose, prayer, answer, warning, as central narrative.

Sūrah al-Mā'idah named after mā'idah, table spread, mentioned in verses 112-115, as table spread requested by disciples and sent down as feast and sign. The extra verified note that sūrah also called Sūrat al-'Uqūd, Chapter of Contracts, as first verse says fulfill contracts, and sūrah discusses contracts, covenants, as earlier verse 1, shows two names: al-Mā'idah and al-'Uqūd. The naming after table spread highlights miracle as sign for disciples and later generations.

**Connection to Inspiration of Disciples**

Verse 111 said Allah inspired disciples believe in Me and messenger, they declared belief and submission. Verse 112 says when disciples asked O Jesus son of Mary would your Lord send down table spread from heaven, Jesus said fear Allah if believers, connecting: after inspiration and declaration of belief and submission, disciples asked for table spread as additional sign to reassure hearts, verify truthfulness, become witnesses, showing that even believers may seek reassurance, as Ibrāhīm asked to see how Allah gives life to dead to reassure heart, and disciples asked for table spread for similar reasons, not due to doubt but reassurance.

**Fear Allah If Believers as Advice**

Ittaqū Allāha in kuntum mu'minīn — fear Allah if you are believers — is advice from 'Īsā to disciples, indicating that true believers fear Allah, do not ask for unnecessary signs, or fear consequences if ask then disbelieve, as warning in verse 115 about torment never inflicted for deniers after table spread sent. The advice shows 'Īsā's concern for disciples, advising taqwā, fear of Allah, as condition of belief, as earlier verses about fearing Allah in secret, being mindful, etc. The next verse will explain disciples' purpose: wish to eat, reassure hearts, verify truthfulness, become witnesses.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:113

> They said, “We ˹only˺ wish to eat from it to reassure our hearts, to verify you are indeed truthful to us, and to become its witnesses.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Disciples Said Wish to Eat Reassure Hearts Verify Truthfulness Become Witnesses**

Qālū nurīdu an na'kula minhā wa taṭma'inna qulūbunā wa na'lama an qad ṣadaqtanā wa nakūna 'alayhā mina al-shāhidīn explains disciples' purpose for requesting table spread. Qālū, they said, disciples, nurīdu, we wish, want, from r-w-d, an na'kula minhā, to eat from it, from a-k-l, minhā, from it, table spread, wa taṭma'inna qulūbunā, and hearts reassured, from ṭ-m-ʾ-n, to be tranquil, qulūb, hearts, from q-l-b, wa na'lama an qad ṣadaqtanā, and we know you were truthful to us, na'lama from '-l-m, ṣadaqtanā from ṣ-d-q, to be truthful, wa nakūna 'alayhā mina al-shāhidīn — and we become over it among witnesses — nakūna from k-w-n, 'alayhā, over it, concerning it, mina al-shāhidīn, among witnesses, shāhidīn from sh-h-d.

**Four Purposes for Requesting Table Spread**

Disciples mentioned four purposes: an na'kula minhā, to eat from it, physical need, provision, wa taṭma'inna qulūbunā, hearts reassured, spiritual reassurance, tranquility, as Ibrāhīm asked to see resurrection to reassure heart, wa na'lama an qad ṣadaqtanā, know you were truthful to us, verification of 'Īsā's truthfulness, as miracle would verify, wa nakūna 'alayhā mina al-shāhidīn, become witnesses over it, to bear witness to miracle, to others, to Banī Isrā'īl, or to be witnesses for future. The four purposes show that request not due to doubt but for reassurance, verification, witnessing, and eating, as legitimate needs.

**We Wish to Eat From It**

Nurīdu an na'kula minhā — we wish to eat from it — indicates desire to eat from heavenly table spread, as provision, blessing, and to partake in miracle, as eating from heavenly food is blessing, as manna and quails for Banī Isrā'īl, and table spread would be provision. The wish to eat shows human need, not just spiritual, but physical, and eating from heavenly table would be honor.

Wa taṭma'inna qulūbunā — and our hearts reassured — taṭma'inna, become tranquil, reassured, from ṭuma'nīnah, tranquility, qulūbunā, our hearts, indicates spiritual purpose: hearts reassured by seeing miracle, increasing faith, tranquility, as Ibrāhīm said *“But to reassure my heart”* (2:260) when asking to see how Allah gives life to dead, not due to doubt but reassurance. The same purpose for disciples: seeing table spread from heaven would reassure hearts, increase certainty, tranquility.

**Know You Were Truthful and Become Witnesses**

Wa na'lama an qad ṣadaqtanā — and we know you were truthful to us — na'lama, we know, an qad ṣadaqtanā, you were truthful to us, indicating verification of 'Īsā's truthfulness as messenger, as miracle would prove truthfulness. Wa nakūna 'alayhā mina al-shāhidīn — and we become among witnesses over it — indicates they would become witnesses to miracle, bearing witness to Banī Isrā'īl or future generations, that table spread came from heaven, as sign. The witnessing purpose shows they wanted to be witnesses to miracle, to testify.

This verse explains purpose of disciples' request for table spread: not doubt or testing, but four legitimate purposes: eating, reassurance, verification, witnessing, showing that request was sincere, not obstinate like Banī Isrā'īl who asked for cow details to evade, but for reassurance and witnessing. The explanation shows disciples' sincerity and 'Īsā's subsequent prayer for table spread as feast and sign, as next verse says. The extra verified note that disciples' request similar to Ibrāhīm's request for reassurance, shows that seeking reassurance via sign is allowed for believers to increase certainty, not due to doubt.

**Extra Verified Note on Shāhidīn**

Shāhidīn, witnesses, indicates those who bear witness to miracle, testify to others, as disciples would witness table spread from heaven and testify to Banī Isrā'īl, or be witnesses to Allah's power, provision, and 'Īsā's truthfulness. The extra verified note that shahādah, witnessing, is important in Islam, as earlier verses about witnessing bequest, etc., shows that disciples wanted to be witnesses to this great sign, as honor and responsibility. The next verse will show 'Īsā's prayer for table spread as feast for first and last and sign from You, provide, Best Provider.

**Connection to Previous Request and Fear Allah**

Verse 112 said disciples asked would your Lord send down table spread from heaven, Jesus said fear Allah if believers. Verse 113 says they said we wish to eat from it to reassure hearts, verify you truthful, become witnesses, connecting: after 'Īsā advised fear Allah if believers, disciples explained sincere purposes, not doubt or testing, but eating, reassurance, verification, witnessing, showing that request was sincere, not requiring fear, but legitimate, so 'Īsā then prayed for table spread, as next verse says, showing that after explanation, prayer made.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:114

> Jesus, son of Mary, prayed, “O Allah, our Lord! Send us from heaven a table spread with food as a feast for us—the first and last of us—and as a sign from You. Provide for us! You are indeed the Best Provider.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Jesus Prayed O Allah Our Lord Send Table Spread From Heaven**

Qāla 'Īsā ibnu Maryama Allāhumma Rabbanā anzil 'alaynā mā'idatan mina al-samā'i takūnu lanā 'īdan li-awwalinā wa ākhirinā wa āyatan minka wa urzuqnā wa Anta Khayru al-rāziqīn reports 'Īsā's prayer. Qāla 'Īsā ibnu Maryama, Jesus son of Mary said, Allāhumma Rabbanā, O Allah our Lord, Allāhumma from Allāh, Rabbanā, our Lord, from r-b-b, anzil 'alaynā, send down upon us, from n-z-l, mā'idatan mina al-samā'i, table spread from heaven, mā'idah from m-y-d, samā' from s-m-w, takūnu lanā 'īdan li-awwalinā wa ākhirinā, be for us feast for first and last of us, 'īd from '-w-d, to return, feast recurring, li-awwalinā, for first of us, awwal from a-w-l, wa ākhirinā, and last of us, ākhir from a-kh-r, wa āyatan minka, and sign from You, āyah from a-y-y, sign, minka, from You, wa urzuqnā, provide for us, from r-z-q, wa Anta Khayru al-rāziqīn — You Best of providers — Khayr, best, rāziqīn, providers.

**Feast for First and Last of Us**

Takūnu lanā 'īdan li-awwalinā wa ākhirinā — be for us feast for first and last — 'īdan, feast, festival, recurring celebration, from 'āda, to return, because feast returns each year, li-awwalinā, for first of us, those present, wa ākhirinā, and last of us, those later, future generations, indicates table spread as feast, 'īd, celebration, for present and future, recurring, as sign and provision that would be celebrated, remembered, as feast day. The same concept of 'īd as recurring feast, as Muslims have 'Īd al-Fiṭr and 'Īd al-Aḍḥā recurring, shows that table spread would be recurring feast, celebrated by first and last, present and future, as sign.

**Sign From You and Provide Best Provider**

Wa āyatan minka wa urzuqnā wa Anta Khayru al-rāziqīn — and sign from You and provide for us, You Best of providers — āyatan minka, sign from You, āyah, sign, miracle, proof, from You, indicating table spread as sign from Allah, proof of 'Īsā's truthfulness and Allah's power, wa urzuqnā, provide for us, provision, from r-z-q, wa Anta Khayru al-rāziqīn, You Best of providers, Khayr al-rāziqīn, Best of those who provide, indicating Allah is Best Provider, as He provides from heaven, provision not from others. The prayer includes both sign and provision, spiritual and physical, as disciples wanted both.

**A Verse That Shows 'Īsā's Prayer for Table Spread**

This verse shows 'Īsā's prayer for table spread from heaven as feast for first and last and sign from Allah, provision, Best Provider, after disciples explained sincere purposes. The prayer shows 'Īsā's humility, calling Allāhumma Rabbanā, O Allah our Lord, acknowledging Allah as Lord, not himself divine, as earlier verses about 'Īsā not divine, only messenger, worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, and prayer for provision from Allah, Best Provider, shows dependence on Allah, not divinity. The extra verified note that 'Īsā's prayer includes 'īdan li-awwalinā wa ākhirinā, feast for first and last, indicates that table spread would be recurring celebration, feast day, for present and future, as sign and provision.

'Īd, feast, from 'āda, to return, because it returns each year, celebration, as 'Īd al-Fiṭr returns after Ramaḍān, 'Īd al-Aḍḥā returns after ḥajj. The extra verified note that 'Īsā asked table spread be 'īd for first and last, meaning recurring feast celebrated by those present and those later, as sign, shows that table spread would be annual celebration, remembering miracle, as Christians later celebrated, or as intended. The verse's closing Anta Khayru al-rāziqīn, You Best of providers, shows that provision ultimately from Allah, Best Provider, not from 'Īsā or others.

**Connection to Disciples' Request**

Verse 112 disciples asked would your Lord send down table spread, Jesus said fear Allah if believers, 113 they said wish to eat, reassure hearts, verify truthful, become witnesses, 114 Jesus prayed O Allah our Lord send table spread from heaven as feast for first and last and sign from You, provide, Best Provider, connecting: after request and explanation of purposes, 'Īsā prayed for table spread with additional purposes: feast for first and last, sign from You, provision, Best Provider, expanding disciples' four purposes to include feast for present and future and sign from Allah, showing comprehensive prayer. The next verse will say Allah answered I am sending it down, but whoever denies afterwards will be subjected to torment never inflicted.

**Prayer Shows Humility and Dependence**

Allāhumma Rabbanā anzil 'alaynā mā'idatan mina al-samā'i — O Allah our Lord send down upon us table spread from heaven — shows 'Īsā's humility, dependence on Allah, acknowledging Allah as Lord, Rabbanā, our Lord, not himself Lord, as earlier verse 72 said Messiah said O Children of Israel worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, and verse 117 I only told them worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, showing consistent message of 'Īsā: worship Allah alone, my Lord and your Lord, not worship me. The prayer therefore refutes divinity claim, as 'Īsā prays to Allah for provision, Best Provider, not providing himself.

**Feast and Sign as Dual Purpose**

Takūnu lanā 'īdan li-awwalinā wa ākhirinā wa āyatan minka — be for us feast for first and last and sign from You — dual purpose: 'īdan, feast, celebration, for first and last, present and future, wa āyatan minka, sign from You, miracle, proof, showing that table spread would be both celebration and sign, provision and proof, physical and spiritual, as disciples wanted eating and reassurance, verification, witnessing, and 'Īsā added feast for first and last and sign from Allah, making it comprehensive. The next verse will show Allah's answer and warning.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.
