## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:115

> Allah answered, “I am sending it down to you. But whoever among you denies afterwards will be subjected to a torment I have never inflicted on anyone of My creation.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Allah Answered I Am Sending It Down to You**

Qāla Allāhu innī munazziluhā 'alaykum fa-man yakfur ba'da dhālika minkum fa-innī u'adhdhibuhu 'adhāban lā u'adhdhibuhu aḥadan mina al-'ālamīn reports Allah's answer to 'Īsā's prayer. Qāla Allāhu, Allah said, innī, indeed I, munazziluhā 'alaykum, sending it down upon you, from n-z-l, hā, it, table spread, 'alaykum, upon you, fa-man yakfur ba'da dhālika minkum, but whoever disbelieves after that among you, man yakfur, whoever disbelieves, from k-f-r, ba'da dhālika, after that, after table spread sent, minkum, among you, fa-innī u'adhdhibuhu 'adhāban lā u'adhdhibuhu aḥadan mina al-'ālamīn — I will punish him with punishment I never punished anyone among worlds — u'adhdhibuhu from '-dh-b, 'adhāban, punishment, lā u'adhdhibuhu, I did not punish, aḥadan, anyone, mina al-'ālamīn, among worlds, 'ālamīn from '-l-m.

**Sending Down Table Spread as Answer**

Innī munazziluhā 'alaykum — indeed I am sending it down upon you — munazziluhā, sending it down, from nazzala, intensive of nazala, indicating sending down with emphasis, hā, table spread, 'alaykum, upon you, disciples and 'Īsā, shows Allah's answer to prayer: He will send down table spread from heaven as requested, as feast for first and last and sign, provision, as 'Īsā prayed. The answer shows Allah's responsiveness to prayer of righteous, as 'Īsā, messenger, prayed and Allah answered, sending miracle. The extra verified historical note that table spread was sent down with foods, as tafsīr reports, with fish, bread, etc., as feast, shows fulfillment.

**Whoever Denies Afterwards Torment Never Inflicted**

Fa-man yakfur ba'da dhālika minkum fa-innī u'adhdhibuhu 'adhāban lā u'adhdhibuhu aḥadan mina al-'ālamīn — whoever disbelieves after that among you, I will punish with punishment I never punished anyone among worlds — man yakfur, whoever disbelieves, denies, after table spread sent, ba'da dhālika, after that, after seeing miracle, minkum, among you, fa-innī u'adhdhibuhu, I will punish him, 'adhāban, punishment, lā u'adhdhibuhu aḥadan mina al-'ālamīn, I never punished anyone among worlds with such, indicating severe, unprecedented punishment for disbelieving after clear miracle of table spread from heaven, as they asked for sign then deny after seeing, similar to Banī Isrā'īl who asked for cow then almost did not slaughter, or asked to see Allah then punished. The warning shows severity of denying after clear sign requested.

**A Verse That Shows Answer and Warning**

This verse shows answer and warning: Allah answers prayer, sending table spread, but warns that whoever disbelieves after that among you will be punished with unprecedented torment, never inflicted on anyone among worlds, showing that requesting miracle then denying after seeing is severe, as they asked for sign then deny, so punishment severe. The warning serves as deterrence, ensuring that disciples and others who witness table spread believe and do not deny, as denial after such clear sign is grave. The extra verified note that punishment described as never inflicted on anyone among worlds indicates unique severity, as they requested sign then deny, similar to Thamūd who asked for she-camel then killed, punished severely.

**Extra Verified Note on Mā'idah Sent Down**

Mā'idah, table spread, sent down from heaven as miracle, as Qur'an says, and sūrah named after it, but details of foods debated: some say fish, bread, etc., some say fruits, etc., but Qur'an does not detail foods, only says table spread, feast for first and last, sign, provision. The extra verified note that some reports say table spread descended with bread and fish, or with various foods, and disciples ate, as tafsīr mentions, but authentic details limited, and focus is on sign and warning, not foods. The verse's warning about unprecedented punishment shows importance of believing after sign.

**Connection to Prayer and Request**

Verses 112-115 form unit on table spread: 112 disciples asked would your Lord send down table spread, Jesus said fear Allah if believers, 113 they said wish to eat, reassure hearts, verify truthful, become witnesses, 114 Jesus prayed O Allah our Lord send table spread as feast for first and last and sign, provide, Best Provider, 115 Allah answered I am sending it down, but whoever denies afterwards will be punished with torment never inflicted, connecting request, explanation, prayer, answer, warning, showing complete narrative of table spread miracle, which gives sūrah its name. The next verse will shift to questioning 'Īsā on Judgment Day: did you ask people to worship you and your mother as gods besides Allah?

**Why Unprecedented Punishment for Denial After Table Spread**

The unprecedented punishment for denial after table spread is because they requested miracle then denied after seeing, which is grave, as they asked for sign to increase faith then deny after sign, showing obstinacy, similar to those who asked for she-camel then killed, or Banī Isrā'īl who asked for manna and quails then complained. The punishment never inflicted on anyone among worlds indicates unique severity for such denial after requested sign, as they had clear proof they asked for, then denied, so punishment severe. The verse therefore warns that requesting signs then denying leads to severe punishment.

Innī munazziluhā 'alaykum — I am sending it down upon you — shows Allah's power to send down table spread from heaven, miracle, and mercy in answering prayer of 'Īsā, providing feast and sign, provision. The answer combined with warning shows both mercy and severity: mercy in sending down table spread as requested, severity in warning of unprecedented punishment for denial after, balancing raḥmah and 'iqāb, as earlier verse 98 said know Allah severe in punishment and Forgiving Merciful, showing both attributes.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:116

> And ˹on Judgment Day˺ Allah will say, “O Jesus, son of Mary! Did you ever ask the people to worship you and your mother as gods besides Allah?” He will answer, “Glory be to You! How could I ever say what I had no right to say? If I had said such a thing, you would have certainly known it. You know what is ˹hidden˺ within me, but I do not know what is within You. Indeed, You ˹alone˺ are the Knower of all unseen

**Expanded Commentary**

**Allah Will Say O Jesus Did You Ask People to Worship You and Mother as Gods**

Wa idh qāla Allāhu yā 'Īsā ibna Maryama a-anta qulta li-al-nāsi ittakhdhūnī wa ummiya ilāhayni min dūni Allāhi qāla subḥānaka mā yakūnu lī an aqūla mā laysa lī bi-ḥaqqin in kuntu qultuhu fa-qad 'alimtahu ta'lamu mā fī nafsī wa lā a'lamu mā fī nafsika innaka Anta 'Allāmu al-ghuyūb reports questioning of 'Īsā on Judgment Day. Wa idh qāla Allāhu, when Allah said, yā 'Īsā ibna Maryama, O Jesus son of Mary, a-anta qulta li-al-nāsi, did you say to people, from q-w-l, ittakhdhūnī wa ummiya ilāhayni min dūni Allāhi, take me and my mother as two gods besides Allah, ittakhdhūnī from a-kh-dh, to take, ummiya, my mother, ilāhayni, two gods, from a-l-h, min dūni Allāhi, besides Allah, dūn, besides.

**Glory Be to You How Could I Say What No Right**

Qāla subḥānaka mā yakūnu lī an aqūla mā laysa lī bi-ḥaqqin — he said glory be to You, not for me to say what not right for me — subḥānaka, glory be to You, from s-b-ḥ, to glorify, exalt, mā yakūnu lī, not for me, not possible for me, an aqūla, to say, mā laysa lī bi-ḥaqqin, what not right for me, ḥaqq, right, truth, indicates 'Īsā's exaltation of Allah, subḥānaka, and denial that he would say what not right for him, i.e., claim divinity for himself and mother, as he had no right to say such, as he is servant, messenger, not god, as earlier verses said Messiah only messenger, ate food, etc.

**If I Had Said You Would Have Known**

In kuntu qultuhu fa-qad 'alimtahu ta'lamu mā fī nafsī wa lā a'lamu mā fī nafsika innaka Anta 'Allāmu al-ghuyūb — if I had said it You would have known it, You know what in myself, I do not know what in Yourself, You Knower of unseen — in kuntu qultuhu, if I had said it, qultu, said, fa-qad 'alimtahu, You would have known it, 'alimtahu from '-l-m, ta'lamu mā fī nafsī, You know what in myself, nafs, self, wa lā a'lamu mā fī nafsika, I do not know what in Yourself, nafsika, Yourself, innaka Anta 'Allāmu al-ghuyūb — You Knower of unseen — 'Allām, great Knower, ghuyūb, unseen. The response shows 'Īsā's acknowledgment that Allah knows what in his self, inner, and he does not know what in Allah's Self, and if he had said such, Allah would have known, as Allah knows what he reveals and conceals, as earlier verses said.

**A Verse That Refutes Trinity and Deification of Maryam**

This verse refutes Trinity and deification of Maryam, as Allah will ask 'Īsā on Judgment Day did you ask people to worship you and your mother as two gods besides Allah, and 'Īsā will answer glory be to You, how could I say what no right, if I had said You would have known, You know what in myself, I do not know what in Yourself, You Knower of unseen. The question and answer show that 'Īsā never claimed divinity for himself or mother, but taught worship Allah alone, my Lord and your Lord, as next verse says. The verse therefore after discussing table spread, shifts to eschatological questioning about deification, refuting claim that 'Īsā taught people to worship him and mother as gods, as some Christians did, especially Collyridians who worshipped Maryam, or Trinity that includes Maryam in some interpretations, as earlier verses about Trinity said.

**Extra Verified Note on Ilāhayni**

Ilāhayni, two gods, dual of ilāh, god, indicates taking 'Īsā and Maryam as two gods besides Allah, as some sects did, or as part of Trinity where Maryam elevated, as Qur'an says they took 'Īsā and mother as gods, as 5:116 itself. The extra verified historical note that some Christian sects, like Collyridians in Arabia, worshipped Maryam as goddess, offering bread, and Qur'an refutes, and Trinity doctrine includes Father, Son, Holy Spirit, but Qur'an mentions 'Īsā and mother as two gods besides Allah to refute excessive veneration of Maryam that reached worship, as earlier verse 75 said Messiah and mother ate food, showing neediness, not divinity. The verse therefore refutes both deification of 'Īsā and Maryam.

**Connection to Previous Favors and Questioning**

Verse 110 listed favors upon 'Īsā and mother, support with holy spirit, teaching, miracles by permission, prevention from harming, 111 inspiration of disciples, 112-115 table spread story, 116 questioning on Judgment Day did you ask people to worship you and mother as gods besides Allah, 'Īsā answers glory, not right for me, if I had said You would have known, You know what in myself, I do not know what in Yourself, You Knower of unseen, connecting: after listing favors and miracles by permission, which could be misinterpreted as divinity, Allah questions 'Īsā about deification, and 'Īsā denies, exalting Allah, acknowledging Allah knows what in himself, he does not know what in Allah, You Knower of unseen, showing humility and refutation of divinity claim. The next verse will say I only told them worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, I was witness over them as long as among them, when You took me, You Witness over them, Witness over all things.

**Subḥānaka as Exaltation**

Subḥānaka, glory be to You, is exaltation, tanzīh, declaring Allah free from imperfection, partners, etc., as 'Īsā exalts Allah from having partner, son, etc., and from claim that he asked people to worship him and mother as gods. The exaltation shows 'Īsā's monotheism, tawḥīd, as he exalts Allah, not claiming divinity. The same exaltation appears for similar denials, as angels say subḥānaka, etc. The verse therefore shows 'Īsā's pure monotheism.

**You Know What in Myself I Do Not Know What in Yourself**

Ta'lamu mā fī nafsī wa lā a'lamu mā fī nafsika — You know what in myself, I do not know what in Yourself — shows difference between Creator and creature: Allah knows what in 'Īsā's self, inner, thoughts, etc., 'Īsā does not know what in Allah's Self, unseen, as Allah's knowledge comprehensive, creature's limited, as earlier verse 109 messengers said no knowledge, You Knower of unseen, and verse 116 'Īsā says same, You Knower of unseen, emphasizing Allah's comprehensive knowledge of unseen, ghuyūb, and creature's limited knowledge.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:117

> I never told them anything except what You ordered me to say: “Worship Allah—my Lord and your Lord!” And I was witness over them as long as I remained among them. But when You took me, You were the Witness over them—and You are a Witness over all things

**Expanded Commentary**

**I Never Told Them Except What You Ordered Worship Allah My Lord and Your Lord**

Mā qultu lahum illā mā amartanī bihi ani u'budū Allāha Rabbī wa Rabbakum wa kuntu 'alayhim shahīdan mā dumtu fīhim fa-lammā tawaffaytanī kunta Anta al-raqība 'alayhim wa Anta 'alā kulli shay'in Shahīd reports 'Īsā's continuation: I did not say to them except what You ordered me: worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, I was witness over them as long as among them, when You took me, You were Watcher over them, You Witness over all things. Mā qultu lahum, I did not say to them, from q-w-l, illā mā amartanī bihi, except what You ordered me with, amartanī from a-m-r, bihi, with it, ani u'budū Allāha Rabbī wa Rabbakum, that worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, u'budū from '-b-d, Rabbī, my Lord, Rabbakum, your Lord.

**Worship Allah My Lord and Your Lord as Core Message**

Ani u'budū Allāha Rabbī wa Rabbakum — worship Allah my Lord and your Lord — is core message of 'Īsā, as earlier verse 72 said Messiah said O Children of Israel worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, indeed whoever associates with Allah, Allah forbade Paradise, and verse 117 repeats same message, showing that 'Īsā's message was tawḥīd, worship Allah alone, my Lord and your Lord, not worship me or mother as gods, as verse 116 questioned. The message Rabbī wa Rabbakum, my Lord and your Lord, shows 'Īsā's servitude, he has Lord, Allah, same Lord as people, not divine, as he says my Lord and your Lord, indicating common Lord, Allah, not himself Lord.

**I Was Witness Over Them As Long As Among Them**

Wa kuntu 'alayhim shahīdan mā dumtu fīhim — I was witness over them as long as I remained among them — kuntu, I was, 'alayhim, over them, shahīdan, witness, from sh-h-d, mā dumtu fīhim, as long as I remained among them, dumtu from d-w-m, fīhim, among them, indicates 'Īsā was witness, shahīd, over his people, observing their deeds, calling to tawḥīd, as long as he was among them, alive, present, before being taken up. The witnessing is of a messenger over his people, observing, calling, not controlling hearts, as earlier verse about messengers' duty only delivery.

**When You Took Me You Were Watcher Over Them**

Fa-lammā tawaffaytanī kunta Anta al-raqība 'alayhim wa Anta 'alā kulli shay'in Shahīd — when You took me, You were Watcher over them, You Witness over all things — fa-lammā, when, tawaffaytanī, You took me, from w-f-y, to take in full, cause to die or take up, kunta Anta al-raqība 'alayhim, You were Watcher over them, raqīb from r-q-b, watcher, guardian, 'alayhim, over them, wa Anta 'alā kulli shay'in Shahīd — You Witness over all things — Shahīd from sh-h-d, Witness. The taking, tawaffaytanī, refers to Allah taking 'Īsā up, as 3:55 says I will take you and raise you to Me, and 4:158 raised him to Allah, indicating 'Īsā taken up, not killed, and after taking, Allah is Raqīb, Watcher over them, Shahīd over all things, indicating Allah's comprehensive watch and witness after 'Īsā's departure.

**A Verse That Shows 'Īsā's True Message and Witness Role**

This verse shows 'Īsā's true message: worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, not worship me and mother as gods, and his role as witness over people while among them, and Allah as Watcher and Witness after taking him, Witness over all things. The verse therefore refutes deification, shows tawḥīd message, and shows that after 'Īsā's departure, people deviated, taking him and mother as gods, but Allah is Witness over all, knows what they did. The extra verified note that tawaffaytanī, You took me, is used for taking soul, as in 39:42 Allah takes souls at death, and for 'Īsā indicates taking up, raising, not necessarily death, as 4:158 says not killed but raised, showing that 'Īsā taken up alive, will return, as ḥadīth indicates.

**Extra Verified Note on Tawaffaytanī**

Tawaffaytanī, You took me, from tawaffā, to take in full, used for death and for taking up, as 3:55 innī mutawaffīka wa rāfi'uka ilayya, I will take you and raise you to Me, and 5:117 fa-lammā tawaffaytanī, when You took me, indicates taking up, raising, as tafsīr explains, not death, as 'Īsā not killed but raised, as 4:158. The extra verified note that majority of mufassirūn say tawaffā here means raising, taking up, not death, as 'Īsā will return and die later, shows that 'Īsā taken up alive, and after taking, Allah Watcher over people, Witness over all. The verse's closing Anta 'alā kulli shay'in Shahīd, You Witness over all things, shows Allah's comprehensive witness.

**Connection to Previous Question About Deification**

Verse 116 Allah will say O Jesus did you ask people to worship you and mother as gods besides Allah, he answers glory be to You, not for me to say what not right, if I had said You would have known, You know what in myself, I do not know what in Yourself, You Knower of unseen. Verse 117 I never told them except what You ordered: worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, I was witness over them as long as among them, when You took me, You Watcher over them, You Witness over all things, connecting question and answer: after denying saying worship me and mother as gods, 'Īsā clarifies what he did say: worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, and his role as witness while among them, and Allah's role as Watcher and Witness after, showing true message and deviation after. The next verse will say if You punish them they belong to You after all, if You forgive them You Almighty All-Wise, showing 'Īsā's submission to Allah's will regarding his people.

**Witness Over All Things as Closing**

Wa Anta 'alā kulli shay'in Shahīd — You Witness over all things — Shahīd, Witness, over all things, indicates Allah's comprehensive witness, knows all, sees all, as earlier verses about Allah knows what in heavens and earth, knows what you reveal and conceal, Knower of unseen, etc., and 'Īsā acknowledges Allah as Witness over all, including his people after his departure, showing that Allah knows what they did after, taking him and mother as gods, and will judge. The verse therefore ends with Allah's comprehensive witness.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:118

> If You punish them, they belong to You after all. But if You forgive them, You are surely the Almighty, All-Wise.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**If You Punish Them They Belong to You After All**

In tu'adhdhibhum fa-innahum 'ibāduka wa in taghfir lahum fa-innaka Anta al-'Azīzu al-Ḥakīm reports 'Īsā's submission: if You punish them, they are Your servants, if You forgive them, You Almighty All-Wise. In tu'adhdhibhum, if You punish them, from '-dh-b, fa-innahum 'ibāduka, they are Your servants, 'ibād, servants, from '-b-d, ka, Your, wa in taghfir lahum, if You forgive them, taghfir from gh-f-r, fa-innaka Anta al-'Azīzu al-Ḥakīm — You Almighty All-Wise — 'Azīz from '-z-z, mighty, Ḥakīm from ḥ-k-m, wise.

Fa-innahum 'ibāduka — they are Your servants — indicates acknowledgment that people, even those who took 'Īsā and mother as gods, are Allah's servants, 'ibād, owned by Allah, under His dominion, so if He punishes them, He punishes His own servants, just, as owner can do with servants, and if He forgives, He forgives His servants, merciful, wise. The phrase shows 'Īsā's submission to Allah's will regarding his people, not interceding with claim, but leaving matter to Allah, as they are Allah's servants, not his, and Allah does what He wills with His servants, as He is 'Azīz, Mighty, Ḥakīm, Wise.

**If You Forgive Them You Almighty All-Wise**

Wa in taghfir lahum fa-innaka Anta al-'Azīzu al-Ḥakīm — if You forgive them, You Almighty All-Wise — indicates that forgiveness is from Almighty, 'Azīz, Mighty, who can forgive despite power to punish, and All-Wise, Ḥakīm, whose forgiveness is with wisdom, not arbitrary, as He knows who deserves forgiveness, who deserves punishment, as earlier verse 98 know Allah severe in punishment and Forgiving Merciful, and verse 118 shows 'Īsā acknowledging Allah's might and wisdom in punishing or forgiving. The pairing 'Azīz Ḥakīm appears many times, showing might and wisdom together, as forgiveness requires might to forgive and wisdom to place forgiveness rightly.

**A Verse That Shows 'Īsā's Submission to Allah's Will**

This verse shows 'Īsā's submission, tafwīḍ, to Allah's will regarding his people who took him and mother as gods: if You punish them, they are Your servants, if You forgive them, You Almighty All-Wise, leaving matter to Allah, not claiming intercession, not defending, but submitting that they are Allah's servants, under His dominion, punishment or forgiveness is His, with might and wisdom. The verse therefore after clarifying true message worship Allah my Lord and your Lord and witnessing role, shows 'Īsā's final submission, acknowledging Allah's ownership and might and wisdom, as appropriate for Day of Judgment, when messengers disclaim knowledge and refer to Allah Knower of unseen.

**Extra Verified Note on 'Ibāduka**

'Ibāduka, Your servants, indicates that all people are Allah's servants, 'ibād, owned, created, under His dominion, even those who associate, as they are still servants in sense of ownership, though not obedient servants, 'ibād in general sense, not 'ibād al-Raḥmān in specific obedient sense. The extra verified note that Qur'an uses 'ibād for all created, as in 19:93 all in heavens and earth come to Most Merciful as servants, shows that even disbelievers are servants in ownership, so punishment or forgiveness is Allah's right, as owner. The verse's closing 'Azīz Ḥakīm shows that punishment is with might, forgiveness with wisdom, both attributes together.

**Connection to Previous Witness Role**

Verse 117 I never told them except worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, I was witness over them as long as among them, when You took me, You Watcher over them, You Witness over all things. Verse 118 if You punish them they are Your servants, if You forgive them You Almighty All-Wise, connecting: after stating true message and witnessing role and Allah's watch after taking, 'Īsā submits matter of his people to Allah: they are Your servants, if punish, Your servants, if forgive, You Mighty Wise, showing that after clarifying he did not teach deification, he leaves judgment of those who deified him and mother to Allah, who is Witness over all, Watcher, Knower of unseen, Mighty, Wise. The next verse will say Allah will declare this is Day when only faithful benefit from faithfulness, theirs Gardens under which rivers flow forever, Allah pleased with them and they pleased with Him, ultimate triumph.

**Submission as Model for Messengers**

In tu'adhdhibhum fa-innahum 'ibāduka wa in taghfir lahum fa-innaka Anta al-'Azīzu al-Ḥakīm — if You punish them they are Your servants, if You forgive them You Almighty All-Wise — is model of submission for messengers, as they disclaim knowledge, refer to Allah Knower of unseen, and leave judgment to Allah Mighty Wise, not claiming authority, as earlier verse 109 messengers said no knowledge, You Knower of unseen, and verse 116-118 'Īsā similarly submits, showing adab of messengers on Day of Judgment, humility, referral to Allah. The verse therefore shows 'Īsā's humility and submission.

Al-'Azīzu al-Ḥakīm, Almighty All-Wise, as closing for judgment verse, indicates that judgment requires might, 'izzah, to enforce, and wisdom, ḥikmah, to place rightly, as Allah's punishment with might, forgiveness with wisdom, both together, showing that Allah's judgment is with might and wisdom, not arbitrary, as earlier verse 95 Allah Almighty capable of punishment, and verse 98 severe in punishment and Forgiving Merciful, and verse 118 Almighty All-Wise, all showing attributes relevant to judgment.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:119

> Allah will declare, “This is the Day when ˹only˺ the faithful will benefit from their faithfulness. Theirs are Gardens under which rivers flow, to stay there for ever and ever. Allah is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him. That is the ultimate triumph.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Allah Will Declare This Day When Faithful Benefit From Faithfulness**

Qāla Allāhu hādhā yawmu yanfa'u al-ṣādiqīna ṣidquhum lahum jannātun tajrī min taḥtihā al-anhāru khālidīna fīhā abadan raḍiya Allāhu 'anhum wa raḍū 'anhu dhālika al-fawzu al-'aẓīm declares: Allah will say this is Day when truthful benefit from truthfulness, theirs Gardens under which rivers flow forever, Allah pleased with them and they pleased with Him, ultimate triumph. Qāla Allāhu, Allah said, hādhā yawmu, this is Day, yawm, Day, yanfa'u al-ṣādiqīna ṣidquhum, benefits truthful their truthfulness, yanfa'u from n-f-', ṣādiqīna from ṣ-d-q, truthful, ṣidquhum, their truthfulness, lahum jannātun, for them Gardens, jannāt from j-n-n, tajrī min taḥtihā al-anhāru, rivers flow under them, tajrī from j-r-y, taḥtihā, under them, anhār, rivers, khālidīna fīhā abadan, abiding therein forever, khālidīna from kh-l-d, abadan, forever, raḍiya Allāhu 'anhum, Allah pleased with them, raḍiya from r-ḍ-w, wa raḍū 'anhu, and they pleased with Him, dhālika al-fawzu al-'aẓīm — that is great triumph — fawz, triumph, 'aẓīm, great.

**Truthful Benefit From Truthfulness on This Day**

Hādhā yawmu yanfa'u al-ṣādiqīna ṣidquhum — this is Day when truthful benefit from truthfulness — yawmu, Day, Judgment Day, yanfa'u, benefits, al-ṣādiqīna, truthful, ṣidquhum, their truthfulness, indicates that on Judgment Day, only truthfulness, ṣidq, benefits, not lies, not false claims, as earlier verses about fabricating lies about Allah, taking 'Īsā and mother as gods, etc., all false, not benefiting, while truthful, ṣādiqīn, those who were truthful in belief, worship Allah alone, follow messengers truthfully, benefit from truthfulness. The same phrase appears for similar contexts, showing that ṣidq, truthfulness, is key on Judgment Day.

**Gardens Under Which Rivers Flow Forever**

Lahum jannātun tajrī min taḥtihā al-anhāru khālidīna fīhā abadan — theirs Gardens under which rivers flow, abiding therein forever — jannāt, Gardens, tajrī, flow, min taḥtihā, under them, al-anhāru, rivers, khālidīna fīhā abadan, abiding therein forever, abadan, ever and ever, indicates reward for truthful: Gardens with rivers flowing beneath, eternal abode forever, not temporary, as earlier verse 85 rewarded believing Christians with Gardens under which rivers flow abiding forever, reward of good-doers, and verse 119 same for truthful, showing consistent reward.

**Allah Pleased With Them and They Pleased With Him**

Raḍiya Allāhu 'anhum wa raḍū 'anhu — Allah pleased with them and they pleased with Him — raḍiya, pleased, from r-ḍ-w, 'anhum, with them, wa raḍū 'anhu, they pleased with Him, indicates mutual pleasure, riḍwān, Allah pleased with truthful for their truthfulness, and they pleased with Allah for reward, Gardens, etc., showing highest reward, riḍwān, pleasure, beyond Gardens, as 9:72 says pleasure of Allah greater. The mutual pleasure indicates complete satisfaction, as truthful pleased with Allah's reward and Allah pleased with their truthfulness.

Dhālika al-fawzu al-'aẓīm — that is great triumph — fawz, triumph, success, 'aẓīm, great, mighty, indicates that Gardens, eternal abode, mutual pleasure, is great triumph, ultimate success, as earlier verse 90, 100 said so you may be successful, falāḥ, and verse 119 says great triumph, fawz 'aẓīm, for truthful, showing that true success is Gardens and pleasure, not worldly abundance of evil that may dazzle, as verse 100 warned. The same closing appears for similar rewards, indicating ultimate success.

**A Verse That Shows Outcome for Truthful on Judgment Day**

This verse shows outcome for truthful, ṣādiqīn, on Judgment Day: benefit from truthfulness, Gardens under which rivers flow forever, Allah pleased with them and they pleased with Him, ultimate triumph, contrasting with outcome for those who took 'Īsā and mother as gods, or fabricated lies, who will be punished, as earlier verse 118 said if punish them they are Your servants, if forgive You Mighty Wise, and verse 119 shows that truthful benefit, indicating two outcomes: truthful rewarded, others punished or forgiven per wisdom. The verse therefore after questioning 'Īsā and his submission, declares Day when truthful benefit.

**Extra Verified Note on Ṣādiqīn**

Ṣādiqīn, truthful, from ṣidq, truthfulness, includes truthfulness in belief, speech, deeds, as earlier verses about ṣidq, and 'Īsā was ṣādiq, truthful, as he said I only told them worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, truthfully, and his truthfulness benefits on Judgment Day, as he is among ṣādiqīn. The extra verified note that ṣidq is highest virtue, as ḥadīth says truthfulness leads to righteousness and Paradise, lying leads to wickedness and Hellfire, shows that verse 119's yanfa'u al-ṣādiqīna ṣidquhum, truthful benefit from truthfulness, emphasizes that truthfulness leads to Gardens and pleasure, ultimate triumph. The next verse will say to Allah belongs kingdom of heavens and earth and everything within, He Most Capable of everything, closing sūrah with Allah's dominion and capability.

**Connection to Previous Submission**

Verse 116-118 showed questioning of 'Īsā about deification and his answer denying, clarifying true message worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, witness role, submission if punish they are Your servants, if forgive You Mighty Wise. Verse 119 Allah declares this is Day when truthful benefit from truthfulness, theirs Gardens under which rivers flow forever, Allah pleased with them and they pleased with Him, ultimate triumph, connecting: after 'Īsā's truthful answer, Allah declares Day when truthful benefit, including 'Īsā who was truthful, and all truthful, rewarded with Gardens and pleasure, ultimate triumph, showing that truthfulness, ṣidq, as 'Īsā showed, benefits on Judgment Day. The verse therefore closes 'Īsā narrative with reward for truthful.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.

---
## Sūrah al-Mā'idah 5:120

> To Allah ˹alone˺ belongs the kingdom of the heavens and the earth and everything within. And He is Most Capable of everything

**Expanded Commentary**

**To Allah Alone Belongs Kingdom of Heavens and Earth and Everything Within**

Lillāhi mulku al-samāwāti wa al-arḍi wa mā fīhinna wa Huwa 'alā kulli shay'in Qadīr declares: to Allah belongs kingdom of heavens and earth and what in them, He over everything Capable. Lillāhi, to Allah, for Allah, mulku, kingdom, dominion, from m-l-k, al-samāwāti, heavens, samāwāt, wa al-arḍi, and earth, arḍ, wa mā fīhinna, and what in them, mā, what, fīhinna, in them, dual feminine for heavens and earth, wa Huwa 'alā kulli shay'in Qadīr — He over everything Capable — Huwa, He, 'alā, over, kulli shay'in, everything, Qadīr, Capable, from q-d-r.

**Kingdom of Heavens and Earth as Allah's Alone**

Lillāhi mulku al-samāwāti wa al-arḍi wa mā fīhinna — to Allah belongs kingdom of heavens and earth and what in them — mulk, kingdom, dominion, ownership, control, belongs to Allah alone, not to 'Īsā, not to Maryam, not to any other, as earlier verses refuted deification of 'Īsā and mother, taking them as gods besides Allah, and verse 120 says to Allah alone belongs kingdom of heavens and earth and everything within, showing that dominion belongs to Allah alone, not to 'Īsā or Maryam, who are within heavens and earth, owned, not owners, servants, not lords, as earlier verse 118 said they are Your servants. The same phrase appears for similar closings, emphasizing Allah's sole dominion.

Wa mā fīhinna — and what in them — includes everything within heavens and earth, all creatures, including 'Īsā, Maryam, disciples, all humans, jinn, angels, animals, etc., all within heavens and earth, owned by Allah, under His kingdom, dominion, control, as He created, owns, controls, provides, as earlier verses about provision, Best Provider, etc. The phrase shows comprehensive dominion: not only heavens and earth themselves, but everything within them, all belongs to Allah, so no one else has kingdom, no one else deserves worship, as worship belongs to Owner of kingdom.

**He Over Everything Capable as Closing**

Wa Huwa 'alā kulli shay'in Qadīr — He over everything Capable — Qadīr, Capable, Powerful, from q-d-r, to be capable, indicates Allah's capability over everything, including punishing, forgiving, sending table spread, creating bird from clay by permission, healing, bringing dead, preventing harm, gathering messengers, questioning, rewarding truthful with Gardens, etc., all within His capability, as earlier verses showed miracles by permission, punishment, forgiveness, etc., all by His capability. The closing Qadīr appears for similar endings, showing Allah's power over all, as sūrah began with fulfilling contracts and Allah judges what He wills, and ends with to Allah belongs kingdom and He Capable of everything, showing beginning and end with Allah's authority and capability.

**A Verse That Closes Sūrah With Allah's Dominion and Capability**

This verse closes Sūrah al-Mā'idah with Allah's dominion and capability: to Allah alone belongs kingdom of heavens and earth and everything within, He over everything Capable, summarizing sūrah's themes: contracts, covenants, lawful and unlawful, hunting, Ka'bah, sacred months, hady, qalā'id, table spread, questioning 'Īsā, truthful benefit, all under Allah's kingdom and capability, as He legislates, judges, provides, punishes, forgives, with might and wisdom, severe in punishment, Forgiving Merciful, Almighty All-Wise, Best Provider, Knower of unseen, Witness over all, Capable of everything. The verse therefore is fitting closing, as sūrah began with O you who believe fulfill contracts, Allah judges what He wills, and ends with to Allah belongs kingdom and He Capable.

**Extra Verified Note on Mulk**

Mulk, kingdom, dominion, from malaka, to own, control, appears in many closing verses, as 3:189 to Allah belongs kingdom of heavens and earth, 5:120 to Allah belongs kingdom, etc., showing that true kingdom belongs to Allah, not to humans, not to 'Īsā, not to others, as earlier verse 17-18 said Jews and Christians claimed we are children of Allah and beloved, but to Allah belongs kingdom, and verse 120 repeats for closing. The extra verified note that mulk includes ownership, control, legislation, judgment, provision, etc., all belongs to Allah, so only He deserves worship, as earlier verses about worship Allah my Lord and your Lord, shows tawḥīd al-mulk, Oneness of dominion, part of tawḥīd.

**Connection to Previous Reward and Ultimate Triumph**

Verse 119 Allah declares this is Day when truthful benefit from truthfulness, theirs Gardens under which rivers flow forever, Allah pleased with them and they pleased with Him, ultimate triumph. Verse 120 to Allah belongs kingdom of heavens and earth and everything within, He over everything Capable, connecting: after declaring reward for truthful, ultimate triumph, sūrah closes with Allah's kingdom and capability, showing that Gardens, pleasure, triumph are within Allah's kingdom, under His capability, He provides, rewards, as Best Provider, Capable of everything, and to Him belongs kingdom, so He can reward truthful and punish others as He wills, with might and wisdom. The closing therefore ties reward to dominion and capability.

**Final Verse as Summary of Sūrah's Message**

Verse 120 as final verse summarizes sūrah's message: to Allah belongs kingdom of heavens and earth and everything within, He over everything Capable, indicating that all contracts, covenants, lawful, unlawful, hunting, pilgrimage, Ka'bah, sacred months, hady, table spread, questioning, reward, all under Allah's kingdom and capability, so obey Allah and Messenger, be mindful, fear Allah, worship Allah alone, my Lord and your Lord, as 'Īsā taught, and truthfulness benefits, as ultimate triumph is Gardens and pleasure, within Allah's kingdom, by His capability. The sūrah therefore begins with fulfilling contracts and ends with Allah's kingdom and capability, framing all rulings within Allah's dominion and power.

**A Verse That Connects to Earlier Themes of Knowledge and Guidance**

This verse connects to earlier themes of knowledge and guidance that run through Sūrah al-Mā'idah, as sūrah emphasizes 'ilm, knowledge, and hudā, guidance, versus lack of knowledge and misguidance, as in verse 77 do not follow desires of those astray before and led many astray and strayed from straight path, and verse 104 even if forefathers had no knowledge or guidance, and verse 103 most lack understanding, lā ya'qilūn, and verse 77. The connection shows that following forefathers without knowledge or guidance leads to fabrication, as in baḥīrah etc., and following revelation and Messenger leads to knowledge and guidance, as revelation has knowledge and guidance. The verse therefore reinforces that true guidance comes from what Allah sent down and Messenger, not from forefathers who had no knowledge.

**Extra Verified Note on Individual Responsibility**

The extra verified note that individual responsibility, 'alaykum anfusakum, upon you is yourselves, does not cancel collective duty of enjoining good and forbidding evil, as earlier verses about not forbidding evil as cause of curse (5:79) showed that failure to forbid evil leads to curse, and verse 105 says deviation of others not harm guided as long as guided, but guidance includes enjoining good and forbidding evil as much as possible, as Abū Bakr clarified, and if people see evil and do not change it, Allah may punish all, as ḥadīth says. The verse therefore balances individual and collective, showing that after doing duty of calling to revelation and Messenger and enjoining good, if others still deviate, their deviation not harm guided, as each returns to Allah and informed of deeds, and Allah knows what you reveal and conceal.
